F\neq\id
, then

F\alpha=r^{-1}F[-y(p_{0})+f_{i}\circ L^{-1}]=r^{-1}F^{\prime}[f_{i}]\circ L^{-1}.

If
F=\hat{\partial}
, this implies that

\nabla_{\alpha}\alpha=A^{-1}\hat{\partial}\alpha=A^{-1}r^{-1}\hat{\partial}_{i}f_{i}\circ L^{-1}=\nabla_{f_{i}}f_{i}\circ L^{-1},

so
\|\nabla_{\alpha}\alpha\|_{\infty}=\|\nabla_{f_{i}}f_{i}\|_{\infty}\leq 1
.
Otherwise, if
F\not\in\{\id,\hat{\partial}\}
, Lemma
3.11
implies that
\|F\alpha\|_{\infty}\lesssim_{d}A^{-1}\rho^{-1}\leq\rho^{-1}
. This proves (
23
).
∎

4.
Lower bounds on
\beta
–numbers

In this section, we prove Theorem
1.2
.
In fact, Theorem
1.2
is an immediate consequence of the following bound.

Proposition 4.1
.

There is a
\delta_{0}>0
with the following property. Let
0<\delta<\delta_{0}
,
A>1
, and
p>0
. If
\rho>1
is sufficiently large,
N=\lfloor\delta A^{4}\rfloor
,
f_{i}
is constructed as in Section
3
,
\Gamma=\Gamma_{f_{N}}
, and
U=[0,1]\times\{0\}\times[0,1]
, then

\int_{0}^{R}\int_{\Psi_{f_{N}}(U)}\beta_{\Gamma}(v,r)^{p}\,\mathrm{d}v\frac{\,\mathrm{d}r}{r}\gtrsim NA^{-p}\gtrsim\delta A^{4-p}.

We prove this by introducing a parametric version of
\beta_{\Gamma}(v,r)
. For any measurable function
\psi\colon\mathbb{H}\to\mathbb{R}
which is constant on cosets of
\langle Y\rangle
, we define
V(v,r)=\Pi(B(v,r))
and

\gamma_{\psi}(v,r)=r^{-4}\inf_{h\in\mathsf{Aff}}\|\psi-h\|_{L_{1}(V(v,r))},

where
\mathsf{Aff}
denotes the set of functions of the form
\alpha(v)=ax(v)+b
,
a,b\in\mathbb{R}
. Note that all vertical planes that are not parallel to the
yz
–plane are graphs of functions in
\mathsf{Aff}
.

When
\psi
is intrinsic Lipschitz,
\beta_{\Gamma_{\psi}}
and
\gamma_{\psi}
are comparable.

Lemma 4.2
.

Let
\lambda\in(0,1)
. There is a
c>1
such that for any
\lambda
–intrinsic Lipschitz function
\psi\colon\mathbb{H}\to\mathbb{R}
, any
x\in\Gamma_{\psi}
, and any
r>0
,

\beta_{\Gamma_{\psi}}(x,c^{-1}r)\lesssim_{\lambda}\gamma_{\psi}(x,r)\lesssim_{\lambda}\beta_{\Gamma_{\psi}}(x,cr).

The proof of this lemma uses the fact that if
h(v)=ax(v)+b
is affine and
P=\Gamma_{h}
is the corresponding vertical plane, then
d_{\mathrm{Kor}}(w,P)\approx_{a}|y(w)-h(w)|
for all
w\in\mathbb{H}
. Since the constant in this inequality depends on
a
, we will need the following lemma.

Lemma 4.3
.

Let
\lambda\in(0,1)
. There exist
m>0
and
\epsilon>0
such that for any
\lambda
–intrinsic Lipschitz graph
\Gamma_{\psi}
, any
u\in\Gamma_{\psi}
and any vertical plane
P
, if

\int_{B(u,r)\cap\Gamma_{\psi}}d_{\mathrm{Kor}}(w,P)\,\mathrm{d}\mu(w)<\epsilon r^{4},

(25)

then
|\slope P|<m
.

Proof.

Since
\Gamma_{\psi}
is Ahlfors
3
–regular, there is a
c=c(\lambda)>0
such that
\mu(\Gamma_{\psi}\cap B(w,s))\geq cs^{3}
for all
w\in\Gamma_{\psi}
and
s>0
.

Let
\epsilon=c\delta^{4}
, let
\delta=\frac{1-\lambda}{100}
, let
m=(4\delta)^{-1}
, and let
P
be a vertical plane satisfying (
25
).
We claim that if
v\in\Gamma_{\psi}\cap B(u,\frac{r}{2})
, then
d_{\mathrm{Kor}}(v,P)<2\delta r
. Suppose not. Then
B(v,\delta r)\subset B(u,r)
and

d_{\mathrm{Kor}}(\Gamma_{\psi}\cap B(v,\delta r),P)\geq\delta r,

so

\int_{B(u,r)\cap\Gamma_{\psi}}d_{\mathrm{Kor}}(w,P)\,\mathrm{d}\mu(w)\geq\mu(\Gamma_{\psi}\cap B(v,\delta r))\delta r\geq c\delta^{4}r^{4}=\epsilon r^{4}.

This is a contradiction, so
d_{\mathrm{Kor}}(v,P)<2\delta r
.

In particular, this implies that
d_{\mathrm{Kor}}(u,P)<2\delta r
.
By Lemma
2.6
,
|\psi(uX^{x})-\psi(u)|\leq\frac{2}{1-\lambda}|x|
for any
x\in\mathbb{R}
, so if
v:=\Psi_{\psi}(uX^{8\delta r})
, then

d_{\mathrm{Kor}}(u,v)\leq 8\delta r+|\psi(uX^{8\delta r})-\psi(u)|\leq\frac{8}{100}r+\frac{16}{100}r\leq\frac{r}{4}.

That is,
v\in\Gamma_{\psi}\cap B(u,\frac{r}{2})
, so
d_{\mathrm{Kor}}(v,P)<2\delta r
.

Let
u^{\prime}\in P\cap B(u,2\delta r)
and let
v^{\prime}\in P\cap B(v,2\delta r)
. Then

|x(v^{\prime})-x(u^{\prime})|\geq|x(v)-x(u)|-4\delta r=4\delta r

and

|y(v^{\prime})-y(u^{\prime})|\leq|y(v)-y(u)|+4\delta r\leq\frac{r}{4}+4\delta r\leq r.

Thus

|\slope(P)|=\frac{|y(v^{\prime})-y(u^{\prime})|}{|x(v^{\prime})-x(u^{\prime})|}\leq(4\delta)^{-1}=m,

as desired.
∎

We now prove Lemma
4.2
.

Proof of Lemma
4.2
.

Let
\Gamma=\Gamma_{\psi}
, let
x\in\Gamma
, and
r>0
.
Let
c=c(\lambda)>0
be as in Lemma
2.7
, so that
V(p,s)\subset\Pi(B(p,cs)\cap\Gamma)
for all
p\in\Gamma
and
s>0
.
Note that by the area formula, we have

\mathcal{H}^{3}(S)\approx\int_{\Pi(S)}\sqrt{1+\nabla_{\psi}\psi(v)}\,\mathrm{d}v=\int_{S}\sqrt{1+\nabla_{\psi}\psi(v)}\,\mathrm{d}v\approx_{\lambda}\mu(S)

for any Borel set
S\subset\Gamma
.

We first prove that
\beta_{\Gamma}(x,c^{-1}r)\lesssim_{\lambda}\gamma_{\psi}(x,r)
.
Let
h\colon V_{0}\to\mathbb{R}
be an affine function so that
r^{-4}\|\psi-h\|_{L_{1}(V(x,r))}\leq 2\gamma_{\psi}(x,r)
and let
P=\Gamma_{h}
. Then
d_{\mathrm{Kor}}(v,P)\leq|\psi(v)-h(v)|
for all
y\in\Gamma
and
\Pi(B(x,c^{-1}r)\cap\Gamma)\subset V(x,r)
, so

\displaystyle\beta(x,c^{-1}r)

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\lambda}r^{-4}\int_{B(x,c^{-1}r)\cap\Gamma}d_{\mathrm{Kor}}(v,P)\,\mathrm{d}\mu(v)

\displaystyle\leq r^{-4}\int_{V(x,r)}|\psi(v)-h(v)|\,\mathrm{d}\mu(v)

\displaystyle\leq 2\gamma_{\psi}(x,r).

Next, we show that
\gamma_{\psi}(x,r)\lesssim_{\lambda}\beta_{\Gamma}(x,cr)
. Let
m=m(\lambda),\epsilon=\epsilon(\lambda)
be as in Lemma
4.3
.
Suppose first that
\beta_{\Gamma}(x,cr)<\frac{\epsilon}{2}
. Then there is a vertical plane
P
that satisfies (
25
) and thus
|\slope(P)|<m
. Let
g
be the affine function such that
\Gamma_{g}=P
; then
d_{\mathrm{Kor}}(v,P)\approx_{m}|g(v)-\psi(v)|
for all
v\in\Gamma
(Lemma
2.5
). Therefore, since
V(x,r)\subset\Pi(B(p,cr)\cap\Gamma)
,

\displaystyle\gamma_{\psi}(x,r)

\displaystyle\leq r^{-4}\int_{V(x,r)}|g(v)-\psi(v)|\,\mathrm{d}\mu(v)

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\lambda}r^{-4}\int_{B(x,cr)\cap\Gamma}d_{\mathrm{Kor}}(v,P)\,\mathrm{d}\mu(v)

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\lambda}\beta_{\Gamma}(x,cr).

Now suppose
\beta_{\Gamma}(x,cr)\geq\frac{\epsilon}{2}\gtrsim_{\lambda}1
. Let
h
be the constant (affine) function
h(v)=\psi(x)
. By Lemma
2.6
, for
y\in B(x,r)
, we have

|h(\Pi(y))-\psi(\Pi(y))|=|\psi(x)-\psi(y)|\leq\frac{2}{1-\lambda}r,

so
|h(v)-\psi(v)|\lesssim_{\lambda}r
for all
v\in V(x,r)
. Therefore,

\gamma_{\psi}(x,r)\leq r^{-4}\|f-h\|_{L_{1}(V(x,r))}\lesssim_{\lambda}r^{-4}\cdot r\mu(V(x,r))\lesssim 1\lesssim_{\lambda}\beta_{\Gamma}(x,r),

as desired.
∎

We can thus prove Proposition
4.1
by bounding
\gamma_{\nu_{i}}
and
\gamma_{f_{i}}
. We will prove the following.

Lemma 4.4
.

For any
A>1
, the following properties hold for all sufficiently large
\rho
.
Let
i<k
. Let
v\in\Gamma_{f_{k}}
and let
b>0
. Then

\gamma_{f_{i}}(v,br_{i})\lesssim_{b}A^{-1}\rho^{-1}.

(26)

Let
J_{i}
be as in Section
3
, let
j\in J_{i}
, and let
s_{0}\in[\frac{1}{3}Ar_{i},\frac{2}{3}Ar_{i}]
,
t_{0}\in[0,r_{i}^{2}]
, and
w=\Psi_{f_{k}}(R_{i,j}(s_{0},t_{0}))
. Then

\gamma_{\nu_{i}}(w,8r_{i})\gtrsim A^{-1}.

(27)

Proof.

First, we prove (
26
). Let
L\colon\mathbb{H}\to\mathbb{R}
be the affine function
L(p)=f_{i}(v)+(x(p)-x(v))\nabla_{f_{i}}f_{i}(v)
and let
v^{\prime}=\Psi_{f_{i}}(v)
. Lemma
2.9
and Lemma
3.11
applied to
f_{i}
imply that for all
u\in\mathbb{H}

\displaystyle|f_{i}(u)-L(u)|\lesssim_{\lambda}d_{\mathrm{Kor}}(v^{\prime},u)^{2}(\|\nabla_{f_{i}}^{2}f_{i}\|_{\infty}+\|Zf_{i}\|_{\infty})\overset{\eqref{eq:flatness-fi}}{\lesssim}d_{\mathrm{Kor}}(v^{\prime},u)^{2}A^{-1}\rho^{-1}r_{i}^{-1}.

Since

d_{\mathrm{Kor}}(v^{\prime},v)\leq|f_{i}(v)-f_{k}(v)|\leq\sum_{m=i}^{k-1}\|\nu_{m}\|_{\infty}\leq 2A^{-1}r_{i}\|\kappa\|_{\infty}\leq 2r_{i},

if
u\in B(v,br_{i})
, then
d_{\mathrm{Kor}}(u,v^{\prime})\leq(b+2)r_{i}
and

|f_{i}(u)-L(u)|\leq(b+2)^{2}A^{-1}\rho^{-1}r_{i}\lesssim_{b}A^{-1}\rho^{-1}r_{i}.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\gamma_{f_{i}}(v,br_{i})\leq(br_{i})^{-4}\|f_{i}-L\|_{L_{1}(V(v,br_{i}))}\lesssim_{b}r_{i}^{-1}\|f_{i}-L\|_{L_{\infty}(B(v,br_{i}))}\lesssim_{b}A^{-1}\rho^{-1},

as desired.

Now we prove (
27
). First, let
w^{\prime}=\Psi_{f_{i}}(w)
. Then, as above,
d_{\mathrm{Kor}}(w,w^{\prime})\leq 2r_{i}
, so
V(w^{\prime},6r_{i})\subset V(w,8r_{i})
. Then
\gamma_{\nu_{i}}(w,8r_{i})\gtrsim\gamma_{\nu_{i}}(w^{\prime},6r_{i})
, so it suffices to prove that
\gamma_{\nu_{i}}(w^{\prime},6r_{i})\gtrsim A^{-1}
.

We first apply a change of coordinates. Let
j\in J_{i}
and let
R=R_{i,j}
, so that
w^{\prime}=\Psi_{f_{i}}(R(s_{0},t_{0}))
. Let
D=R([s_{0}-\frac{1}{12}r_{i},s_{0}+\frac{1}{12}r_{i}]\times[0,r_{i}^{2}])
. We claim that if
\rho
is large enough, then
D\subset V(w^{\prime},6r_{i})
.

Let
t\in[0,r_{i}^{2}]
. Lemma
3.7

implies that
|z(R(s_{0},t))-z(R(s_{0},t_{0}))|\leq\frac{4}{3}r_{i}^{2}
, so by (
21
),

|f_{i}(R(s_{0},t))-f_{i}(w^{\prime})|\leq\|Zf_{i}\|_{\infty}|z(R(s_{0},t))-z(R(s_{0},t_{0}))|\lesssim A^{-1}\rho^{-1}r_{i}.

We suppose that
\rho
is large enough that
|f_{i}(R(s_{0},t))-f_{i}(w^{\prime})|\leq r_{i}
; then

\displaystyle d_{\mathrm{Kor}}

\displaystyle(\Psi_{f_{i}}(R(s_{0},t)),w^{\prime})

\displaystyle\leq 2\sqrt{|z(R(s_{0},t))-z(R(s_{0},t_{0}))|}+|f_{i}(R(s_{0},t))-f_{i}(w^{\prime})|

\displaystyle\leq 2\sqrt{\frac{4}{3}r_{i}^{2}}+r_{i}\leq 4r_{i}.

For
t\in[0,r_{i}^{2}]
, the curve
\lambda_{t}(s)=\Psi_{f_{i}}(R(s,t))
is a horizontal curve on
\Gamma_{f_{i}}
with velocity
\lambda_{t}^{\prime}(s)=X+\nabla_{f_{i}}f_{i}(\lambda_{t}(s))Y
. Since
\|\nabla_{f_{i}}f_{i}\|_{\infty}\leq 1
, we have

d_{\mathrm{Kor}}(\lambda_{t}(s),\lambda_{t}(s^{\prime}))\leq\sqrt{2}|s-s^{\prime}|.

If
|s-s_{0}|\leq r_{i}
, then

d_{\mathrm{Kor}}(w^{\prime},\Psi_{f_{i}}(R(s,t)))\leq d_{\mathrm{Kor}}(w^{\prime},\Psi_{f_{i}}(R(s_{0},t)))+d_{\mathrm{Kor}}(\lambda_{t}(s_{0}),\lambda_{t}(s))\leq 6r_{i},

so
R(s,t)\in\Pi(B(w^{\prime},6r_{i}))
. Thus
D\subset V(w^{\prime},6r_{i})
.

For any
h\in\mathsf{Aff}
,

\displaystyle\|\nu_{i}-h\|_{L_{1}(V(w^{\prime},6r_{i}))}

\displaystyle\geq\int_{D}|\nu_{i}(v)-h(v)|\,\mathrm{d}v

\displaystyle\geq\frac{3}{4}\int_{s_{0}-\frac{1}{12}r_{i}}^{s_{0}+\frac{1}{12}r_{i}}\int_{0}^{r_{i}^{2}}|\nu_{i}(R(s,t))-h(R(s,t))|\,\mathrm{d}t\,\mathrm{d}s,

where we used Lemma
3.7
to bound the Jacobian of
R
.

Since
h\in\mathsf{Aff}
is constant on vertical lines, there is an affine function
h_{0}\colon\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
such that
h(R(s,t))=h_{0}(s)
. Since
j\in J_{i}
,

\nu_{i}(R(s,t))=\kappa_{i,j}(R(s,t))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:def-kappa}}}{{=}}A^{-1}r_{i}\kappa(\hat{s},\hat{t}),

where
\hat{s}=A^{-1}r_{i}^{-1}s
and
\hat{t}=r_{i}^{-2}t
.

Let

M=\min_{\begin{subarray}{c}c\in\mathbb{R}\\
\hat{u}\in[\frac{1}{4},\frac{3}{4}]\end{subarray}}\int_{0}^{1}|\kappa(\hat{u},\hat{v})-c|\,\mathrm{d}\hat{v}.

We chose
\kappa
so that
\kappa
is zero on
\partial[0,1]^{2}
and positive on
[\frac{1}{5},\frac{4}{5}]^{2}
, so
M>0
by compactness.

Since
s_{0}\in[\frac{1}{3},\frac{2}{3}]
, if
s\in[s_{0}-\frac{1}{12}r_{i},s_{0}+\frac{1}{12}r_{i}]
, then

\hat{s}\in[\frac{1}{4},\frac{3}{4}]
.
Therefore, substituting
\hat{t}=r_{i}^{-2}t
, we find

\displaystyle\|\nu_{i}-h\|_{L_{1}(V(w^{\prime},6r_{i}))}

\displaystyle\gtrsim r_{i}^{2}\int_{s_{0}-\frac{1}{12}r_{i}}^{s_{0}+\frac{1}{12}r_{i}}\int_{0}^{1}|A^{-1}r_{i}\kappa(\hat{s},\hat{t})-h_{0}(s)|\,\mathrm{d}\hat{t}\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\geq A^{-1}r_{i}^{3}\int_{s_{0}-\frac{1}{12}r_{i}}^{s_{0}+\frac{1}{12}r_{i}}M\,\mathrm{d}s\geq\frac{1}{6}A^{-1}r_{i}^{4}M.

This holds for all
h\in\mathsf{Aff}
, so
\gamma_{\nu_{i}}(w^{\prime},6r_{i})\gtrsim A^{-1}
.
∎

Finally, we prove Proposition
4.1
and Theorem
1.2
.

Proof of Proposition
4.1
and Theorem
1.2
.

By Proposition
3.1
, there is a
\delta>0
such that if
0\leq i\leq\delta A^{4}
, then
\sum_{j\in J_{i}}|Q_{i,j}|>\frac{1}{2}
. Let
N=\lfloor\delta A^{4}\rfloor
, let
f=f_{N}
, and let
\Gamma=\Gamma_{f}
.

Let
c
be as in Lemma
4.2
. Let
0\leq i<N
and
j\in J_{i}
. Let
s\in[\frac{1}{3}Ar_{i},\frac{2}{3}Ar_{i}]
and
t\in[0,r_{i}^{2}]
and let
w=\Psi_{f}(R_{i,j}(s,t))
.
For any
\psi,\phi\colon\mathbb{H}\to\mathbb{R}
,
\gamma
satisfies the reverse triangle inequality

\gamma_{\psi+\phi}(w,r)\geq\gamma_{\psi}(w,r)-\gamma_{\phi}(w,r),

so since
f=f_{i}+\nu_{i}+\sum_{m=i+1}^{N-1}\nu_{m}
,

\gamma_{f}(w,8r_{i})\geq\gamma_{\nu_{i}}(w,8r_{i})-\gamma_{f_{i}}(w,8r_{i})-\sum_{m=i+1}^{\infty}\gamma_{\nu_{m}}(w,8r_{i}).

(28)

Lemma
4.4
implies that when
\rho
is sufficiently large,
\gamma_{\nu_{i}}(w,8r_{i})\gtrsim A^{-1}
and
\gamma_{f_{i}}(w,8r_{i})\lesssim A^{-1}\rho^{-1}
. Furthermore, for
m\geq 1
,
\|\nu_{m}\|_{\infty}\lesssim A^{-1}r_{m}
, so

\gamma_{\nu_{m}}(w,8r_{i})\leq(8r_{i})^{-4}|V(w,8r_{i})|\cdot\|\nu_{m}\|_{\infty}\lesssim r_{i}^{-1}A^{-1}r_{m}\lesssim A^{-1}\rho^{i-m}.

Therefore,

\gamma_{f_{i}}(w,8r_{i})+\sum_{m=i+1}^{\infty}\gamma_{\nu_{m}}(w,8r_{i})\lesssim A^{-1}\rho^{-1}.

When
\rho
is large, this is small compared to
\gamma_{\nu_{i}}(w,8r_{i})
, so

\gamma_{f}(w,8r_{i})\geq\frac{1}{2}\gamma_{\nu_{i}}(w,8r_{i})\gtrsim A^{-1}

and
\beta_{\Gamma_{f}}(w,8cr_{i})\gtrsim\gamma_{f}(w,8r_{i})\gtrsim A^{-1}
. In fact, for
r\in[8cr_{i},16cr_{i}]
,

\beta_{\Gamma}(w,r)\gtrsim\beta_{\Gamma}(w,8cr_{i})\gtrsim A^{-1}.

Therefore, by Lemma
3.7
,

\displaystyle\int_{Q_{i,j}}\beta_{\Gamma}(\Psi_{f}(v),r)^{p}\,\mathrm{d}v

\displaystyle\geq\frac{3}{4}\int_{\frac{1}{3}Ar_{i}}^{\frac{2}{3}Ar_{i}}\int_{0}^{r_{i}^{2}}\beta_{\Gamma}(\Psi_{f}(R_{i,j}(s,t)),r)^{p}\,\mathrm{d}t\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\gtrsim Ar_{i}^{3}\cdot A^{-p}\gtrsim|Q_{i,j}|A^{-p},

and

\int_{\Psi_{f}(U)}\beta_{\Gamma}(v,r)^{p}\,\mathrm{d}v\\
\geq\sum_{i\in J_{i}}\int_{\Psi_{f}(Q_{i,j})}\beta_{\Gamma}(v,r)^{p}\,\mathrm{d}v\gtrsim\sum_{i\in J_{i}}A^{-p}|Q_{i,j}|\gtrsim A^{-p}.

We suppose that
\rho>4
so that the intervals
[8cr_{i},16cr_{i}]
are disjoint and let
R>16c
, so that
R>16cr_{0}
and
\Psi_{f}(U)\subset B(\mathbf{0},R)
. Then

\int_{0}^{R}\int_{\Gamma\cap B(\mathbf{0},R)}\beta_{\Gamma}(v,r)^{p}\,\mathrm{d}v\frac{\,\mathrm{d}r}{r}\geq\sum_{i=0}^{N-1}\int_{8cr_{i}}^{16cr_{i}}\int_{\Psi_{f}(U)}\beta_{\Gamma}(v,r)^{p}\,\mathrm{d}v\frac{\,\mathrm{d}r}{r}\\
\gtrsim\sum_{i=0}^{N-1}\int_{8cr_{i}}^{16cr_{i}}A^{-p}\frac{\,\mathrm{d}r}{r}\geq N\log 2\cdot A^{-p}\gtrsim\delta A^{4-p}.

This proves Proposition
4.1
. By Lemma
2.8
,
\,\mathrm{d}v\approx\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{3}(v)
, so

\int_{0}^{R}\int_{\Gamma\cap B(\mathbf{0},R)}\beta_{\Gamma}(v,r)^{p}\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}^{3}(v)\frac{\,\mathrm{d}r}{r}\approx\int_{0}^{R}\int_{\Gamma\cap B(\mathbf{0},R)}\beta_{\Gamma}(v,r)^{p}\,\mathrm{d}v\frac{\,\mathrm{d}r}{r}\gtrsim\delta A^{4-p}.

When
p<4
, this integral goes to infinity as
A\to\infty
, proving Theorem
1.2
.
∎

5.
Reduction to vertical planes

Now we begin the proof of Theorem
1.1
, which will take up the rest of this paper. In this section and the rest of the paper,
K\colon\mathbb{H}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}\to\mathbb{R}^{2}
will denote a smooth orthogonal kernel which is homogeneous of degree
-3
and
\widehat{K}
will denote the (also orthogonal) kernel
\widehat{K}(v)=K(v^{-1})
. Many of our bounds will depend on
K
, so we omit
K
in subscripts like
\lesssim_{K}
.

Let
\phi\colon\mathbb{H}\to\mathbb{R}
be an intrinsic Lipschitz function. We define
\eta_{\phi}=\mu|_{\Gamma_{\phi}}
, where
\mu
is the measure defined in Section
2.3
. Then
T\eta_{\phi}(p)=T^{K}\eta_{\phi}(p)
is given by

T\eta_{\phi}(p):=\pv(p)\int_{\Gamma_{\phi}}\widehat{K}(p^{-1}w)\,\mathrm{d}w

for all
p\in\mathbb{H}
such that the principal value on the right exists, i.e., for all
p\in\mathbb{H}
such that

\lim_{\begin{subarray}{c}r\to 0\\
R\to\infty\end{subarray}}\int_{\Gamma_{\phi}\cap(B(p,R)\setminus B(p,r))}\widehat{K}(p^{-1}w)\,\mathrm{d}w

(29)

converges. Let
\mathbf{1}
be the function equal to
1
on all of
\mathbb{H}
; then, using the operator notation in Section
2.2
, we can write
T\eta_{\phi}=T_{\eta_{\phi}}\mathbf{1}
.

In this section, we will show that when
\phi
is a bounded smooth function and
p\in\Gamma_{\phi}
, then
T\eta_{\phi}(p)
is the principal value of a singular integral on a vertical plane.
For any
0<r<s
, any
p\in\mathbb{H}
, and any vertical plane
Q
through
p
, let

A_{r,s}^{Q}(p)=Q\cap(B(p,s)\setminus B(p,r))\subset Q

and let
A_{r,s}^{Q}=A_{r,s}^{Q}(\mathbf{0})
. When
Q=V_{0}
we will suppress the superscripts.

For a point
p\in\mathbb{H}
, a vertical plane
Q
through
\mathbf{0}
with finite slope, a function
f\colon\mathbb{H}\to\mathbb{R}
which is constant on cosets of
\langle Y\rangle
, an intrinsic Lipschitz function
\phi
, and
0<r<R
we let

\tilde{T}^{K;Q}_{\phi;r,R}f(p)=\tilde{T}^{Q}_{\phi;r,R}f(p):=\int_{\Psi_{\phi}(p)A_{r,R}^{Q}}\widehat{K}(\Psi_{\phi}(p)^{-1}\Psi_{\phi}(v))f(v)\,\mathrm{d}v,

(30)

and

\tilde{T}^{Q}_{\phi}f(p):=\lim_{\begin{subarray}{c}r\to 0\\
R\to\infty\end{subarray}}\tilde{T}^{Q}_{\phi;r,R}f(p),

if this limit exists. Note that
\tilde{T}^{Q}_{\phi}f
and
\tilde{T}^{Q}_{\phi;r,R}f
are constant on cosets of
\langle Y\rangle
.

When
p\in\Gamma_{\phi}
and
f=\mathbf{1}
is a constant function, we can substitute
w=\Psi_{\phi}(v)
to write
\tilde{T}^{Q}_{\phi}\mathbf{1}
like the right side of (
29
):

\tilde{T}^{Q}_{\phi}\mathbf{1}(p)=\lim_{\begin{subarray}{c}r\to 0\\
R\to\infty\end{subarray}}\int_{pA^{Q}_{r,R}}\widehat{K}(p^{-1}\Psi_{\phi}(v))\,\mathrm{d}v=\lim_{\begin{subarray}{c}r\to 0\\
R\to\infty\end{subarray}}\int_{\Psi_{\phi}\big(pA^{Q}_{r,R}\big)}\widehat{K}(p^{-1}w)\,\mathrm{d}w.

(31)

In this section, we will compare the integrals in (
29
) and (
31
) and prove the following proposition.

Proposition 5.1
.

Let
\phi\colon\mathbb{H}\to\mathbb{R}
be a smooth function which is constant on cosets of
\langle Y\rangle
and let
Q
be a vertical plane through
\mathbf{0}
. Let

C=\max\big\{\|\phi\|_{\infty},\|\nabla_{\phi}\phi\|_{\infty},\|\nabla^{2}_{\phi}\phi\|_{\infty},\|\partial_{z}\phi\|_{\infty},|\slope Q|\big\}

and suppose that
C<\infty
.
Then for any
p\in\Gamma_{\phi}
, the limits
T\eta_{\phi}(p)
and
\tilde{T}^{Q}_{\phi}\mathbf{1}(p)
exist and

T\eta_{\phi}(p)=\tilde{T}^{Q}_{\phi}\mathbf{1}(p).

(32)

In fact,

\left|T\eta_{\phi}(p)-\tilde{T}^{Q}_{\phi;r,R}\mathbf{1}(p)\right|\lesssim_{C}r+R^{-1}.

In particular, under these conditions,
\tilde{T}^{Q}_{\phi}\mathbf{1}(p)
is independent of
Q
, so we write
\tilde{T}_{\phi}=\tilde{T}^{V_{0}}_{\phi}
.

The implicit constants in the proofs in this section almost all depend on
C
, so we will omit the dependence on
C
from our notation.

We first establish bounds on
\tilde{T}^{Q}_{\phi;s,t}\mathbf{1}(p)
.

Lemma 5.2
.

Let
\phi
,
Q
, and
C
be as in Proposition
5.1
. Then for any
0<r^{\prime}<r<1<R<R^{\prime}
and
p\in\Gamma_{\phi}
,

\left|\tilde{T}^{Q}_{\phi;r^{\prime},r}\mathbf{1}(p)\right|\lesssim r

and

\left|\tilde{T}^{Q}_{\phi;R,R^{\prime}}\mathbf{1}(p)\right|\lesssim R^{-1}.

In particular, the principal value
\tilde{T}^{Q}_{\phi}\mathbf{1}(p)
exists for all
p\in\Gamma_{\phi}.

Furthermore, for any bounded function
f\colon\mathbb{H}\to\mathbb{R}
which is constant on cosets of
\langle Y\rangle
and any
0<s<t
,

\left|\tilde{T}^{Q}_{\phi;s,t}f(p)\right|\lesssim\|f\|_{\infty}\log\frac{t}{s}.

Let
Q
be a vertical plane through
\mathbf{0}
with finite slope. Then, by Lemma
2.8
,
\mu|_{Q}
is a
3
–regular left-invariant measure on
Q
. The uniqueness (up to scaling) of the Haar measure on
Q
implies that
\mu|_{Q}
is a constant multiple of
\mathcal{L}|_{Q}
, i.e.
\mu|_{Q}
is a
3
–uniform measure. Hence, the following useful lemma follows easily, see e.g.
[
Mer22
]
for the details.

Lemma 5.3
.

Let
Q
be a vertical plane with finite slope. There exists a
c>0
, depending on the slope of
Q
, so that for any
v\in Q
,
0\leq r_{1}\leq r_{2}\leq\infty
, and any Borel integrable function
f\colon\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
,

\int_{A^{Q}_{r_{1},r_{2}}(v)}f(d_{\mathrm{Kor}}(v,w))\,\mathrm{d}w=c\int_{r_{1}}^{r_{2}}f(r)r^{2}\,\mathrm{d}r.

For
r>0
, let
B_{r}=B(\mathbf{0},r)\subset\mathbb{H}
.

Proof of Lemma
5.2
.

By translation, we may assume without loss of generality that
\mathbf{0}\in\Gamma_{\phi}
and
p=\mathbf{0}
.
For arbitrary
s<t
, we define

I_{s,t}=\tilde{T}^{Q}_{\phi;s,t}\mathbf{1}(p)=\int_{A_{s,t}^{Q}}\widehat{K}(\Psi_{\phi}(v))\,\mathrm{d}v.

We will bound
|I_{r^{\prime},r}|\lesssim r
and
|I_{R,R^{\prime}}|\lesssim R^{-1}
.

We will use the following symmetrization argument. Let
\theta(x,y,z)=(-x,-y,z)
. Then
\theta(A_{s,t}^{Q})=A_{s,t}^{Q}
and
K
is
\mathbb{H}
–odd, so

\displaystyle|I_{s,t}|\overset{\eqref{eq:int-even-odd}}{=}\frac{1}{2}\left|\int_{A_{s,t}^{Q}}\widehat{K}(\Psi_{\phi}(v))+\widehat{K}(\Psi_{\phi}(\theta(v)))\,\mathrm{d}v\right|=\frac{1}{2}\left|\int_{A_{s,t}^{Q}}-\widehat{K}(\theta(\Psi_{\phi}(v)))+\widehat{K}(\Psi_{\phi}(\theta(v)))\,\mathrm{d}v\right|.

Since
\Psi_{\phi}(v)=vY^{\phi(v)-y(v)}
and

\Psi_{\phi}(\theta(v))=\theta(v)Y^{\phi(\theta(v))-y(\theta(v))}=\theta(v)Y^{\phi(\theta(v))+y(v)},

we have

\displaystyle\theta(\Psi_{\phi}(v))=\theta(v)Y^{-\phi(v)+y(v)}=\Psi_{\phi}(\theta(v))Y^{-\phi(v)-\phi(\theta(v))},

and by the mean value theorem,

\displaystyle\left|\widehat{K}(\Psi_{\phi}(\theta(v)))-\widehat{K}(\theta(\Psi_{\phi}(v)))\right|=\left|Y_{\mathsf{L}}\widehat{K}(m(v))\right|\cdot|\phi(v)+\phi(\theta(v))|,

where
m(v)
is a point on the horizontal line segment between
\theta(\Psi_{\phi}(v))
and
\Psi_{\phi}(\theta(v))
. That is,

\displaystyle|I_{s,t}|\leq\frac{1}{2}\int_{A^{Q}_{s,t}}\left|Y_{\mathsf{L}}\widehat{K}(m(v))\right|\cdot|\phi(v)+\phi(\theta(v))|\,\mathrm{d}v.

(33)

Since
m(v)\in\theta(v)\langle Y\rangle
and because
Q
has bounded slope, we have
\|m(v)\|_{\mathrm{Kor}}\gtrsim\|v\|_{\mathrm{Kor}}
for all
v\in Q
.
Since
m(v)
is between
\theta(\Psi_{\phi}(v))
and
\Psi_{\phi}(\theta(v))
,

\|m(v)\|_{\mathrm{Kor}}\lesssim\max\{\|\Psi_{\phi}(v)\|_{\mathrm{Kor}},\|\Psi_{\phi}(\theta(v))\|_{\mathrm{Kor}}\}\lesssim\|v\|_{\mathrm{Kor}}.

That is, for all
v\in Q
,

\|m(v)\|_{\mathrm{Kor}}\approx\|v\|_{\mathrm{Kor}}.

(34)

Furthermore, the bounds on
\phi
and its derivatives give bounds on
\phi(v)+\phi(\theta(v))
. On one hand,

|\phi(v)+\phi(\theta(v))|\leq 2\|\phi\|_{\infty}\leq 2C\lesssim 1.

On the other hand, by Lemma
2.9
,
|\phi(v)+\phi(\theta(v))|\lesssim\|v\|_{\mathrm{Kor}}^{2}
, so for all
v\in Q
,

|\phi(v)+\phi(\theta(v))|\lesssim\min\{1,\|v\|_{\mathrm{Kor}}^{2}\}.

(35)

Therefore, by these bounds, the
(-4)
–homogeneity of
Y_{\mathsf{L}}\widehat{K}
and Lemma
5.3
,

\begin{split}|I_{s,t}|&\lesssim\int_{A_{s,t}^{Q}}\left|Y_{\mathsf{L}}\widehat{K}(m(v))\right|\min\{1,\|v\|_{\mathrm{Kor}}^{2}\}\,\mathrm{d}v\\
&\lesssim\int_{A_{s,t}^{Q}}\min\{\|v\|_{\mathrm{Kor}}^{-4},\|v\|_{\mathrm{Kor}}^{-2}\}\,\mathrm{d}v\\
&\lesssim\int_{s}^{t}\min\{\rho^{-4},\rho^{-2}\}\cdot\rho^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\rho\lesssim\min\{s^{-1}-t^{-1},t-s\}.\end{split}

(36)

In particular, for any
0<r^{\prime}<r<1<R<R^{\prime}<\infty
,
|I_{r^{\prime},r}|\lesssim r
and
|I_{R,R^{\prime}}|\lesssim R^{-1}
. Thus

\left|\tilde{T}^{Q}_{\phi;r^{\prime},R^{\prime}}\mathbf{1}(p)-\tilde{T}^{Q}_{\phi;r,R}\mathbf{1}(p)\right|\leq|I_{r^{\prime},r}|+|I_{R,R^{\prime}}|\lesssim r+R^{-1}.

That is,
\tilde{T}^{Q}_{\phi;r,R}\mathbf{1}(p)
converges as
r\to 0
and
R\to\infty
, so the principal value
\tilde{T}^{Q}_{\phi}\mathbf{1}(p)
exists.

Finally, if
f
is constant on cosets of
\langle Y\rangle
and
0<s<t
,

\left|\tilde{T}^{Q}_{\phi;s,t}f(p)\right|\leq\int_{A^{Q}_{s,t}}\left|\widehat{K}(\Psi_{\phi}(v))f(v)\right|\,\mathrm{d}v\lesssim\int_{A^{Q}_{s,t}}\|v\|^{-3}_{\mathrm{Kor}}\|f\|_{\infty}\,\mathrm{d}v\\
\lesssim\|f\|_{\infty}\int_{s}^{t}\rho^{-3}\cdot\rho^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\rho=\|f\|_{\infty}\log\frac{t}{s},

as desired.
∎

The next lemma lets us compare
\Gamma_{\phi}\cap B(p,r)
and
\Psi_{\phi}(V\cap B(p,r))
when
V
is a vertical plane. Let
\Pi_{V}\colon\mathbb{H}\to V
be the projection from
\mathbb{H}
to
V
along cosets of
\langle Y\rangle
, as in Section
2.1
. Let
B_{r}=B(\mathbf{0},r)
.

Lemma 5.4
.

Let
\phi
and
C
be as in Proposition
5.1
. Let
\Gamma=\Gamma_{\phi}
and
p\in\Gamma
.
Let
W
be the vertical tangent plane to
\Gamma
at
p
, so that
\slope W=\nabla_{\phi}\phi(p)
. Then there is a
c>0
depending only on
C
such that for
r>0
,

W\cap B(p,r-cr^{2})\subset\Pi_{W}(\Gamma\cap B(p,r))\subset W\cap B(p,r+cr^{2})

and for
R>0
,

pV_{0}\cap B(p,R-c)\subset\Pi_{pV_{0}}(\Gamma\cap B(p,R))\subset pV_{0}\cap B(p,R+c).

Though the inclusions hold for all
r
and
R
, they are most useful when
r
is small and
R
is large.

Proof.

Without loss of generality, we may suppose that
p=\mathbf{0}
so that
\Pi_{pV_{0}}=\Pi
.
Let
\sigma=\nabla_{\phi}\phi(p)
. By Lemma
2.9
, we have
\phi(q)=\sigma x(q)+O(\|q\|_{\mathrm{Kor}}^{2})
for any
q\in\mathbb{H}
. Then there is a
c>C
such that

d_{\mathrm{Kor}}(\Psi_{\phi}(q),\Pi_{W}(q))=d_{\mathrm{Kor}}(qY^{\phi(q)-y(q)},qY^{\sigma x(q)-y(q)})=|\phi(q)-\sigma x(q)|\leq c\|q\|_{\mathrm{Kor}}^{2}.

In particular, for
r>0
, if
q\in W\cap B_{r-cr^{2}}
, then
d_{\mathrm{Kor}}(\Psi_{\phi}(q),q)\leq c\|q\|_{\mathrm{Kor}}^{2}\leq cr^{2}
. Therefore,
\Psi_{\phi}(q)\in B_{r}
and

q=\Pi_{W}(\Psi_{\phi}(q))\in\Pi_{W}(\Gamma\cap B_{r}),

so
W\cap B_{r-cr^{2}}\subset\Pi_{W}(\Gamma\cap B_{r})
.

Conversely, if
q^{\prime}\in\Gamma\cap B_{r}
, then
\Pi_{W}(q^{\prime})\in W\cap B_{r+cr^{2}}
, so
\Pi_{W}(\Gamma\cap B_{r})\subset W\cap B_{r+cr^{2}}
.
This proves the first part of the lemma.

Similarly, since
\|\phi\|_{\infty}\leq C
, we have

d_{\mathrm{Kor}}(\Psi_{\phi}(q),\Pi(q))=d_{\mathrm{Kor}}(qY^{\phi(q)-y(q)},qY^{-y(q)})=|\phi(q)|\leq C

for all
q
. Therefore,

V_{0}\cap B_{R-c}\subset\Pi(\Gamma\cap B_{R})\subset V_{0}\cap B_{R+c},

as desired.
∎

This lets us write
T\eta_{\phi}(p)
in terms of an integral on
pV_{0}
.

Lemma 5.5
.

Let
\Gamma=\Gamma_{\phi}
,
p\in\Gamma
, and
W
be as in Lemma
5.4
. For
0<r<R
, let

E_{r,R}=(pV_{0}\cap B(p,R))\setminus\Pi_{pV_{0}}(W\cap B(p,r)).

Then
T\eta_{\phi}(\mathbf{0})
exists and

T\eta_{\phi}(\mathbf{0})=\lim_{\begin{subarray}{c}r\to 0\\
R\to\infty\end{subarray}}\int_{E_{r,R}}\widehat{K}(\Psi_{\phi}(v))\,\mathrm{d}v.

(37)

Proof.

Again, we suppose that
p=\mathbf{0}
, so that
E_{r,R}=(V_{0}\cap B_{R})\setminus\Pi(W\cap B_{r})
.

We first note that the limit on the right side of (
37
) exists. If
r
is sufficiently small and
R
is sufficiently large, then
\Pi(W\cap B_{r})\subset V_{0}\cap B_{R}
. If in addition
0<r^{\prime}<r<R<R^{\prime}
, then
E_{r,R}\subset E_{r^{\prime},R^{\prime}}
and

E_{r^{\prime},R^{\prime}}\setminus E_{r,R}=\Pi(A^{W}_{r^{\prime},r})\cup A_{R,R^{\prime}},

so by Lemma
5.2
,

\left|\int_{E_{r^{\prime},R^{\prime}}}\widehat{K}(\Psi_{\phi}(v))\,\mathrm{d}v-\int_{E_{r,R}}\widehat{K}(\Psi_{\phi}(v))\,\mathrm{d}v\right|\lesssim r+R^{-1}.

As
r\to 0
and
R\to\infty
, this goes to zero, so the limit in (
37
) exists.

Now we compare this limit with
T\eta_{\phi}
.
For any
r>0
and any vertical plane
P
through
\mathbf{0}
, let

F^{P}_{r}=(P\cap B_{r})\mathbin{\triangle}\Pi_{P}(\Gamma\cap B_{r})

where
A\mathbin{\triangle}B
is the symmetric difference
(A\setminus B)\cup(B\setminus A)
.
Comparing
E_{r,R}
to
\Pi(\Gamma\cap(B_{R}\setminus B_{r}))
, we find that

(V_{0}\cap B_{R})\mathbin{\triangle}\Pi(\Gamma\cap B_{R})=F^{V_{0}}_{R},

and

\Pi(W\cap B_{r})\mathbin{\triangle}\Pi(\Gamma\cap B_{r})=\Pi((W\cap B_{r})\mathbin{\triangle}\Pi_{W}(\Gamma\cap B_{r}))=\Pi(F^{W}_{r}),

so

E_{r,R}\mathbin{\triangle}\Pi(\Gamma\cap(B_{R}\setminus B_{r}))\subset F^{V_{0}}_{R}\cup\Pi(F^{W}_{r}).

Therefore, as in (
29
) and (
31
),

\displaystyle\left|T_{r,R}\eta_{\phi}(p)-\int_{E_{r,R}}\widehat{K}(\Psi_{\phi}(v))\,\mathrm{d}v\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\int_{\Gamma\cap(B_{R}\setminus B_{r})}\widehat{K}(w)\,\mathrm{d}w-\int_{E_{r,R}}\widehat{K}(\Psi_{\phi}(w))\,\mathrm{d}w\right|

\displaystyle\leq\int_{F^{V_{0}}_{R}\cup\Pi(F^{W}_{r})}\left|\widehat{K}(\Psi_{\phi}(w))\right|\,\mathrm{d}w.

Let
c
be as in Lemma
5.4
. Then for
0<r<R<\infty
,

F^{W}_{r}\subset W\cap(B_{r+cr^{2}}\setminus B_{r-cr^{2}})=A^{W}_{r-cr^{2},r+cr^{2}},

and

F_{R}^{V_{0}}\subset A_{R-c,R+c}.

By Lemma
5.3
, and using that
\Psi_{\phi}
is constant on cosets of
\langle Y\rangle
, we get

\displaystyle\left|T_{r,R}\eta_{\phi}(p)-\int_{E_{r,R}}\widehat{K}(\Psi_{\phi}(v))\,\mathrm{d}v\right|

\displaystyle\leq\int_{A_{R-c,R+c}}\left|\widehat{K}(\Psi_{\phi}(w))\right|\,\mathrm{d}w+\int_{\Pi(A^{W}_{r-cr^{2},r+cr^{2}})}\left|\widehat{K}(\Psi_{\phi}(w))\right|\,\mathrm{d}w

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{eq:def-dv}}{=}\int_{A_{R-c,R+c}}\left|\widehat{K}(\Psi_{\phi}(w))\right|\,\mathrm{d}w+\int_{A^{W}_{r-cr^{2},r+cr^{2}}}\left|\widehat{K}(\Psi_{\phi}(w))\right|\,\mathrm{d}w

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{R-c}^{R+c}\rho^{-3}\cdot\rho^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\rho+\int_{r-cr^{2}}^{r+cr^{2}}\rho^{-3}\cdot\rho^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\rho

\displaystyle=\log\frac{R+c}{R-c}+\log\frac{r+cr^{2}}{r-cr^{2}}.

This goes to zero as
r\to 0
and
R\to\infty
, so it implies (
37
), as desired.
∎

Finally, we prove Proposition
5.1
.

Proof of Proposition
5.1
.

Again, we suppose that
p=\mathbf{0}
.
Let
W
and
E_{r,R}=(V_{0}\cap B_{R})\setminus\Pi(W\cap B_{r})
be as in Lemma
5.5
. We may suppose that
R
is large enough and
r
is small enough that
\Pi(W\cap B_{r})\subset V_{0}\cap B_{R}
.
Let
P
be a vertical plane through
\mathbf{0}
with
|\slope P|<C
and let

J_{r,R}:=\left|\int_{E_{r,R}}\widehat{K}(\Psi_{\phi}(v))\,\mathrm{d}v-\tilde{T}^{P}_{\phi;r,R}\mathbf{1}(\mathbf{0})\right|=\left|\int_{E_{r,R}}\widehat{K}(\Psi_{\phi}(v))\,\mathrm{d}v-\int_{A^{P}_{r,R}}\widehat{K}(\Psi_{\phi}(v))\,\mathrm{d}v\right|.

We claim that
J_{r,R}\lesssim r+R^{-1}
.

Since
\Pi\circ\theta=\theta\circ\Pi
, it follows that
E_{r,R}
and
A^{P}_{r,R}
are symmetric around the
z
–axis, i.e.,
\theta(E_{r,R})=E_{r,R}
and
\theta(A^{P}_{r,R})=A^{P}_{r,R}
. Let
D\subset V_{0}
be a Borel set such that
D\subset A_{\epsilon,\epsilon^{-1}}
for some
\epsilon>0
.
We claim that if
\theta(D)=D
, then for any
t>0
,

\int_{s_{t}(D)}\widehat{K}(\Psi_{\phi}(v))\,\mathrm{d}v\lesssim_{\epsilon}\min\{t,t^{-1}\}.

Let
I_{t}=\int_{s_{t}(D)}\widehat{K}(\Psi_{\phi}(v))\,\mathrm{d}v
. As in the proof of Lemma
5.2
, the
\mathbb{H}
–oddness of
K
and the mean value theorem imply that

|I_{t}|=\frac{1}{2}\left|\int_{s_{t}(D)}-\widehat{K}(\theta(\Psi_{\phi}(v)))+\widehat{K}(\Psi_{\phi}(\theta(v)))\,\mathrm{d}v\right|\\
\leq\frac{1}{2}\int_{A_{t\epsilon,t\epsilon^{-1}}}|Y_{\mathsf{L}}\widehat{K}(m(v))|\cdot|\phi(v)+\phi(\theta(v))|\,\mathrm{d}v,

where for every
v
,
m(v)
is a point on the horizontal line segment between
\theta(\Psi_{\phi}(v))
and
\Psi_{\phi}(\theta(v))
with
\|m(v)\|_{\mathrm{Kor}}\approx\|v\|_{\mathrm{Kor}}
. As in (
36
), by (
35
), the
(-4)
–homogeneity of
Y_{\mathsf{L}}\widehat{K}
, and Lemma
5.3
,

|I_{t}|\lesssim\int_{A_{t\epsilon,t\epsilon^{-1}}}\min\{\|v\|_{\mathrm{Kor}}^{-4},\|v\|_{\mathrm{Kor}}^{-2}\}\,\mathrm{d}v\lesssim\int_{t\epsilon}^{t\epsilon^{-1}}\min\{\rho^{-4},\rho^{-2}\}\cdot\rho^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\rho\\
\lesssim\min\{t^{-1}\epsilon^{-1},t\epsilon^{-1}\}\lesssim_{\epsilon}\min\{t^{-1},t\}.

It follows that if
g
is an
\mathbb{H}
–even bounded Borel function supported on
A_{\epsilon,\epsilon^{-1}}
and
t>0
, then

\left|\int_{V_{0}}\widehat{K}(\Psi_{\phi}(v))g(s_{t}(v))\,\mathrm{d}v\right|\lesssim\|g\|_{\infty}\min\{t^{-1},t\}.

(38)

Now we apply this to
J_{r,R}
. The supports of
\mathbf{1}_{E_{r,R}}
and
\mathbf{1}_{\Pi(A^{P}_{r,R})}
are too large to apply (
38
) directly, but we can write

\mathbf{1}_{E_{r,R}}=\mathbf{1}_{V_{0}\cap B_{R}}-\mathbf{1}_{\Pi(W\cap B_{r})}

\mathbf{1}_{\Pi(A^{P}_{r,R})}=\mathbf{1}_{\Pi(P\cap B_{R})}-\mathbf{1}_{\Pi(P\cap B_{r})}.

Let
g=\mathbf{1}_{V_{0}\cap B_{1}}-\mathbf{1}_{\Pi(P\cap B_{1})}
and
h=\mathbf{1}_{\Pi(P\cap B_{1})}-\mathbf{1}_{\Pi(W\cap B_{1})}
so that

\mathbf{1}_{E_{r,R}}-\mathbf{1}_{\Pi(A^{P}_{r,R})}=(\mathbf{1}_{V_{0}\cap B_{R}}-\mathbf{1}_{\Pi(P\cap B_{R})})+(\mathbf{1}_{\Pi(P\cap B_{r})}-\mathbf{1}_{\Pi(W\cap B_{r})})=g\circ s_{R^{-1}}+h\circ s_{r^{-1}}.

Then
g
and
h
are
\mathbb{H}
–even, and there is an
\epsilon>0
such that both are supported in
A_{\epsilon,\epsilon^{-1}}
. Therefore,

\displaystyle J_{r,R}

\displaystyle=\left|\int_{V_{0}}\widehat{K}(\Psi_{\phi}(v))\mathbf{1}_{E_{r,R}}\,\mathrm{d}v-\int_{P}\widehat{K}(\Psi_{\phi}(v))\mathbf{1}_{A^{P}_{r,R}}\,\mathrm{d}v\right|

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{eq:def-dv}}{=}\left|\int_{V_{0}}\widehat{K}(\Psi_{\phi}(v))(\mathbf{1}_{E_{r,R}}-\mathbf{1}_{\Pi(A^{P}_{r,R})})\,\mathrm{d}v\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\int_{V_{0}}\widehat{K}(\Psi_{\phi}(v))(g(s_{R^{-1}}(v))+h(s_{r^{-1}}(v)))\,\mathrm{d}v\right|

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{eq:symmetric-function}}{\lesssim}r+R^{-1}.

This implies

\tilde{T}^{P}_{\phi}\mathbf{1}(\mathbf{0})=\lim_{\begin{subarray}{c}r\to 0\\
R\to\infty\end{subarray}}\int_{A^{P}_{r,R}}\widehat{K}(\Psi_{\phi}(v))\,\mathrm{d}v=\lim_{\begin{subarray}{c}r\to 0\\
R\to\infty\end{subarray}}\int_{E_{r,R}}\widehat{K}(\Psi_{\phi}(v))\,\mathrm{d}v,

so by Lemma
5.5
,
\tilde{T}^{P}_{\phi}\mathbf{1}(\mathbf{0})=T\eta_{\phi}(\mathbf{0})
, as desired.
∎

6.
Singular integrals on perturbed surfaces and the proof of Proposition
1.3

In Section
3
, we constructed intrinsic Lipschitz functions
f_{i}
that depend on parameters
A
,
\rho
, and
i
. In that section,
A
and
\rho
were fixed while
i
varies; in this section, we will need to vary
A
and
\rho
, so we will write
f_{i}
as
f_{i,A,\rho}
when we need to specify
A
and
\rho
.

Each surface
\Gamma_{f_{n,A,\rho}}
can be constructed by starting with the vertical plane
V_{0}
, then repeatedly perturbing it at smaller and smaller scales. In this section, we will state bounds on the change in the singular integral
\tilde{T}_{\zeta}\mathbf{1}
when
\zeta
is perturbed and use these bounds to prove Proposition
1.3
.

For any intrinsic Lipschitz function
\zeta\colon\mathbb{H}\to\mathbb{R}
, we let
F_{\zeta}\colon\mathbb{H}\to\mathbb{R}
,

F_{\zeta}(p):=\tilde{T}_{\zeta}\mathbf{1}(p)=\pv(\Psi_{\zeta}(p))\int_{\Psi_{\zeta}(p)V_{0}}\widehat{K}(\Psi_{\zeta}(p)^{-1}\Psi_{\zeta}(v))\,\mathrm{d}v.

(39)

For any
\psi\colon\mathbb{H}\to\mathbb{R}
which is constant on cosets of
\langle Y\rangle
and
t\in\mathbb{R}
, let
G_{\zeta,\psi}(t):=F_{\zeta+t\psi}
. We can then bound
F_{\zeta+\psi}-F_{\zeta}=G_{\zeta,\psi}(1)-G_{\zeta,\psi}(0)
by bounding the derivatives of
G_{\zeta,\psi}
.
In our applications,
\zeta
and
\psi
will satisfy bounds like those in Lemma
3.12
, so that the length scale of
\psi
is much smaller than the length scale of
\zeta
.

We denote
G^{\prime}_{\zeta,\psi}(t)(p)=\partial_{t}[G_{\zeta,\psi}(t)(p)]
and
G^{\prime\prime}_{\zeta,\psi}(t)(p)=\partial^{2}_{t}[G_{\zeta,\psi}(t)(p)]
. (This is a slight abuse of notation because the limits in the partial derivatives may only converge pointwise and not uniformly.)
For
r\leq R
, we define truncations

F^{r,R}_{\zeta}(p):=\tilde{T}^{r,R}_{\zeta}\mathbf{1}(p)=\int_{\Psi_{\zeta}(p)A_{r,R}}\widehat{K}(\Psi_{\zeta}(p)^{-1}\Psi_{\zeta}(v))\,\mathrm{d}v

(40)

and
G^{r,R}_{\zeta,\psi}(t):=F^{r,R}_{\zeta+t\psi}
. We will prove the following formula for
G_{\zeta,\psi}^{\prime}
.

Proposition 6.1
.

Let
\zeta,\psi\colon\mathbb{H}\to\mathbb{R}
be smooth functions that are constant on cosets of
\langle Y\rangle
. Suppose that
\|\psi\|_{\infty}<\infty
and that
\zeta
is intrinsic Lipschitz. Then, for any
p\in\Gamma_{\zeta}
,

\lim_{\begin{subarray}{c}r\to 0\\
R\to\infty\end{subarray}}(G^{r,R}_{\zeta,\psi})^{\prime}(0)(p)=G^{\prime}_{\zeta,\psi}(0)(p).

Furthermore, there is a Sobolev-type norm
\|\psi\|_{W_{\zeta}}
depending on
\psi
and its derivatives of order at most
2
such that

|G_{\zeta,\psi}^{\prime}(0)(\mathbf{0})|\lesssim_{\zeta}\|\psi\|_{W_{\zeta}}.

If
\alpha
and
\gamma
satisfy the bounds in Lemma
3.12
for some
c>0
, then

\|G_{\alpha,\gamma}^{\prime}(0)\|_{\infty}\lesssim_{c}A^{-1}.

We refer the reader to Lemma
7.1
for the details of the bound on
|G_{\zeta,\psi}^{\prime}(0)(\mathbf{0})|
.

To use this to bound
F_{\zeta+\psi}=G_{\zeta,\psi}(1)
, we need the following proposition, which likewise bounds
G^{\prime\prime}_{\zeta,\psi}
in terms of a Sobolev-type norm on
\zeta
and
\psi
. Let
A>1
and let
\hat{\partial}=A\nabla_{\zeta}
. As in Section
3
, we let
\{Z,\hat{\partial}\}^{n}
denote the set of differential operators that can be written as words of length
n
. Let
\{Z,\hat{\partial}\}^{*}
denote the set of all words.

Proposition 6.2
.

For any
A>1
and any
C>0
, if
\rho
is sufficiently large, then the following bounds hold. Let
\zeta,\psi\colon\mathbb{H}\to\mathbb{R}
be constant on cosets of
\langle Y\rangle
. Suppose that for any word
E\in\{Z,\hat{\partial}\}^{*}
of length at most 3,

\|E\psi\|_{\infty}\leq CA^{-1}

(41)

and if
E\not\in\{\id,\hat{\partial}\}
,

\|E\zeta\|_{\infty}\leq C\rho^{-1}.

(42)

Then, for any
p\in\mathbb{H}
, the function
t\mapsto G_{\zeta,\psi}(t)(p)
is
C^{2}
and satisfies

\left\|G^{\prime\prime}_{\zeta,\psi}(t)\right\|_{\infty}\lesssim_{C}A^{-3}

for all
t\in[0,1]
.
If
\alpha
and
\gamma
satisfy the bounds in Lemma
3.12
for some
c>0
, then

\|G_{\alpha,\gamma}^{\prime\prime}(0)\|_{\infty}\lesssim_{c}A^{-3}.

We will use Proposition
6.1
and Proposition
6.2
to prove the following bounds on
F_{f_{i}}
.

Lemma 6.3
.

Let
A>0
and suppose that
\rho>0
is sufficiently large, depending on
A
.
Let
i\geq 0
and let
f_{i}
and
\nu_{i}
be as in Section
3
. Then there is an
\epsilon>0
such that:

(1)

\|G_{f_{i},\nu_{i}}^{\prime}(0)\|_{\infty}\lesssim A^{-1}
.

(2)

For each
v\in\mathbb{H}
, the function
t\mapsto G_{f_{i},\nu_{i}}(t)(v)
is
C^{2}
, and for all
t\in[0,1]
,

\|G_{f_{i},\nu_{i}}^{\prime\prime}(t)\|_{\infty}\lesssim A^{-3}
.

(3)

For all
0\leq i<j
,

|\langle G_{f_{i},\nu_{i}}^{\prime}(0),G_{f_{j},\nu_{j}}^{\prime}(0)\rangle|\lesssim\rho^{-\epsilon}.

(43)

(4)

If
K
is the Riesz kernel
\mathsf{R}
and
i<\epsilon A^{4}
, then
\|G_{f_{i},\nu_{i}}^{\prime}(0)\|_{L_{2}(U)}\gtrsim A^{-1}
.

This lemma implies Proposition
1.3
.

Proof of Proposition
1.3
.

In this proof, we use
\|\cdot\|_{U}
to denote
\|\cdot\|_{L_{2}(U)}
. Let
g_{i}=G_{f_{i},\nu_{i}}
.
By Taylor’s theorem,

\|F_{f_{i+1}}-(F_{f_{i}}+g_{i}^{\prime}(0))\|_{U}\lesssim\sup_{0\leq t\leq 1}\|g_{i}^{\prime\prime}(t)\|_{U}.

Therefore, for any
n
,

\left\|F_{f_{n}}-\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}g^{\prime}_{i}(0)\right\|_{U}\lesssim\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}\sup_{0\leq t\leq 1}\|g_{i}^{\prime\prime}(t)\|_{U}.

(44)

Furthermore,

\displaystyle\left\|\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}g_{i}^{\prime}(0)\right\|_{U}^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i=0,\dots,n-1\\
j=0,\dots,n-1\end{subarray}}\langle g_{i}^{\prime}(0),g_{j}^{\prime}(0)\rangle

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}\|g_{i}^{\prime}(0)\|_{U}^{2}+2\sum_{0\leq i<j<n}\langle g_{i}^{\prime}(0),g_{j}^{\prime}(0)\rangle.

Let
\epsilon
be as in Lemma
6.3
and suppose that
n<\epsilon A^{4}
. Then on one hand,

\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}\left\|g_{i}^{\prime}(0)\right\|_{U}^{2}\approx nA^{-2}.

On the other hand,

\sum_{0\leq i<j<n}|\langle g_{i}^{\prime}(0),g_{j}^{\prime}(0)\rangle|\lesssim n^{2}\rho^{-\epsilon},

so if
\rho
is sufficiently large, then

\left\|\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}g_{i}^{\prime}(0)\right\|_{U}\gtrsim\sqrt{n}A^{-1},

(45)

while

\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}\sup_{0\leq t\leq 1}\|g_{i}^{\prime\prime}(t)\|_{U}\lesssim nA^{-3}.

(46)

Combining these estimates with (
44
), we see that there is some
c>1
such that

\|F_{f_{n}}\|_{U}\geq c^{-1}\sqrt{n}A^{-1}-cnA^{-3}.

Let
\delta=\min\{\epsilon,c^{-4}/16\}
and take
N=\lfloor\delta A^{4}\rfloor
. When
A
is sufficiently large,

\|F_{f_{N}}\|_{U}\geq\frac{c^{-3}}{5}A-\frac{c^{-3}}{15}A\gtrsim A,

as desired.
∎

These bounds point to a possible link between the norm of
F_{f_{n}}
and the
\beta
–numbers studied in Section
4
. The bounds in Section
4
show that there is a
\delta>0
such that if
\rho
is sufficiently large and
n<\delta A^{4}
, then

\int_{0}^{R}\int_{\Psi_{f_{n}}(U)}\beta_{\Gamma_{f_{n}}}(v,r)^{2}\,\mathrm{d}v\frac{\,\mathrm{d}r}{r}\gtrsim nA^{-2}.

(47)

Each layer of bumps with aspect ratio
A
contributes roughly
A^{-2}
to the integral.

Similarly, the proof of Proposition
1.3
shows that if
\rho
is sufficiently large and
n<\delta A^{4}
, then

\|F_{f_{n}}\|_{U}^{2}\approx nA^{-2}.

Indeed, the proof shows that

\|F_{f_{n}}\|_{U}^{2}\approx\sum_{i=1}^{n}\|g_{i}^{\prime}(0)\|^{2}_{U}
when
\frac{n}{A^{4}}
is small. Since
\|g_{i}^{\prime}(0)\|^{2}_{U}\approx A^{-2}
when
i<\epsilon A^{4}
, each step in the construction of
f_{n}
contributes roughly
A^{-2}
to
\|F_{f_{n}}\|_{U}^{2}
.

This suggests the following question.

Question 6.4
.

How is the integral (
47
) for an intrinsic Lipschitz graph
\Gamma
related to the
L_{2}
–norm of the Riesz transform for functions on
\Gamma
?

In the rest of this paper, we will prove Propositions
6.1
and
6.2
and Lemma
6.3
.

We prove Proposition
6.1
in Section
7
. The key step is to write
G^{r,R}_{\zeta,\psi}(t)
in two ways, (
52
) and (
53
).
The Euclidean analogues of these expressions are identical, but since
\mathbb{H}
is noncommutative, they differ in
\mathbb{H}
. In practice, (
52
) is easier to bound when
r
and
R
are large and (
53
) is easier when
r
and
R
are small, so the two expressions together let us bound
G^{r,R}_{\zeta,\psi}
and its derivatives at all scales.

By Section
3.1
, we can rescale
f_{i}
and
\nu_{i}
to obtain functions
\alpha
and
\gamma
that satisfy the bounds in Lemma
3.12
. By the scale-invariance of the Riesz transform,
\|G_{f_{i},\nu_{i}}^{\prime}(0)\|_{\infty}=\|G_{\alpha,\gamma}^{\prime}(0)\|_{\infty}
, so part (
1
) of Lemma
6.3
follows from Proposition
6.1
.

Similarly, in Section
11
, we use (
52
) and (
53
) again to prove Proposition
6.2
. As before,
\|G_{f_{i},\nu_{i}}^{\prime\prime}(0)\|_{\infty}=\|G_{\alpha,\gamma}^{\prime\prime}(0)\|_{\infty}
, so part (
2
) of Lemma
6.3
follows from Proposition
6.2
.

To prove parts (
3
) and (
4
) of Lemma
6.3
, we approximate
G_{\alpha,\gamma}^{\prime}(0)
by a translation-invariant singular integral operator on a plane. For any vertical plane
P\subset\mathbb{H}
, let
\lambda_{P}\colon\mathbb{H}\to\mathbb{R}
be the affine function such that
\Gamma_{\lambda_{P}}=P
, and let
H_{P,\gamma}\colon P\to\mathbb{R}
be the function

H_{P,\gamma}(q):=G^{\prime}_{\lambda_{P},\gamma}(0)(q)

for all
q\in P
. The map
\gamma\mapsto H_{P,\gamma}
is then a translation-invariant operator from functions on
P
to functions on
P
.

Given
v\in\Gamma_{\alpha}
, we let
P_{v}
be the vertical tangent plane to
\Gamma_{\alpha}
at
v
.
By Lemma
2.9
and Lemma
3.12
,
P_{v}
is close to
\Gamma_{\alpha}
on a ball around
v
whose radius grows with
\rho
. In Section
8
, we show that
H_{P_{v},\gamma}
approximates
G^{\prime}_{\alpha,\gamma}(0)
on a ball around
v
whose radius also grows with
\rho
.
We use this approximation to prove the lower bound
\|G_{f_{i},\nu_{i}}^{\prime}(0)\|_{L_{2}(U)}\gtrsim A^{-1}
(Section
9
), to prove that
G_{f_{i},\nu_{i}}^{\prime}(0)
is continuous as a function from
V_{0}
to
\mathbb{R}
(Lemma
8.2
), and to prove the orthogonality bound (
43
) (Section
10
). This completes the proof of Lemma
6.3
.

7.
First-order estimates for
\tilde{T}_{\zeta+\psi}

Let
\zeta,\psi\colon\mathbb{H}\to\mathbb{R}
be smooth functions that are constant on cosets of
\langle Y\rangle
and suppose that
\zeta(\mathbf{0})=0
. Let
G_{\zeta,\psi}(t)=F_{\zeta+t\psi}=\tilde{T}_{\zeta+t\psi}\mathbf{1}
and
G^{r,R}_{\zeta,\psi}=F^{r,R}_{\zeta+t\psi}
be as in Section
6
.

In this section, we will derive expressions for
(G^{r,R}_{\zeta,\psi})^{\prime}(t)
and prove the following lemma, which is a quantitative version of Proposition
6.1
.

Lemma 7.1
.

Let
\zeta,\psi\colon\mathbb{H}\to\mathbb{R}
be smooth functions that are constant on cosets of
\langle Y\rangle
. Suppose that
\|\psi\|_{\infty}<\infty
and that
\zeta
is intrinsic Lipschitz. Then, for any
p\in\Gamma_{\zeta}
,

\lim_{\begin{subarray}{c}r\to 0\\
R\to\infty\end{subarray}}(G^{r,R}_{\zeta,\psi})^{\prime}(0)(p)=G^{\prime}_{\zeta,\psi}(0)(p).

Furthermore, let
0<r<1
. Let
L=\|\nabla_{\zeta}\zeta\|_{\infty}
and let
B=B(p,(2L+1)r)
. For a smooth function
g\colon\mathbb{H}\to\mathbb{R}
, define

\|g\|_{W_{\zeta}(B)}:=\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}E\in\{\partial_{z},\nabla_{\zeta}\}^{*}\\
\ell(E)\leq 2\end{subarray}}\|E\psi\|_{L_{\infty}(B)},

and

\|g\|_{W^{\prime}_{\zeta}(B)}:=\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}E\in\{\partial_{z},\nabla_{\zeta}\}^{*}\setminus\{\id,\nabla_{\zeta}\}\\
\ell(E)\leq 2\end{subarray}}\|E\psi\|_{L_{\infty}(B)}.

Then for any
C>0
and any
s
and
S
such that
0<s\leq r\leq S
, if
\|\zeta\|_{W^{\prime}_{\zeta}(B)}\leq C
, then

\left|G_{\zeta,\psi}^{\prime}(0)(p)-\big(G^{s,S}_{\zeta,\psi}\big)^{\prime}(0)(p)\right|\lesssim_{L,C}\|\psi\|_{W_{\zeta}(B)}s+\|\psi\|_{\infty}S^{-1}.

In particular, since
G^{r,r}_{\zeta,\psi}(t)(p)=0
for all
t
and
p
,

|G_{\zeta,\psi}^{\prime}(0)(p)|=|G_{\zeta,\psi}^{\prime}(0)(p)-(G^{r,r}_{\zeta,\psi})^{\prime}(0)(p)|\lesssim_{L,C}(r+r^{-1})\|\psi\|_{W_{\zeta}(B)}.

Proposition
6.1
follows immediately.

Proof of Proposition
6.1
.

Suppose that
\alpha
and
\gamma
are as in Lemma
3.12
. Then
\|\nabla_{\alpha}\alpha\|_{\infty}\leq 1
,
\|\alpha\|_{W^{\prime}_{\alpha}(\mathbb{H})}\lesssim A^{-1}\rho^{-1}
, and
\|\gamma\|_{W_{\alpha}(\mathbb{H})}\lesssim A^{-1}
when
\rho
is sufficiently large. Therefore, letting
r=1
,

|G_{\alpha,\gamma}^{\prime}(0)(p)|\lesssim A^{-1}.

∎

We prove Lemma
7.1
by calculating
G^{r,R}_{\zeta,\psi}(t)
in two ways. By left-invariance, it suffices to consider the case that
\zeta(\mathbf{0})=0
and
p=\mathbf{0}
. Then on one hand, by (
40
),

G^{r,R}_{\zeta,\psi}(t)(\mathbf{0})=\int_{Y^{t\psi(\mathbf{0})}A_{r,R}}\widehat{K}(\Psi_{\zeta+t\psi}(\mathbf{0})^{-1}\Psi_{\zeta+t\psi}(v))\,\mathrm{d}v.

The domain of integration depends on
t
, but since the integrand is constant on cosets of
\langle Y\rangle
, we can replace
Y^{t\psi(\mathbf{0})}A_{r,R}
by

A^{t}_{r,R}:=\Pi(Y^{t\psi(\mathbf{0})}A_{r,R})=Y^{t\psi(\mathbf{0})}A_{r,R}Y^{-t\psi(\mathbf{0})}.

This is a copy of
A_{r,R}
, sheared in the
z
–direction, and

G^{r,R}_{\zeta,\psi}(t)(\mathbf{0})=\int_{A^{t}_{r,R}}\widehat{K}(Y^{-t\psi(\mathbf{0})}\Psi_{\zeta}(v)Y^{t\psi(v)})\,\mathrm{d}v.

(48)

Differentiating (
48
) gives an expression for
(G^{r,R}_{\zeta,\psi})^{\prime}
which is found in Lemma
7.2
below. The changing boundary will lead to boundary terms in the derivative, but we will see that when
r
and
R
are large, this derivative is small.

On the other hand, just as we translated
\zeta
so that the graph of
\zeta
goes through
\mathbf{0}
, we can translate
\zeta+\tau\psi
so that its graph goes through
\mathbf{0}
. By Lemma
2.2
, there is a function
\zeta_{\tau}
such that
\Gamma_{\zeta_{\tau}}=Y^{-\tau\psi(\mathbf{0})}\Gamma_{\zeta+\tau\psi},
which can be written as follows. For any
\tau\in\mathbb{R}
and
w\in V_{0}
, let

w_{\tau}:=Y^{\tau\psi(\mathbf{0})}wY^{-\tau\psi(\mathbf{0})}=w-\tau\psi(\mathbf{0})x(w)Z

(49)

and

\zeta_{\tau}(w):=(\zeta+\tau\psi)(w_{\tau})-(\zeta+\tau\psi)(\mathbf{0}).

(50)

Then
\zeta_{0}=\zeta
,
\zeta_{\tau}(\mathbf{0})=0
, and

\Gamma_{\zeta_{\tau}}=Y^{-\tau\psi(\mathbf{0})}\Gamma_{\zeta+\tau\psi}.

By the left-invariance of
\tilde{T}
, for any
w\in V_{0}
and
\tau\in\mathbb{R}
, we have

G^{r,R}_{\zeta,\psi}(\tau)(w)=F^{r,R}_{\zeta+\tau\psi}(w)=F^{r,R}_{\zeta_{\tau}}(Y^{-\tau\psi(\mathbf{0})}w)=F^{r,R}_{\zeta_{\tau}}(w_{\tau}),

where the last equality uses the fact that
F^{r,R}_{\zeta_{\tau}}
is constant on cosets of
\langle Y\rangle
.
In particular,

G^{r,R}_{\zeta,\psi}(\tau)(\mathbf{0})=F^{r,R}_{\zeta_{\tau}}(\mathbf{0})=\int_{A_{r,R}}\widehat{K}(\Psi_{\zeta_{\tau}}(\mathbf{0})^{-1}\Psi_{\zeta_{\tau}}(v))\,\mathrm{d}v=\int_{A_{r,R}}\widehat{K}(\Psi_{\zeta_{\tau}}(v))\,\mathrm{d}v,

(51)

so we can compute
(G^{r,R}_{\zeta,\psi})^{\prime}
by differentiating (
51
) (see Lemma
7.3
below). This avoids the boundary terms in Lemma
7.2
. We will see that when
r
and
R
are small, the derivative of (
51
) is small.

We first consider the derivative of (
48
). For any
R>0
, let
M_{R}(x)=\frac{1}{4}\sqrt{R^{4}-x^{4}}
so that
B(\mathbf{0},R)\cap V_{0}=\{(x,0,z)\mid|x|\leq R,|z|\leq M_{R}(x)\}
. Recall that
Y_{\mathsf{L}}
is the left-invariant vector field
Y_{\mathsf{L}}(x,y,z):=(0,1,\frac{x}{2})
and
Y_{\mathsf{R}}
is the right-invariant vector field
Y_{\mathsf{R}}(x,y,z):=(0,1,-\frac{x}{2})
.

For the rest of this section, we suppose that
\zeta
and
\psi
are as in Lemma
7.1
and that
\zeta(\mathbf{0})=0
so that
\mathbf{0}\in\Gamma_{\zeta}
. We let
0<r<1
,
L=\|\nabla_{\zeta}\zeta\|_{\infty}
, and
B=B(\mathbf{0},(2L+1)r)
, and we suppose that
\|\zeta\|_{W^{\prime}_{\zeta}(B)}\leq C
. For
q\in\mathbb{H}
, we let
\overline{q}=\Psi_{\zeta}(q)
.

Lemma 7.2
.

Let
R^{\prime}>R>0
. Then

(G^{R,R^{\prime}}_{\zeta,\psi})^{\prime}(0)(\mathbf{0})=\int_{A_{R,R^{\prime}}}\psi(w)Y_{\mathsf{L}}\widehat{K}(\overline{w})-\psi(\mathbf{0})Y_{\mathsf{R}}\widehat{K}(\overline{w})\,\mathrm{d}q\\
-\psi(\mathbf{0})\int_{-R}^{R}x\cdot\widehat{K}(\Psi_{\zeta}(x,0,z))\bigg|_{z=-M_{R}(x)}^{M_{R}(x)}\,\mathrm{d}x+\psi(\mathbf{0})\int_{-r}^{r}x\cdot\widehat{K}(\Psi_{\zeta}(x,0,z))\bigg|_{z=-M_{r}(x)}^{M_{r}(x)}\,\mathrm{d}x.

(52)

where
f(z)\big|_{z=a}^{b}
denotes
f(b)-f(a)
.
Further,

\left|(G^{R,R^{\prime}}_{\zeta,\psi})^{\prime}(0)(\mathbf{0})\right|\lesssim\|\psi\|_{\infty}R^{-1}.

Proof.

We proceed by differentiating (
48
). By the definition of
M_{R}
, we have

A_{R,R^{\prime}}=\{(x,0,z)\mid z\in[-M_{R^{\prime}}(x),M_{R^{\prime}}(x)]\setminus(-M_{R}(x),M_{R}(x))\}.

Let
A_{R,R^{\prime}}(x,t):=\{z\mid(x,0,z)\in A^{t}_{R,R^{\prime}}\}
. Since
Y^{y}(x,0,z)Y^{-y}=(x,0,z-yx)
, we have

A_{R,R^{\prime}}(x,t)=[-M_{R^{\prime}}(x)-t\psi(\mathbf{0})x,M_{R^{\prime}}(x)-t\psi(\mathbf{0})x]\\
\setminus(-M_{R}(x)-t\psi(\mathbf{0})x,M_{R}(x)-t\psi(\mathbf{0})x).

Let
w=(x,0,z)
and
\lambda_{t}(w)=Y^{-t\psi(\mathbf{0})}\overline{w}Y^{t\psi(w)}
. Then by (
48
),

\displaystyle G^{R,R^{\prime}}_{\zeta,\psi}(t)

\displaystyle=\int_{-R^{\prime}}^{R^{\prime}}\int_{A_{R,R^{\prime}}(x,t)}\widehat{K}(\lambda_{t}(x,0,z))\,\mathrm{d}z\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle(G^{R,R^{\prime}}_{\zeta,\psi})^{\prime}(t)

\displaystyle=\int_{A^{t}_{R,R^{\prime}}}\frac{\,\mathrm{d}}{\,\mathrm{d}t}[\widehat{K}(\lambda_{t}(q))]\,\mathrm{d}q-\int_{-R^{\prime}}^{R^{\prime}}\psi(\mathbf{0})x\cdot\widehat{K}(\lambda_{t}(x,0,z))\bigg|_{z=-M_{R^{\prime}}(x)-t\psi(\mathbf{0})x}^{M_{R^{\prime}}(x)-t\psi(\mathbf{0})x}\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\qquad+\int_{-R}^{R}\psi(\mathbf{0})x\cdot\widehat{K}(\lambda_{t}(x,0,z))\bigg|_{z=-M_{R}(x)-t\psi(\mathbf{0})x}^{M_{R}(x)-t\psi(\mathbf{0})x}\,\mathrm{d}x.

When
t=0
,

\displaystyle(G^{R,R^{\prime}}_{\zeta,\psi})^{\prime}(0)(\mathbf{0})

\displaystyle=\int_{A_{R,R^{\prime}}}\psi(q)Y_{\mathsf{L}}\widehat{K}(\overline{q})-\psi(\mathbf{0})Y_{\mathsf{R}}\widehat{K}(\overline{q})\,\mathrm{d}q

\displaystyle\qquad-\psi(\mathbf{0})\int_{-R^{\prime}}^{R^{\prime}}x\widehat{K}(\Psi_{\zeta}(x,0,z))\bigg|_{z=-M_{R^{\prime}}(x)}^{M_{R^{\prime}}(x)}\,\mathrm{d}x+\psi(\mathbf{0})\int_{-R}^{R}x\widehat{K}(\Psi_{\zeta}(x,0,z))\bigg|_{z=-M_{R}(x)}^{M_{R}(x)}\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle=:I_{1}-I_{2}^{R^{\prime}}+I_{2}^{R}.

This proves (
52
).

We thus consider
I_{1}
,
I_{2}^{R^{\prime}}
, and
I_{2}^{R}
. Since
Y_{\mathsf{L}}\widehat{K}
and
Y_{\mathsf{R}}\widehat{K}
are homogeneous of degree
-4
,

\displaystyle\left|\psi(w)Y_{\mathsf{L}}\widehat{K}(\overline{w})-\psi(\mathbf{0})Y_{\mathsf{R}}\widehat{K}(\overline{w})\right|\lesssim\|\psi\|_{\infty}\|w\|_{\mathrm{Kor}}^{-4}.

By Lemma
5.3
,

|I_{1}|\lesssim\int_{A_{R,R^{\prime}}}\|\psi\|_{\infty}\|w\|_{\mathrm{Kor}}^{-4}\,\mathrm{d}w\lesssim\int_{R}^{R^{\prime}}\|\psi\|_{\infty}\kappa^{-4}\cdot\kappa^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\kappa\leq\|\psi\|_{\infty}R^{-1}.

Let
s\in[R,R^{\prime}]
.
Since
\|(x,0,M_{s}(x))\|_{\mathrm{Kor}}=s
, we have
|\widehat{K}(\Psi_{\zeta}(x,0,M_{s}(x)))|\lesssim s^{-3}
and

|I^{s}_{2}(t)|\lesssim\psi(\mathbf{0})\int_{-s}^{s}|x|s^{-3}\,\mathrm{d}x\lesssim\|\psi\|_{\infty}s^{-1}.

Putting these bounds together,

|(G^{R,R^{\prime}}_{\zeta,\psi})^{\prime}(0)(\mathbf{0})|\lesssim\|\psi\|_{\infty}R^{-1}+\|\psi\|_{\infty}(R^{\prime})^{-1}+\|\psi\|_{\infty}R^{-1}\lesssim\|\psi\|_{\infty}R^{-1},

as desired.
∎

Now we differentiate (
51
).

Lemma 7.3
.

Let
0<s^{\prime}<s\leq r
.
Then

(G^{s^{\prime},s}_{\zeta,\psi})^{\prime}(0)(\mathbf{0})=\int_{A_{s^{\prime},s}}\left(\psi(w)-\psi(\mathbf{0})-\psi(\mathbf{0})x\partial_{z}\zeta(w)\right)Y_{\mathsf{L}}\widehat{K}(\overline{w})\,\mathrm{d}w.

(53)

Furthermore,

|(G^{s^{\prime},s}_{\zeta,\psi})^{\prime}(0)(\mathbf{0})|\lesssim_{C}\|\psi\|_{W_{\zeta}(B)}s.

(54)

To prove this lemma, we will need the following bound, which will also be used in Section
11
. Recall that
\theta(x,y,z)=(-x,-y,z)
is rotation around the
z
–axis.

Lemma 7.4
.

Let
j\geq 0
and let
M
be a smooth
(-j)
–homogeneous kernel. Then

\displaystyle|M(\Psi_{a}(w))-(-1)^{j}M(\Psi_{a}(\theta(w)))|\lesssim_{M,L}\|\zeta\|_{W^{\prime}_{\zeta}(B)}\|w\|_{\mathrm{Kor}}^{1-j},\qquad\forall w\in B(\mathbf{0},r).

Proof.

Since
M
is
(-j)
–homogeneous, we note that
M(\theta(p))=M(s_{-1}(p))=(-1)^{j}M(p)
for all
p
.
As in the proof of Lemma
5.2
, for all
w\in V_{0}
, the points
\theta(\Psi_{\zeta}(w))=\theta(w)Y^{-\zeta(w)}
and
\Psi_{\zeta}(\theta(w))=\theta(w)Y^{\zeta(\theta(w))}
lie in the same coset of
\langle Y\rangle
. Furthermore, by Lemma
2.9
, if
w\in B(\mathbf{0},r)
, then

\zeta(w)=x(w)\nabla_{\zeta}\zeta(\mathbf{0})+O_{L}(C\|w\|_{\mathrm{Kor}}^{2}),

so the distance between these points satisfies

\displaystyle|\zeta(w)+\zeta(\theta(w))|=(x(w)-x(w))\nabla_{\zeta}\zeta(\mathbf{0})+O_{L}(C\|w\|_{\mathrm{Kor}}^{2})\lesssim_{L}C\|w\|_{\mathrm{Kor}}^{2}.

(55)

By the Mean Value Theorem, there is a point
k(w)
lying on the horizontal line between
\theta(\Psi_{\zeta}(w))
and
\Psi_{\zeta}(\theta(w))
such that

|M(\Psi_{\zeta}(w))-(-1)^{j}M(\Psi_{\zeta}(\theta(w)))|=|M(\theta(\Psi_{\zeta}(w)))-M(\Psi_{\zeta}(\theta(w)))|\\
=|Y_{\mathsf{L}}M(k(w))||\zeta(w)+\zeta(\theta(w))|\lesssim_{L}C\|w\|_{\mathrm{Kor}}^{2}|Y_{\mathsf{L}}M(k(w))|.

Since
\zeta
is intrinsic Lipschitz with constant depending on
L
, we have

\|k(w)\|_{\mathrm{Kor}}\approx_{L}\|\Pi(w)\|_{\mathrm{Kor}}\leq 2\|w\|_{\mathrm{Kor}}.

By Lemma
2.1
,
Y_{\mathsf{L}}M
is
(-j-1)
–homogeneous, so

\displaystyle|M(\Psi_{a}(w))-(-1)^{j}M(\Psi_{a}(\theta(w)))|\lesssim_{L,M}C\|w\|_{\mathrm{Kor}}^{2}\|k(w)\|_{\mathrm{Kor}}^{-j-1}\lesssim_{L}C\|w\|_{\mathrm{Kor}}^{1-j}

as desired.
∎

Now we prove Lemma
7.3
. We take advantage of the symmetry of
A_{s^{\prime},s}
by decomposing functions into odd and even parts. For any function
f\colon\mathbb{H}\to\mathbb{R}
, we have the following even-odd decomposition:

\displaystyle f(w)=\frac{1}{2}(f(w)+f(\theta(w)))+\frac{1}{2}(f(w)-f(\theta(w)))=:f^{\mathsf{e}}(w)+f^{\mathsf{o}}(w).

(56)

Let
E\subseteq\mathbb{H}
be a subset for which
\theta(E)=E
. As
\int_{E}f(w)\,\mathrm{d}w=\int_{E}f(\theta(w))\,\mathrm{d}w
, we get that
\int_{E}f^{\mathsf{o}}(w)\,\mathrm{d}w=0
and so if
f
is integrable on
E
, then

\displaystyle\int_{E}f(w)\,\mathrm{d}w=\int_{E}f^{\mathsf{e}}(w)\,\mathrm{d}w.

(57)

Moreover, if
g\colon\mathbb{H}\to\mathbb{R}
and
fg
is integrable on
E
, then

\int_{E}fg\,\mathrm{d}w=\int_{E}(f^{\mathsf{e}}+f^{\mathsf{o}})(g^{\mathsf{e}}+g^{\mathsf{o}})\,\mathrm{d}w=\int_{E}f^{\mathsf{e}}g^{\mathsf{e}}+f^{\mathsf{o}}g^{\mathsf{o}}\,\mathrm{d}w.

(58)

Proof of Lemma
7.3
.

By (
51
),

(G^{s^{\prime},s}_{\zeta,\psi})^{\prime}(0)(\mathbf{0})=\frac{\,\mathrm{d}}{\,\mathrm{d}\tau}\left[\int_{A_{s^{\prime},s}}\widehat{K}(\Psi_{\zeta_{\tau}}(w))\,\mathrm{d}w\right]_{\tau=0}=\int_{A_{s^{\prime},s}}\partial_{\tau}[\zeta_{\tau}(w)](0)\cdot Y_{\mathsf{L}}\widehat{K}(\overline{w})\,\mathrm{d}w.

(59)

We differentiate (
50
) to get

\displaystyle\partial_{\tau}[\zeta_{\tau}(w)]

\displaystyle=-\psi(\mathbf{0})x(w)\partial_{z}[\zeta+\tau\psi](w_{\tau})+\psi(w_{\tau})-\psi(\mathbf{0})

where
w_{\tau}=w-\tau\psi(\mathbf{0})x(w)Z
is as in (
49
). Let

m(w)=\partial_{\tau}[\zeta_{\tau}(w)](0)=\psi(w)-\psi(\mathbf{0})-\psi(\mathbf{0})x(w)\partial_{z}\zeta(w),

so that

(G^{s^{\prime},s}_{\zeta,\psi})^{\prime}(0)(\mathbf{0})=\int_{A_{s^{\prime},s}}m(w)Y_{\mathsf{L}}\widehat{K}(\overline{w})\,\mathrm{d}w;

this is (
53
).

For
w\in V_{0}
, let
N_{Y_{\mathsf{L}}\widehat{K}}(w):=Y_{\mathsf{L}}\widehat{K}(\overline{w})
. We will estimate
I:=(G^{s^{\prime},s}_{\zeta,\psi})^{\prime}(0)(\mathbf{0})
by decomposing
m
and
N_{Y_{\mathsf{L}}\widehat{K}}
into odd and even parts.

By (
58
),

I=\int_{A_{s^{\prime},s}}m^{\mathsf{e}}(w)N_{Y_{\mathsf{L}}\widehat{K}}^{\mathsf{e}}(w)+m^{\mathsf{o}}(w)N_{Y_{\mathsf{L}}\widehat{K}}^{\mathsf{o}}(w)\,\mathrm{d}w.

Let
w\in B(\mathbf{0},r)
and let
\kappa=\|w\|_{\mathrm{Kor}}
. Note that
0\leq\kappa\leq r\leq 1
.
By Lemma
2.9
,

\psi(w)-\psi(\mathbf{0})=x(w)\nabla_{\zeta}\psi(\mathbf{0})+O(\|\psi\|_{W_{\zeta}(B)}\kappa^{2}),

and

\partial_{z}\zeta(w)=\partial_{z}\zeta(\mathbf{0})+O\left(\|\partial_{z}^{2}\zeta\|_{L_{\infty}(B)}\kappa^{2}+\|\nabla_{\zeta}\partial_{z}\zeta\|_{L_{\infty}(B)}\kappa\right)=\partial_{z}\zeta(\mathbf{0})+O(C\kappa).

Therefore,

m(w)=x(w)\nabla_{\zeta}\psi(\mathbf{0})-\psi(\mathbf{0})x(w)\partial_{z}\zeta(\mathbf{0})+O(\|\psi\|_{W_{\zeta}(B)}\kappa^{2})+O(\psi(\mathbf{0})x(w)C\kappa)\\
=x(w)\nabla_{\zeta}\psi(\mathbf{0})-\psi(\mathbf{0})x(w)\partial_{z}\zeta(\mathbf{0})+O\big(\|\psi\|_{W_{\zeta}(B)}(1+C)\kappa^{2}\big).

(60)

Thus
|m^{\mathsf{e}}(w)|\lesssim_{C}\|\psi\|_{W_{\zeta}(B)}\kappa^{2}
.

Similarly,
\psi(w)-\psi(\mathbf{0})=O(\|\psi\|_{W_{\zeta}(B)}\kappa)
and
\partial_{z}\zeta=O(\|\zeta\|_{W^{\prime}_{\zeta}(B)})
, so

m(w)=O(\|\psi\|_{W_{\zeta}(B)}\kappa)+O(\psi(0)\kappa\|\zeta\|_{W^{\prime}_{\zeta}(B)})=O(\|\psi\|_{W_{\zeta}(B)}(1+C)\kappa),

(61)

and
|m^{\mathsf{o}}(w)|\lesssim_{C}\|\psi\|_{W_{\zeta}(B)}\kappa
. Since
Y_{\mathsf{L}}\widehat{K}
is
-4
–homogeneous,
|N_{Y_{\mathsf{L}}\widehat{K}}^{\mathsf{e}}(w)|\lesssim\kappa^{-4}
. By Lemma
7.4
,
|N_{Y_{\mathsf{L}}\widehat{K}}^{\mathsf{o}}(w)|\lesssim_{C}\kappa^{-3}
.

Therefore,

|m^{\mathsf{e}}(w)N_{Y_{\mathsf{L}}\widehat{K}}^{\mathsf{e}}(w)+m^{\mathsf{o}}(w)N_{Y_{\mathsf{L}}\widehat{K}}^{\mathsf{o}}(w)|\lesssim_{C}\|\psi\|_{W_{\zeta}(B)}\kappa^{-2}.

By Lemma
5.3
,

|I|\lesssim_{C}\int_{s^{\prime}}^{s}\|\psi\|_{W_{\zeta}(B)}\kappa^{-2}\cdot\kappa^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\kappa\leq\|\psi\|_{W_{\zeta}(B)}s,

as desired.
∎

Finally, we prove Lemma
7.1
.

Proof of Lemma
7.1
.

For
w\in V_{0}
and
\tau\in\mathbb{R}
, let
\psi_{\tau}(w):=\psi(w_{\tau})
, where
w_{\tau}
is as in (
49
). Let
t\in\mathbb{R}
. Then by (
50
),

\zeta_{\tau+t}(w)=(\zeta+\tau\psi+t\psi)(w_{\tau+t})-(\zeta+\tau\psi+t\psi)(\mathbf{0})=(\zeta_{\tau}+t\psi_{\tau})(Y^{t\psi(\mathbf{0})}w)-t\psi(\mathbf{0}),

so by Lemma
2.2
,

\Gamma_{\zeta_{\tau+t}}=Y^{-t\psi(\mathbf{0})}\Gamma_{\zeta_{\tau}+t\psi_{\tau}}.

Therefore, for
s<S
,

G^{s,S}_{\zeta,\psi}(\tau+t)(\mathbf{0})=G^{s,S}_{\zeta_{\tau},\psi_{\tau}}(t)(\mathbf{0}).

Differentiating with respect to
t
gives

(G^{s,S}_{\zeta,\psi})^{\prime}(\tau)(\mathbf{0})=(G^{s,S}_{\zeta_{\tau},\psi_{\tau}})^{\prime}(0)(\mathbf{0}).

Let
0<s^{\prime}<s\leq r
and let
r\leq S<S^{\prime}
. Let
B^{\prime}=B(\mathbf{0},(2L+3)r)
.
If
\tau
is sufficiently small, then
\|\nabla_{\zeta_{\tau}}\zeta_{\tau}\|_{\infty}<L+1
,
\|\zeta_{\tau}\|_{W^{\prime}_{\zeta_{\tau}}(B^{\prime})}\leq C+1
, and
\|\psi_{\tau}\|_{W_{\zeta_{\tau}}(B^{\prime})}\leq 2\|\psi\|_{W_{\zeta}(B)}
.
Then Lemma
7.2
and Lemma
7.3
imply that

\left|(G^{s^{\prime},S^{\prime}}_{\zeta_{\tau},\psi_{\tau}})^{\prime}(0)(\mathbf{0})-(G^{s,S}_{\zeta_{\tau},\psi_{\tau}})^{\prime}(0)(\mathbf{0})\right|=\left|(G^{s^{\prime},s}_{\zeta_{\tau},\psi_{\tau}})^{\prime}(0)(\mathbf{0})+(G^{S,S^{\prime}}_{\zeta_{\tau},\psi_{\tau}})^{\prime}(0)(\mathbf{0})\right|\lesssim_{L,C}\|\psi\|_{\infty}s+\|\psi\|_{W_{\zeta}(B)}S^{-1}.

(62)

That is,
(G^{s,S}_{\zeta,\psi})^{\prime}(\tau)(\mathbf{0})=(G^{s,S}_{\zeta_{\tau},\psi_{\tau}})^{\prime}(0)(\mathbf{0})
is Cauchy as
s\to 0
and
S\to\infty
, with bounds independent of
\tau
.
Thus,
(G^{s,S}_{\zeta,\psi})^{\prime}(\tau)
converges uniformly as
s\to 0
and
S\to\infty
. This lets us pass the derivative under the limit, so by Lemma
5.2
,

G_{\zeta,\psi}^{\prime}(\tau)=\frac{\,\mathrm{d}}{\,\mathrm{d}\tau}\lim_{\begin{subarray}{c}s\to 0\\
S\to\infty\end{subarray}}G^{s,S}_{\zeta,\psi}(\tau)=\lim_{\begin{subarray}{c}s\to 0\\
S\to\infty\end{subarray}}(G^{s,S}_{\zeta,\psi})^{\prime}(\tau).

Finally, (
62
) implies that

|G_{\zeta,\psi}^{\prime}(0)(p)-(G^{s,S}_{\zeta,\psi})^{\prime}(0)(p)|\lesssim_{L,C}\|\psi\|_{\infty}s+\|\psi\|_{W_{\zeta}(B)},

as desired.
∎

This implies Proposition
6.1
and thus part (
1
) of Lemma
6.3
.

8.
Approximating by a planar singular integral

For any vertical plane
P\subset\mathbb{H}
with nonzero slope, let
\lambda_{P}\colon\mathbb{H}\to\mathbb{R}
be the affine function such that
\Gamma_{\lambda_{P}}=P
. For any bounded smooth function
\phi\colon\mathbb{H}\to\mathbb{R}
which is constant on cosets of
\langle Y\rangle
, let
H_{P,\phi}\colon P\to\mathbb{R}
be the function

H_{P,\phi}(p):=G^{\prime}_{\lambda_{P},\phi}(0)(p)

for any
p\in P
. By Lemma
7.1
and Lemma
7.3
,
H_{P,\phi}(p)
exists and

H_{P,\phi}(p)=\lim_{\begin{subarray}{c}r\to 0\\
R\to\infty\end{subarray}}(G^{r,R}_{\lambda_{P},\phi})^{\prime}(0)(p)=\lim_{\begin{subarray}{c}r\to 0\\
R\to\infty\end{subarray}}\int_{pA_{r,R}}(\phi(q)-\phi(p))Y_{\mathsf{L}}\widehat{K}(p^{-1}\Pi_{P}(q))\,\mathrm{d}q;

recall that we denote this limit by

H_{P,\phi}(p)=\pv(p)\int_{pV_{0}}(\phi(q)-\phi(p))Y_{\mathsf{L}}\widehat{K}(p^{-1}\Pi_{P}(q))\,\mathrm{d}q.

(63)

The functions which are constant on cosets of
\langle Y\rangle
are naturally identified with functions on
P
, so we can view
\phi\mapsto H_{P,\phi}
as a singular integral operator acting on functions from
P
to
\mathbb{R}
.
It is translation-invariant in the sense that if
P_{0}
goes through
\mathbf{0}
,
v_{0}\in P_{0}
, and
\hat{\phi}(v)=\phi(v+v_{0})
for all
v\in P_{0}
, then

H_{P_{0},\hat{\phi}}(v)=H_{P_{0},\phi}(v+v_{0})

for all
v\in P_{0}
.

In this section, we will show that when
P
is tangent to
\Gamma_{f_{i}}
at
p
, then
H_{P,\nu_{i}}
approximates
G_{f_{i},\nu_{i}}^{\prime}(0)
in a neighborhood of
p
. We will use this to bound how quickly
G_{f_{i},\nu_{i}}^{\prime}(0)
can vary, and in the next section, we will use this approximation to bound the correlation between
G_{f_{i},\nu_{i}}^{\prime}(0)
and
G_{f_{j},\nu_{j}}^{\prime}(0)
when
i\neq j
.

After rescaling
f_{i}
and
\nu_{i}
as in Section
3.1
, it suffices to consider functions
\alpha
and
\gamma
that satisfy the conclusion of Lemma
3.12
, i.e., satisfy (
22
) and (
23
) for some
c>0
. Many of the constants in the following bounds will depend on the value of
c
, so we omit
c
from the subscripts for the rest of this section. We will prove the following lemmas.

Lemma 8.1

(
H
approximates
G^{\prime}
)
.

Let
\epsilon=\frac{1}{10}
. Let
\alpha
and
\gamma
satisfy Lemma
3.12
for some sufficiently large
\rho
. Let
p\in\Gamma_{\alpha}
and let
P
be the tangent plane to
\Gamma_{\alpha}
at
p
. For any
q\in P
such that
d_{\mathrm{Kor}}(p,q)\leq\rho^{\epsilon}
,

\left|G_{\alpha,\gamma}^{\prime}(0)(q)-H_{P,\gamma}(q)\right|\lesssim\rho^{-\epsilon}.

Furthermore, for any
0<r\leq 1\leq R
,

\left|H_{P,\gamma}(q)-(G_{\lambda_{P},\gamma}^{r,R})^{\prime}(0)(q)\right|\lesssim A^{-1}(R^{-1}+r).

(64)

Lemma 8.2

(Hölder bounds on
G^{\prime}_{\alpha,\gamma}
)
.

Let
\epsilon=\frac{1}{10}
. For all
p,q\in\Gamma_{\alpha}
,

\left|G_{\alpha,\gamma}^{\prime}(0)(p)-G_{\alpha,\gamma}^{\prime}(0)(q)\right|\lesssim d_{\mathrm{Kor}}(p,q)^{\epsilon}+\rho^{-\frac{1}{2}}.

(65)

We apply Lemma
8.2
to
G_{f_{i},\nu_{i}}^{\prime}
by rescaling.

Corollary 8.3

(Hölder bounds on
G_{f_{i},\nu_{i}}^{\prime}
)
.

Let
\epsilon>0
be as above. Let
i\geq 0
, let
r_{i}=A^{-1}\rho^{-i}
, and let
p,q\in\Gamma_{f_{i}}
. Then

\left|G_{f_{i},\nu_{i}}^{\prime}(0)(p)-G_{f_{i},\nu_{i}}^{\prime}(0)(q)\right|\lesssim(r_{i}^{-1}d_{\mathrm{Kor}}(p,q))^{\epsilon}+\rho^{-\frac{1}{2}}.

(66)

Proof.

Let
g=G_{f_{i},\nu_{i}}^{\prime}(0)
.
Let
s_{i}=s^{-1}_{r_{i}}
. Let
\alpha(p)=r_{i}^{-1}f_{i}(s_{i}^{-1}(p))
and
\gamma(p)=r_{i}^{-1}\nu_{i}(s_{i}^{-1}(p))
. These satisfy Lemma
3.12
and
g(p)=G_{\alpha,\gamma}^{\prime}(0)(s_{i}(p))
. If
p,q\in\Gamma_{f_{i}}
, then
s_{i}(p),s_{i}(q)\in\Gamma_{\alpha}
, so, by Lemma
8.2
,

\left|g(p)-g(q)\right|=\left|G_{\alpha,\gamma}^{\prime}(0)(s_{i}(p))-G_{\alpha,\gamma}^{\prime}(0)(s_{i}(q))\right|\lesssim(r_{i}^{-1}d_{\mathrm{Kor}}(p,q))^{\epsilon}+\rho^{-\frac{1}{2}}.

∎

The proofs of Lemmas
8.1
and
8.2
are based on the following bounds.

Lemma 8.4
.

Let
\alpha
and
\gamma
satisfy Lemma
3.12
. Let
C>0
and let
P
be a vertical plane with
|\slope(P)|\leq C
. Let
W=X+\slope(P)Y
.
Let
\nabla_{P}=\nabla_{\lambda_{P}}
. Let
i,j\geq 0
and
i+j\leq 3
. Then for any
p\in P
,

|W^{i}Z^{j}\gamma(p)|=|\nabla_{P}^{i}Z^{j}\gamma(p)|\lesssim_{C}A^{-1}(1+d_{\mathrm{Kor}}(p,\Gamma_{\alpha}))^{i}.

(67)

Lemma 8.5
.

Let
\alpha
and
\gamma
satisfy Lemma
3.12
. Let
p\in\Gamma_{\alpha}
and let
P
be the tangent plane to
\Gamma_{\alpha}
at
p
. When
\rho
is sufficiently large,

G_{\alpha,\gamma}^{\prime}(0)(p)=H_{P,\gamma}(p)+O(\rho^{-\frac{1}{2}}).

Lemma 8.6
.

Let
\alpha
and
\gamma
satisfy Lemma
3.12
. Let
p\in\Gamma_{\alpha}
and let
C>0
. Then for any two planes
P
and
Q
through
p
with slopes at most
C
,

\displaystyle|H_{P,\gamma}(p)-H_{Q,\gamma}(p)|\lesssim_{C}|\slope P-\slope Q|

Lemma 8.7
.

Let
\alpha
and
\gamma
satisfy Lemma
3.12
. Let
p\in\Gamma_{\alpha}
and
q\in\mathbb{H}
. Let
P
be a plane through
\mathbf{0}
with
|\slope P|\leq 1
, and suppose that
d_{\mathrm{Kor}}(p,q)\leq 1
. Then

|H_{pP,\gamma}(p)-H_{qP,\gamma}(q)|\lesssim d_{\mathrm{Kor}}(p,q)^{\frac{1}{5}}.

Given these lemmas, we prove Lemmas
8.1
and
8.2
as follows.

Proof of Lemma
8.1
.

Let
p\in\Gamma_{\alpha}
and let
P_{p}
be the tangent plane to
\Gamma_{\alpha}
at
p
. Let
\lambda
be the affine function such that
\Gamma_{\lambda}=P_{p}
, and let
q\in P_{p}
be such that
d_{\mathrm{Kor}}(p,q)\leq\rho^{\epsilon}
.

Let
\kappa=d_{\mathrm{Kor}}(p,q)
. By Lemma
2.9
and Lemma
3.12
,
\overline{q}=qY^{t}
, where
t=\alpha(q)-\lambda(q)=O(\rho^{-1}\kappa^{2})
. We choose
\rho
large enough that
|t|<1
. Let
P_{\overline{q}}
be the tangent plane to
\Gamma_{\alpha}
at
\overline{q}
and let
Q=Y^{-t}P_{\overline{q}}
be the plane through
q
parallel to
P_{\overline{q}}
.
Then by the triangle inequality,

\big|G_{\alpha,\gamma}^{\prime}(0)(q)-H_{P_{p},\gamma}(q)\big|\leq\big|G_{\alpha,\gamma}^{\prime}(0)(q)-H_{P_{\overline{q}},\gamma}(\overline{q})\big|\\
+\big|H_{P_{\overline{q}},\gamma}(\overline{q})-H_{Q,\gamma}(q)\big|+\big|H_{Q,\gamma}(q)-H_{P_{p},\gamma}(q)\big|.

By Lemma
8.5
,

\big|G_{\alpha,\gamma}^{\prime}(0)(q)-H_{P_{\overline{q}},\gamma}(\overline{q})\big|\lesssim\rho^{-\frac{1}{2}}.

Since
P_{\overline{q}}
and
Q
are parallel and
d_{\mathrm{Kor}}(q,\overline{q})\leq t<1
, Lemma
8.7
implies that

\big|H_{P_{\overline{q}},\gamma}(\overline{q})-H_{Q,\gamma}(q)\big|\lesssim d_{\mathrm{Kor}}(q,\overline{q})^{\frac{1}{5}}\lesssim(\rho^{-1}\kappa^{2})^{\frac{1}{5}}.

Finally, by Lemma
8.6
and Lemma
2.9
,

\big|H_{Q,\gamma}(q)-H_{P_{p},\gamma}(q)\big|\lesssim|\nabla_{\alpha}\alpha(q)-\nabla_{\alpha}\alpha(p)|\lesssim\kappa\rho^{-1}+\kappa^{2}\rho^{-1}.

Since
\kappa\leq\rho^{\frac{1}{10}}
, these bounds imply that

\big|G_{\alpha,\gamma}^{\prime}(0)(q)-H_{P_{p},\gamma}(q)\big|\lesssim\rho^{-\frac{1}{2}}+(\rho^{-1}\kappa^{2})^{\frac{1}{5}}+\kappa\rho^{-1}+\kappa^{2}\rho^{-1}\lesssim\rho^{-\frac{1}{10}},

as desired.

To prove (
64
), we apply Lemma
7.1
with
\zeta=\lambda
,
\psi=\gamma
, and
r=1
. Let
q\in P_{p}
such that
\kappa=d_{\mathrm{Kor}}(p,q)\leq\rho^{\epsilon}
as above. Note that

d_{\mathrm{Kor}}(q,\Gamma_{\alpha})\leq d_{\mathrm{Kor}}(q,\overline{q})\lesssim\rho^{-1}\kappa^{2}\lesssim 1.

Since
|\slope(P_{p})|\leq 1
, we take
B=B(q,3)
. Since
\lambda
is affine,
\|\lambda\|_{W^{\prime}_{\lambda}(B)}=0
. For any
v\in B
, we have
d_{\mathrm{Kor}}(v,\Gamma_{\alpha})\lesssim 3+d_{\mathrm{Kor}}(q,\Gamma_{\alpha})\lesssim 1
, so Lemma
8.4
implies that
\|\gamma\|_{W_{\lambda}(B)}\lesssim A^{-1}
. By Lemma
7.1
and Lemma
7.3
,

\big|H_{P_{p},\gamma}(q)-(G^{r,R}_{\lambda,\gamma})^{\prime}(0)(q)\big|\lesssim\|\gamma\|_{W_{\lambda}(B)}r+\|\gamma\|_{\infty}R^{-1}\lesssim A^{-1}(r+R^{-1}),

as desired.
∎

Proof of Lemma
8.2
.

We claim that there is an
\epsilon>0
such that for all
p,q\in\Gamma_{\alpha}
,

\big|G_{\alpha,\gamma}^{\prime}(0)(p)-G_{\alpha,\gamma}^{\prime}(0)(q)\big|\lesssim d_{\mathrm{Kor}}(p,q)^{\epsilon}+\rho^{-\frac{1}{2}}.

Let
r=d_{\mathrm{Kor}}(p,q)
. By Proposition
6.1
,
\|G_{\alpha,\gamma}^{\prime}(0)\|_{\infty}\lesssim 1
, so it suffices to consider the case that
r\leq 1
.

By Lemma
8.5
, we have

\big|G_{\alpha,\gamma}^{\prime}(0)(p)-G_{\alpha,\gamma}^{\prime}(0)(q)\big|\lesssim\big|H_{P_{p},\gamma}(0)(p)-H_{P_{q},\gamma}(0)(q)\big|+\rho^{-\frac{1}{2}}.

(68)

Let
Q
be the plane parallel to
P_{p}
that goes through
q
. Lemma
8.6
and Lemma
8.7
imply that there is an
\epsilon>0
such that

\displaystyle\big|H_{P_{p},\gamma}(0)(p)-H_{P_{q},\gamma}(0)(q)\big|

\displaystyle\leq\big|H_{P_{p},\gamma}(0)(p)-H_{Q,\gamma}(0)(q)\big|+\big|H_{Q,\gamma}(0)(q)-H_{P_{q},\gamma}(0)(q)\big|

\displaystyle\lesssim r^{\epsilon}+|\slope Q-\slope P_{q}|

\displaystyle=r^{\epsilon}+|\nabla_{\alpha}\alpha(p)-\nabla_{\alpha}\alpha(q)|.

(69)

By Lemma
2.9
with
a=\alpha
and
m=\nabla_{\alpha}
and by Lemma
3.12
,

|\nabla_{\alpha}\alpha(p)-\nabla_{\alpha}\alpha(q)|\lesssim r\|\nabla^{2}_{\alpha}\alpha\|_{\infty}+r^{2}\|\partial_{z}\nabla_{\alpha}\alpha\|_{\infty}\lesssim r\rho^{-1}\lesssim r.

Combining this with (
68
) and (
69
), we see that

\big|G_{\alpha,\gamma}^{\prime}(0)(p)-G_{\alpha,\gamma}^{\prime}(0)(q)\big|\lesssim r^{\epsilon}+r+\rho^{-\frac{1}{2}}\lesssim r^{\epsilon}+\rho^{-\frac{1}{2}},

as desired.
∎

8.1.
Proofs of Lemmas
8.4
–
8.7

Now we prove the lemmas that we used in the proofs of Lemmas
8.1
and
8.2
. First, we prove Lemma
8.4
, which bounds derivatives of
\gamma
near
\Gamma_{\alpha}
.

Proof of Lemma
8.4
.

Recall that
\Pi_{P}=\Pi_{\lambda_{P}}
is the projection to
P
and that
\nabla_{P}(v)=\nabla_{\lambda_{P}}(v)=X(v)+(y(v)-\lambda_{P}(v))Z(v)
. Since
\nabla_{P}
is constant on vertical lines, we have
[\nabla_{P},Z]=0
.

Since
W=X+\slope(P)Y
is horizontal, for any
u\in P
, the curve
g(w)=uW^{w}
is a horizontal curve in
P
, so its projection
\Pi\circ g
is an integral curve of
\nabla_{P}
. For any function
a
which is constant on cosets of
\langle Y\rangle
,

W^{i}a(u)=(a\circ g)^{(i)}(0)=(a\circ\Pi\circ g)^{(i)}(0)=\nabla_{P}^{i}a(\Pi(u))=\nabla_{P}^{i}a(u).

Therefore, for any
i
and
j
and any
p\in P
,

W^{i}Z^{j}[\gamma](p)=\nabla_{P}^{i}Z^{j}[\gamma](p).

This proves the first equality in (
67
).

We claim that for any
p\in\mathbb{H}
and any
i\geq 0
,
j\geq 0
with
i+j\leq 3
, we have

|\nabla_{P}^{i}Z^{j}\gamma(p)|\lesssim(1+|\lambda_{P}(p)-\alpha(p)|)^{i}.

(70)

Since
\alpha
is intrinsic Lipschitz,
|\lambda_{P}(p)-\alpha(p)|\lesssim d_{\mathrm{Kor}}(p,\Gamma_{\alpha})
for all
p\in P
, so this will imply the lemma.

Let
h\colon\mathbb{H}\to\mathbb{R}
be a smooth function that is constant on cosets of
\langle Y\rangle
. Let
\Lambda(v)=1+|\lambda_{P}(v)-\alpha(v)|
. For
c>0
,
d\geq 0
and
n\geq 0
, we say that
h
has
(c,d,n)
–derivative growth
if for any word
E\in\{Z,\nabla_{\alpha}\}^{*}
of length at most
d
and any
q\in\mathbb{H}
, we have

|Eh(q)|\leq cA^{-1}\Lambda(q)^{n}.

In particular,
|h(q)|\lesssim A^{-1}\Lambda(q)^{n}
. We claim that
\nabla_{P}^{j}Z^{i}\gamma
has
(c_{j},3-i-j,j)
–derivative growth when
i+j\leq 3
. This will imply (
70
).

When
j=0
, Lemma
3.12
implies that
\gamma
has
(c_{0},3,0)
–derivative growth and
Z^{i}\gamma
has
(c_{0},3-i,0)
–derivative growth for some
c_{0}\lesssim A^{-1}
.

We thus proceed by induction. Suppose that
h
has
(c_{n},d,n)
–derivative growth for some
d\leq 3
and consider
\nabla_{P}h
. Note that

\nabla_{P}h=\nabla_{\alpha}h+(\alpha-\lambda_{P})Zh.

For any
0\leq l\leq d-1
, any
E=E_{1}\dots E_{l}\in\{Z,\nabla_{\alpha}\}^{*}
, and any
q\in\mathbb{H}
,

|E\nabla_{P}h(q)|\leq|E\nabla_{\alpha}h(q)|+|E[(\alpha-\lambda_{P})Zh](q)|\\
\leq c_{n}\Lambda(q)^{n}+\sum_{S\subset\{1,\dots,l\}}|E_{S}[\alpha-\lambda_{P}](q)\cdot E_{S^{c}}Zh(q)|,

where
E_{S}=\prod_{i\in S}E_{i}
. By Lemma
3.12
,
|E_{S}[\alpha-\lambda_{P}](v)|\lesssim_{C}1
unless
S=\emptyset
and
E_{S}=\id
. Furthermore,
E_{S^{c}}Z
is a word of length at most
d
, so
|E_{S^{c}}Zh(q)|\leq c_{n}\Lambda(q)^{n}
. Therefore,

|E\nabla_{P}h(q)|\lesssim_{C}c_{n}\Lambda(q)^{n}+2^{l}c_{n}\Lambda(q)^{n}+|\lambda_{P}(q)-\alpha(q)|\cdot c_{n}\Lambda(q)^{n}\lesssim_{C,d}c_{n}\Lambda(q)^{n+1}.

That is,
\nabla_{P}h
has
(c_{n+1},d-1,n+1)
–derivative growth for some
c_{n+1}\lesssim_{C,d}c_{n}
.

For
0\leq i\leq 3
,
Z^{i}\gamma
has
(c_{0},3-i,0)
–derivative growth for some
c_{0}\lesssim A^{-1}
, so for
0\leq j\leq 3-i
, there are
c_{j}\lesssim_{C}A^{-1}
such that
\nabla_{P}^{j}Z^{i}\gamma
has
(c_{j},3-i-j,j)
–derivative growth. In particular, for all
p\in P
,

\big|\nabla_{P}^{j}Z^{i}\gamma(p)\big|\leq c_{j}\Lambda(p)^{j}\lesssim_{C}A^{-1}(1+|\lambda_{P}(p)-\alpha(p)|)^{j},

as desired.
∎

Proof of Lemma
8.5
.

Let
p\in\Gamma_{\alpha}
and let
P
be the vertical tangent plane to
\Gamma_{\alpha}
at
p
.
We claim that

G_{\alpha,\gamma}^{\prime}(0)(p)=\frac{\,\mathrm{d}}{\,\mathrm{d}t}F_{\alpha+t\gamma}(p)\big|_{t=0}=H_{P,\gamma}(p)+O(\rho^{-\frac{1}{2}}).

After translating, we may suppose that
p=\mathbf{0}
. Let
\gamma_{0}(w)=\gamma(w)-\gamma(\mathbf{0})
for all
w\in\mathbb{H}
. By Proposition
6.1
and (
63
), we can write

\left|G_{\alpha,\gamma}^{\prime}(0)(\mathbf{0})-H_{P,\gamma}(\mathbf{0})\right|\leq\limsup_{r\to 0}\left|\left(G^{r,\sqrt{\rho}}_{\alpha,\gamma}\right)^{\prime}(0)(\mathbf{0})-\int_{A_{r,\sqrt{\rho}}}\gamma_{0}(w)Y_{\mathsf{L}}\widehat{K}(\Pi_{P}(w))\,\mathrm{d}w\right|\\
+\limsup_{R\to\infty}\left|\left(G^{\sqrt{\rho},R}_{\alpha,\gamma}\right)^{\prime}(0)(\mathbf{0})-\int_{A_{\sqrt{\rho},R}}\gamma_{0}(w)Y_{\mathsf{L}}\widehat{K}(\Pi_{P}(w))\,\mathrm{d}w\right|.

(71)

We start by bounding the
R\to\infty
term.
Since
P
has bounded slope, we have
\|\Pi_{P}(w)\|_{\mathrm{Kor}}\approx\|w\|_{\mathrm{Kor}}
.
By the homogeneity of
Y_{\mathsf{L}}\widehat{K}
and the boundedness of
\gamma
,

\left|\int_{A_{\sqrt{\rho},R}}\gamma_{0}(w)Y_{\mathsf{L}}\widehat{K}(\Pi_{P}(w))\,\mathrm{d}w\right|\lesssim\left|\int_{A_{\sqrt{\rho},R}}\|w\|_{\mathrm{Kor}}^{-4}\,\mathrm{d}w\right|\approx\int_{\sqrt{\rho}}^{\infty}\kappa^{-4}\kappa^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\kappa=\rho^{-\frac{1}{2}},

using Lemma
5.3
to change variables from
w
to
\kappa
.
By Lemma
7.2
, for all
R>\sqrt{\rho}
,

\left|(G^{\sqrt{\rho},R}_{\alpha,\gamma})^{\prime}(0)(\mathbf{0})-\int_{A_{\sqrt{\rho},R}}\gamma_{0}(w)Y_{\mathsf{L}}\widehat{K}(\Pi_{P}(w))\,\mathrm{d}w\right|\lesssim\rho^{-\frac{1}{2}}+\rho^{-\frac{1}{2}}\approx\rho^{-\frac{1}{2}}.

(72)

Now we consider the
r\to 0
term.
By (
53
), letting
w=(x,0,z)
, for any
0<r<\sqrt{\rho}
,

(G^{r,\sqrt{\rho}}_{\alpha,\gamma})^{\prime}(0)(\mathbf{0})-\int_{A_{r,\sqrt{\rho}}}\gamma_{0}(w)Y_{\mathsf{L}}\widehat{K}(\Pi_{P}(w))\,\mathrm{d}w\\
=\int_{A_{r,\sqrt{\rho}}}\gamma_{0}(w)\left(Y_{\mathsf{L}}\widehat{K}(\Psi_{\alpha}(w))-Y_{\mathsf{L}}\widehat{K}(\Pi_{P}(w))\right)-\gamma(\mathbf{0})x\partial_{z}\alpha(w)Y_{\mathsf{L}}\widehat{K}(\Psi_{\alpha}(w))\,\mathrm{d}w,

(73)

and we will bound the terms in the integrand separately.

We start with the first term in (
73
). Let
w\in V_{0}
and let
\kappa=\|w\|_{\mathrm{Kor}}
.
Let
\lambda=\lambda_{P}
, so that
\Gamma_{\lambda}=P
.
By Lemma
2.9
,

\displaystyle|\alpha(w)-\lambda(w)|\lesssim\rho^{-1}\kappa^{2}

(74)

By the Mean Value Theorem, there is some
t
between
\lambda_{P}(w)
and
\alpha(w)
such that

\displaystyle\left|Y_{\mathsf{L}}\widehat{K}(wY^{\alpha(w)})-Y_{\mathsf{L}}\widehat{K}(wY^{\lambda_{P}(w)})\right|=|\alpha(w)-\lambda_{P}(w)|\cdot|Y_{\mathsf{L}}^{2}\widehat{K}(wY^{t})|.

Furthermore,
\|wY^{t}\|_{\mathrm{Kor}}\approx\|w\|_{\mathrm{Kor}}
, so by the
(-5)
–homogeneity of
Y_{\mathsf{L}}^{2}\widehat{K}
,

\left|Y_{\mathsf{L}}\widehat{K}(wY^{\alpha(w)})-Y_{\mathsf{L}}\widehat{K}(wY^{\lambda_{P}(w)})\right|\overset{\eqref{e:small-bound}}{\lesssim}\rho^{-1}\kappa^{2}\cdot\kappa^{-5}=\rho^{-1}\kappa^{-3}.

(75)

We apply Lemma
2.9
to
\gamma_{0}
to get

|\gamma_{0}(w)|\lesssim\kappa\|\nabla_{\alpha}\gamma_{0}\|_{\infty}+\kappa^{2}\|\partial_{z}\gamma_{0}\|_{\infty}\lesssim\kappa+\kappa^{2}

by Lemma
3.12
. Since
\|\gamma_{0}\|_{\infty}\lesssim 1
, we have
|\gamma_{0}(w)|\lesssim\min\{\kappa+\kappa^{2},1\}\lesssim\kappa
.
Therefore,

\left|\int_{A_{r,\sqrt{\rho}}}\gamma_{0}(w)(Y_{\mathsf{L}}\widehat{K}(\Psi_{\alpha}(w))-Y_{\mathsf{L}}\widehat{K}(\Pi_{P}(w)))\,\mathrm{d}w\right|\\
\lesssim\int_{A_{r,\sqrt{\rho}}}\|w\|_{\mathrm{Kor}}\cdot\rho^{-1}\|w\|_{\mathrm{Kor}}^{-3}\,\mathrm{d}w\lesssim\int_{r}^{\sqrt{\rho}}\kappa^{-2}\rho^{-1}\kappa^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\kappa\leq\rho^{-\frac{1}{2}},

(76)

using Lemma
5.3
in the penultimate inequality.

It remains to bound the second term in (
73
).
We write

\displaystyle\int_{A_{r,\sqrt{\rho}}}x\partial_{z}\alpha(w)Y_{\mathsf{L}}\widehat{K}(\Psi_{\alpha}(w))\,\mathrm{d}w

\displaystyle=\int_{A_{r,\sqrt{\rho}}}x(\partial_{z}\alpha(w)-\partial_{z}\alpha(\mathbf{0}))Y_{\mathsf{L}}\widehat{K}(\Psi_{\alpha}(w))\,\mathrm{d}w

\displaystyle\qquad+\int_{A_{r,\sqrt{\rho}}}x\partial_{z}\alpha(\mathbf{0})(Y_{\mathsf{L}}\widehat{K}(\Psi_{\alpha}(w))-Y_{\mathsf{L}}\widehat{K}(\Pi_{P}(w)))\,\mathrm{d}w

\displaystyle\qquad+\int_{A_{r,\sqrt{\rho}}}x\partial_{z}\alpha(\mathbf{0})\cdot Y_{\mathsf{L}}\widehat{K}(\Pi_{P}(w))\,\mathrm{d}w

\displaystyle=:I_{1}+I_{2}+I_{3}.

To bound
I_{1}
, let
m=\partial_{z}\alpha
. By Lemma
3.12
, we have
\|m\|_{\infty}\lesssim\rho^{-1}
,
\|\nabla_{\alpha}m\|_{\infty}\lesssim\rho^{-1}
, and
\|\partial_{z}m\|_{\infty}\lesssim\rho^{-1}
, so by Lemma
2.9
, letting
\kappa=\|w\|_{\mathrm{Kor}}
as above,

\displaystyle|m(w)-m(\mathbf{0})|\lesssim\min\{\rho^{-1},\rho^{-1}(\kappa+\kappa^{2})\}\lesssim\rho^{-1}\kappa.

Therefore, using Lemma
5.3
,

|I_{1}|\lesssim\int_{A_{r,\sqrt{\rho}}}\left|x\rho^{-1}\|w\|_{\mathrm{Kor}}Y_{\mathsf{L}}\widehat{K}(\Psi_{\alpha}(w))\right|\,\mathrm{d}w\\
\lesssim\rho^{-1}\int_{A_{r,\sqrt{\rho}}}\|w\|_{\mathrm{Kor}}^{-2}\,\mathrm{d}w\lesssim\rho^{-1}\int_{r}^{\sqrt{\rho}}\kappa^{-2}\kappa^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\kappa\leq\rho^{-\frac{1}{2}}.

By (
75
)

|I_{2}|\lesssim\int_{A_{r,\sqrt{\rho}}}\left|x\partial_{z}\alpha(\mathbf{0})\rho^{-1}\|w\|_{\mathrm{Kor}}^{-3}\right|\lesssim\int_{A_{r,\sqrt{\rho}}}\rho^{-2}\|w\|_{\mathrm{Kor}}^{-2}\,\mathrm{d}w\lesssim\rho^{-2}\int_{r}^{\sqrt{\rho}}\,\mathrm{d}\kappa\leq\rho^{-\frac{3}{2}}.

Finally, recall that
\theta(x,y,z)=(-x,-y,z)
and let
h(w)=x(w)\partial_{z}\alpha(\mathbf{0})\cdot Y_{\mathsf{L}}\widehat{K}(\Pi_{P}(w))
. The symmetry of
Y_{\mathsf{L}}\widehat{K}
implies that

h(\theta(w))=x(\theta(w))\partial_{z}\alpha(\mathbf{0})\cdot Y_{\mathsf{L}}\widehat{K}(\Pi_{P}(\theta(w)))=-x(w)\partial_{z}\alpha(\mathbf{0})\cdot Y_{\mathsf{L}}\widehat{K}(\Pi_{P}(w))=-h(w).

Since
\theta(A_{r,\sqrt{\rho}})=A_{r,\sqrt{\rho}}
,

I_{3}=\int_{A_{r,\sqrt{\rho}}}x\partial_{z}\alpha(\mathbf{0})Y_{\mathsf{L}}\widehat{K}(\Pi_{P}(w))\,\mathrm{d}w=0,

and

\int_{A_{r,\sqrt{\rho}}}x\partial_{z}\alpha(w)Y_{\mathsf{L}}\widehat{K}(\Psi_{\alpha}(w))\,\mathrm{d}w=I_{1}+I_{2}+I_{3}=O(\rho^{-\frac{1}{2}}).

(77)

Combining these inequalities, we find that

\left|G_{\alpha,\gamma}^{\prime}(0)(\mathbf{0})-H_{P,\gamma}(\mathbf{0})\right|\lesssim\rho^{-\frac{1}{2}},

as desired.
∎

Proof of Lemma
8.6
.

Without loss of generality, we may suppose that
p=\mathbf{0}
. We claim that

\left|\pv\int_{V_{0}}(\gamma(q)-\gamma(\mathbf{0}))\left(Y_{\mathsf{L}}\widehat{K}(\Pi_{P}(q))-Y_{\mathsf{L}}\widehat{K}(\Pi_{Q}(q))\right)\,\mathrm{d}q\right|\lesssim_{C}|\slope P-\slope Q|.

Let
M(q)=Y_{\mathsf{L}}\widehat{K}(\Pi_{P}(q))-Y_{\mathsf{L}}\widehat{K}(\Pi_{Q}(q))
.
By the smoothness and homogeneity of
Y_{\mathsf{L}}\widehat{K}
, for all
q\in V_{0}
, we have

|M(q)|\lesssim_{C}|\slope P-\slope Q|\|q\|_{\mathrm{Kor}}^{-4}

and
M(\theta(q))=M(q)
. Let
\gamma_{0}(q)=\gamma(q)-\gamma(\mathbf{0})
, and let
