\gamma_{0}^{\mathsf{e}}(q)=\frac{1}{2}(\gamma_{0}(q)+\gamma_{0}(\theta(q)))
so that

\int_{A_{r,R}}(\gamma(q)-\gamma(\mathbf{0}))M(q)\,\mathrm{d}q=\int_{A_{r,R}}\gamma_{0}^{\mathsf{e}}(q)M(q)\,\mathrm{d}q.

On one hand, since
\|\gamma\|_{\infty}\lesssim 1
, Lemma
5.3
implies that for any
R>1
,

\left|\int_{A_{1,R}}\gamma_{0}^{\mathsf{e}}(q)M(q)\,\mathrm{d}q\right|\lesssim|\slope P-\slope Q|\|\gamma\|_{\infty}\int_{1}^{R}\kappa^{-4}\kappa^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\kappa\lesssim|\slope P-\slope Q|.

On the other hand, by Lemma
3.12
and Lemma
2.9
,

\gamma_{0}(q)=x(q)\nabla_{\alpha}\gamma(q)+O(\|q\|_{\mathrm{Kor}}^{2}),

so
|\gamma_{0}^{\mathsf{e}}(q)|\lesssim\|q\|_{\mathrm{Kor}}^{2}
. Therefore, for any
r<1
,

\left|\int_{A_{r,1}}\gamma_{0}^{\mathsf{e}}(q)M(q)\,\mathrm{d}q\right|\lesssim|\slope P-\slope Q|\int_{r}^{1}\kappa^{-2}\kappa^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\kappa\lesssim|\slope P-\slope Q|.

Combining these two inequalities, we have

\left|\pv\int_{V_{0}}\gamma_{0}^{\mathsf{e}}(q)M(q)\,\mathrm{d}q\right|\leq\lim_{r\to 0}\left|\int_{A_{r,1}}\gamma_{0}^{\mathsf{e}}(q)M(q)\,\mathrm{d}q\right|+\lim_{R\to\infty}\left|\int_{A_{1,R}}\gamma_{0}^{\mathsf{e}}(q)M(q)\,\mathrm{d}q\right|\\
\lesssim_{C}|\slope P-\slope Q|.

∎

Finally, we prove Lemma
8.7
. We will need the following bound.

Lemma 8.8
.

Let
C>0
, let
P
be a plane through the origin with
|\slope(P)|\leq C
and let
\psi\colon\mathbb{H}\to\mathbb{R}
be a bounded smooth function such that
\psi(\mathbf{0})=0
and
\psi
is constant on cosets of
\langle Y\rangle
. Let
r>0
, and let

\epsilon=\|\partial_{z}\psi\|_{L_{\infty}(B(\mathbf{0},r))}+\|X^{2}\psi\|_{L_{\infty}(V_{0}\cap B(\mathbf{0},r))}.

Then

\displaystyle\left|\pv\int_{V_{0}}\psi(v)Y_{\mathsf{L}}\widehat{K}(\Pi_{P}(v))\,\mathrm{d}v\right|\lesssim_{C}\epsilon r+\|\psi\|_{\infty}r^{-1}.

Proof.

Let
v\in V_{0}\cap B(\mathbf{0},r)
and let
\kappa=\|v\|_{\mathrm{Kor}}
.
By Taylor approximation, there is a
c>0
such that

\displaystyle|\psi(v)-X\psi(\mathbf{0})x(v)|\leq c\epsilon\kappa^{2}.

Let
D=V_{0}\cap B(\mathbf{0},r)
. Then

\displaystyle\left|\pv\int_{D}\psi(v)Y_{\mathsf{L}}\widehat{K}(\Pi_{P}(v))\,\mathrm{d}v\right|

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\left|\pv\int_{D}X\psi(\mathbf{0})x(v)Y_{\mathsf{L}}\widehat{K}(\Pi_{P}(v))\,\mathrm{d}v\right|+\pv\int_{D}\left|c\epsilon\|v\|_{\mathrm{Kor}}^{2}Y_{\mathsf{L}}\widehat{K}(\Pi_{P}(v))\right|\,\mathrm{d}v.

The first term is
0
by symmetry, and since
\|v\|_{\mathrm{Kor}}^{2}Y_{\mathsf{L}}\widehat{K}(v)
is
(-2)
–homogeneous, Lemma
5.3
implies that

\left|\pv\int_{D}\psi(v)Y_{\mathsf{L}}\widehat{K}(\Pi_{P}(v))\,\mathrm{d}v\right|\lesssim_{C}c\epsilon\int_{0}^{r}\kappa^{-2}\kappa^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\kappa\lesssim\epsilon r.

Additionally, by the homogeneity of
Y_{\mathsf{R}}K
and Lemma
5.3
,

\displaystyle\left|\pv\int_{V_{0}\setminus D}\psi(v)Y_{\mathsf{L}}\widehat{K}(\Pi_{P}(v))\,\mathrm{d}v\right|\lesssim\int_{r}^{\infty}\|\psi\|_{\infty}\kappa^{-4}\kappa^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\kappa\lesssim\|\psi\|_{\infty}r^{-1}.

Summing these two inequalities proves the lemma.
∎

We now prove Lemma
8.7
.

Proof of Lemma
8.7
.

Recall that
p\in\Gamma_{\alpha}
,
q\in\mathbb{H}
, and
d_{\mathrm{Kor}}(p,q)\leq 1
. After a translation, we may suppose that
p=\mathbf{0}
.
Then for any
h\in\mathbb{H}
,

\displaystyle H_{hP,\gamma}(h)

\displaystyle=\pv(\mathbf{0})\int_{V_{0}}(\gamma(h)-\gamma(hv))Y_{\mathsf{L}}\widehat{K}(\Pi_{P}(v))\,\mathrm{d}\mu,

so, letting

\nu(v)=\gamma(\mathbf{0})-\gamma(v)-\gamma(q)+\gamma(qv),

\displaystyle H_{P,\gamma}(\mathbf{0})-H_{qP,\gamma}(q)

\displaystyle=\pv(\mathbf{0})\int_{V_{0}}\nu(v)Y_{\mathsf{L}}\widehat{K}(\Pi_{P}(v))\,\mathrm{d}\mu.

Then
\|\nu\|_{\infty}\leq 4\|\gamma\|_{\infty}\lesssim 1
.
We abbreviate partial derivatives of
\nu(x,y,z)
and
\gamma(x,y,z)
as
\gamma_{x}=\partial_{x}\gamma
,
\gamma_{xz}=\partial_{x}\partial_{z}\gamma
, etc.

Let
\kappa=d_{\mathrm{Kor}}(\mathbf{0},q)\leq 1
and let
r=\kappa^{-\frac{1}{5}}
. We claim that

|\nu_{z}(v)|\lesssim\kappa^{\frac{4}{5}}
and
|\nu_{xx}(v)|\lesssim\kappa^{\frac{2}{5}}
for all
v\in V_{0}\cap B(\mathbf{0},r)
. This will let us apply Lemma
8.8
to
\nu
.

We write
q=X^{x_{0}}Z^{z_{0}}Y^{t}
; note that
|x_{0}|\leq\kappa
,
|t|\leq\kappa
, and
|z_{0}|\leq\kappa^{2}
.
Since
\gamma
is constant on cosets of
\langle Y\rangle
,

\nu(x,0,z)=\nu(X^{x}Z^{z})=\gamma(\mathbf{0})-\gamma(X^{x}Z^{z})-\gamma(qY^{-t})+\gamma(qY^{-t}\cdot Y^{t}X^{x}Y^{-t}Z^{z})\\
=\gamma(0,0,0)-\gamma(x,0,z)-\gamma(x_{0},0,z_{0})+\gamma(x_{0}+x,0,z_{0}-tx+z).

Suppose that
v=(x,0,z)\in V_{0}\cap B(\mathbf{0},r)
. Then

\displaystyle\nu_{z}(v)

\displaystyle=\gamma_{z}(v^{\prime})-\gamma_{z}(v)

\displaystyle\nu_{xx}(v)

\displaystyle=\gamma_{xx}(v^{\prime})-2t\gamma_{xz}(v^{\prime})+t^{2}\gamma_{zz}(v^{\prime})-\gamma_{xx}(v),

where
v^{\prime}=(x_{0}+x,0,z_{0}-tx+z)
.

Note that
|x(v^{\prime})|\leq r+\kappa\lesssim r
and
|z(v^{\prime})|\leq\kappa^{2}+r^{2}+\kappa r\lesssim r^{2}
, so there is a
c>0
such that
v,v^{\prime}\in B(\mathbf{0},cr)
. Let
S=V_{0}\cap B(\mathbf{0},cr)
. By Lemma
8.4
, for
i+j\leq 3
,

\|\partial_{x}^{i}\partial_{z}^{j}\gamma\|_{L_{\infty}(S)}\lesssim 1+r^{i}.

(78)

In particular,
|t\gamma_{xz}(v^{\prime})|\lesssim\kappa(1+r)\lesssim\kappa^{\frac{4}{5}}
and
|t^{2}\gamma_{zz}(v^{\prime})|\lesssim\kappa^{2}
.

It remains to bound
|\gamma_{xx}(v^{\prime})-\gamma_{xx}(v)|
and
|\gamma_{z}(v^{\prime})-\gamma_{z}(v)|
. Since
v^{-1}v^{\prime}=(x_{0},0,z_{0}-tx)
, the Mean Value Theorem and (
78
) imply that

|\gamma_{xx}(v^{\prime})-\gamma_{xx}(v)|\leq|x_{0}|\|\gamma_{xxx}\|_{L_{\infty}(S)}+|z_{0}-tx|\|\gamma_{xxz}\|_{L_{\infty}(S)}\\
\lesssim\kappa(1+r^{3})+(\kappa^{2}+\kappa r)(1+r^{2})\lesssim\kappa r^{3}=\kappa^{\frac{2}{5}}.

Likewise,

|\gamma_{z}(v^{\prime})-\gamma_{z}(v)|\leq|x_{0}|\|\gamma_{xz}\|_{L_{\infty}(S)}+|z_{0}-tx|\|\gamma_{zz}\|_{L_{\infty}(S)}\\
\lesssim\kappa(1+r)+(\kappa^{2}+\kappa r)\lesssim\kappa r=\kappa^{\frac{4}{5}}.

Combining these inequalities, we obtain
|\nu_{z}(v)|\lesssim\kappa^{\frac{4}{5}}
and
|\nu_{xx}(v)|\lesssim\kappa^{\frac{2}{5}}
for all
v\in V_{0}\cap B(\mathbf{0},r)
. By Lemma
8.8
, this implies

|H_{P,\gamma}(\mathbf{0})-H_{qP,\gamma}(q)|=\left|\pv(\mathbf{0})\int_{P}\nu(v)Y_{\mathsf{L}}\widehat{K}(\Pi_{P}(v))\,\mathrm{d}\mu\right|\\
\lesssim_{C}r(\kappa^{\frac{4}{5}}+\kappa^{\frac{2}{5}})+r^{-1}\|\nu\|_{\infty}\lesssim\kappa^{\frac{1}{5}}=d_{\mathrm{Kor}}(\mathbf{0},q)^{\frac{1}{5}},

as desired.
∎

9.
Lower bounds on the first derivative

Now we use the approximations in the previous section to prove lower bounds on
G^{\prime}_{\alpha,\gamma}(0)
. Our main estimate is the following lemma, which shows that we can estimate
G^{\prime}_{\alpha,\gamma}(0)(p)
in terms of the restriction of
\gamma
to the vertical line
p\langle Z\rangle
.

Specifically, let
P
be the vertical tangent plane to
\Gamma_{\alpha}
at
p
, i.e.,
P=p\langle W,Z\rangle
, where
W=X+\nabla_{\alpha}\alpha(p)Y
. Let
\Pi_{P}\colon\mathbb{H}\to P
,
\Pi_{P}(pW^{w}Z^{z}Y^{y})=pW^{w}Z^{z}
be the projection to
P
along cosets of
Y
. Let
\pi_{p}\colon\mathbb{H}\to p\langle Z\rangle
,
\pi_{p}(pW^{w}Z^{z}Y^{y})=pZ^{z}
. This map is constant along cosets of
\langle Y\rangle
and projects
P
to
p\langle Z\rangle
along cosets of
\langle W\rangle
. We will show the following bound.

Lemma 9.1
.

Let
A>1
. When
\rho
is sufficiently large (depending only on
A
), the following holds.
Let
\alpha
and
\gamma
be functions satisfying Lemma
3.12
.
Let
p\in\Gamma_{\alpha}
and let
P
be the vertical tangent plane to
\Gamma_{\alpha}
at
p
. Let
\pi_{p}
be as above. Then

G^{\prime}_{\alpha,\gamma}(0)(p)=H_{P,\gamma\circ\pi_{p}}(p)+O(A^{-2})\\
=\pv(p)\int_{pV_{0}}(\gamma(\pi_{p}(q))-\gamma(p))Y_{\mathsf{L}}\widehat{K}(p^{-1}\Pi_{P}(q))\,\mathrm{d}q+O(A^{-2}).

(79)

Proof.

The second equality in (
79
) is (
63
), so it suffices to prove the first equality. After a left-translation, we may suppose that
p=\mathbf{0}
.

By Lemma
8.5
,

|G^{\prime}_{\alpha,\gamma}(0)(\mathbf{0})-H_{P,\gamma\circ\pi_{p}}(\mathbf{0})|\leq|G^{\prime}_{\alpha,\gamma}(0)(\mathbf{0})-H_{P,\gamma}(\mathbf{0})|+|H_{P,\gamma}(\mathbf{0})-H_{P,\gamma\circ\pi_{p}}(\mathbf{0})|\\
\lesssim\rho^{-\frac{1}{2}}+|H_{P,\gamma-\gamma\circ\pi_{p}}(\mathbf{0})|.

(80)

We thus consider
H_{P,\gamma-\gamma\circ\pi_{p}}(\mathbf{0})
.
Recall that by (
63
),

H_{P,\gamma-\gamma\circ\pi_{p}}(\mathbf{0})=\pv(\mathbf{0})\int_{V_{0}}(\gamma(q)-\gamma\circ\pi_{p}(q))Y_{\mathsf{L}}\widehat{K}(\Pi_{P}(q))\,\mathrm{d}q.

Since
\|\gamma\|_{\infty}\lesssim A^{-1}
, Lemma
5.3
implies that

\int_{V_{0}\setminus B(\mathbf{0},A)}|(\gamma(q)-\gamma\circ\pi_{p}(q))Y_{\mathsf{L}}\widehat{K}(\Pi_{P}(q))|\,\mathrm{d}q\lesssim\int_{A}^{\infty}A^{-1}\kappa^{-4}\kappa^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\kappa\lesssim A^{-2}.

(81)

Let
D=P\cap B(\mathbf{0},3A)
. We claim that if
v=W^{w}Z^{z}\in D
, then

\gamma(v)=\gamma(Z^{z})+w\cdot W\gamma(Z^{z})+O(A^{-3}w^{2}).

Let
\sigma=\nabla_{\alpha}\alpha(\mathbf{0})
and let
\lambda\colon\mathbb{H}\to\mathbb{R}
,
\lambda(x,y,z)=\sigma x
so that
\Gamma_{\lambda}=P
.
Recall that for all
q\in\mathbb{H}
, we have
(\nabla_{\alpha})_{q}=X_{q}+(y(q)-\alpha(q))Z_{q}
. If
v\in P
, then
y(v)=\lambda(v)
, so

W_{v}=(\nabla_{\alpha})_{v}-(\lambda(v)-\alpha(v))Z_{v}+\sigma Y_{v}.

Let
m\colon\mathbb{H}\to\mathbb{R}
be a smooth function which is constant on cosets of
\langle Y\rangle
. Then
Ym=0
, so for
v\in P
,

Wm(v)=Xm(v)=\nabla_{\alpha}m(v)-(\lambda(v)-\alpha(v))Zm(v)=(\nabla_{\alpha}-(\lambda-\alpha)Z)[m](v).

We can apply this identity to
\alpha
,
\gamma
, and their derivatives with respect to
\nabla_{\alpha}
and
Z
, which are all constant on cosets of
\langle Y\rangle
.

Note that by Lemma
3.12
and Lemma
2.9
,

\lim_{\rho\to\infty}\max\{\|\lambda-\alpha\|_{L_{\infty}(D)},\|\nabla_{\alpha}\alpha-\nabla_{\alpha}\alpha(\mathbf{0})\|_{L_{\infty}(D)},\|Z\alpha\|_{\infty}\}=0.

(82)

One consequence of (
82
) is that for all
v\in D
,

W\gamma(v)=\nabla_{\alpha}\gamma(v)+O\left(\|\lambda-\alpha\|_{L_{\infty}(D)}\|Z\gamma\|_{\infty}\right)\overset{\eqref{eq:misc-bounds}}{=}\nabla_{\alpha}\gamma(v)+o_{\rho}(1),

where
o_{\rho}(1)
is little–
o
notation denoting an error term bounded by a function of
\rho
that goes to zero as
\rho\to\infty
.

We can bound the second derivative similarly. Evaluating all functions at
v\in D
,

\displaystyle W^{2}\gamma

\displaystyle=W(\nabla_{\alpha}-(\lambda-\alpha)Z)[\gamma]=\nabla^{2}_{\alpha}\gamma-(\lambda-\alpha)Z\nabla_{\alpha}\gamma-W[\lambda-\alpha]\cdot Z\gamma-(\lambda-\alpha)WZ\gamma.

By Lemma
3.12
,
\|Z\nabla_{\alpha}\gamma\|_{\infty}\lesssim A^{-2}
, so by (
82
),
(\lambda-\alpha)Z\nabla_{\alpha}\gamma=o_{\rho}(1)
. Likewise,
\|Z\gamma\|_{\infty}\lesssim A^{-1}
. Since
\nabla_{\alpha}\lambda(v)=\nabla_{\alpha}\alpha(\mathbf{0}),

W[\lambda-\alpha](v)=\nabla_{\alpha}\alpha(\mathbf{0})-\nabla_{\alpha}\alpha(v)-(\lambda(v)-\alpha(v))\cdot Z\alpha\overset{\eqref{eq:misc-bounds}}{=}o_{\rho}(1).

Finally,

(\lambda(v)-\alpha(v))WZ\gamma(v)=(\lambda(v)-\alpha(v))\nabla_{\alpha}Z\gamma(v)-(\lambda(v)-\alpha(v))^{2}Z^{2}\gamma(v)\overset{\eqref{eq:misc-bounds}}{=}o_{\rho}(1),

so
W^{2}\gamma(v)=\nabla_{\alpha}^{2}\gamma(v)+o_{\rho}(1)
. Thus, by Lemma
3.12
, we can choose
\rho
large enough that
|W\gamma(v)|\lesssim A^{-2}
and
|W^{2}\gamma(v)|\lesssim A^{-3}
for all
v\in D
.

By Taylor’s theorem, if
v=W^{w}Z^{z}\in D
, then

\gamma(v)=\gamma(Z^{z})+wW\gamma(Z^{z})+O(A^{-3}w^{2}).

Let
\theta(x,y,z)=(-x,-y,z)
and let
\gamma^{\mathsf{e}}(v)=\frac{1}{2}(\gamma(v)+\gamma(\theta(v)))
. Then

\gamma^{\mathsf{e}}(v)=\gamma(Z^{z})+O(A^{-3}w^{2}).

If
q\in B(\mathbf{0},A)
, then
\Pi_{P}(q)=\pi_{p}(q)W^{x(q)}
and
\Pi_{P}(q)\in D
, so
\gamma^{\mathsf{e}}(q)=\gamma(\pi_{p}(q))+O(A^{-3}x(q)^{2}).

By (
81
),

H_{P,\gamma-\gamma\circ\pi_{p}}(\mathbf{0})=\pv(\mathbf{0})\int_{V_{0}\cap B(\mathbf{0},A)}(\gamma(q)-\gamma(\pi_{p}(q)))Y_{\mathsf{L}}\widehat{K}(\Pi_{P}(q))\,\mathrm{d}q+O(A^{-2}).

Let
0<r<A
and let
U=V_{0}\cap(B(\mathbf{0},A)\setminus B(\mathbf{0},r))
. Then by symmetry and the
(-4)
–homogeneity of
Y_{\mathsf{L}}\widehat{K}
,

\left|\int_{U}(\gamma(q)-\gamma(\pi_{p}(q)))Y_{\mathsf{L}}\widehat{K}(\Pi_{P}(q))\,\mathrm{d}q\right|=\left|\int_{U}(\gamma^{\mathsf{e}}(q)-\gamma(\pi_{p}(q)))Y_{\mathsf{L}}\widehat{K}(\Pi_{P}(q))\,\mathrm{d}q\right|\\
\lesssim\int_{U}A^{-3}x(q)^{2}\|q\|_{\mathrm{Kor}}^{-4}\,\mathrm{d}q\lesssim A^{-3}\int_{r}^{A}\kappa^{2}\kappa^{-4}\kappa^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\kappa\leq A^{-2},

where we use Lemma
5.3
to change variables from
q
to
\kappa
.

This holds for any
r
, so

|H_{P,\gamma-\gamma\circ\pi_{p}}(\mathbf{0})|\lesssim A^{-2}
. The lemma then follows from (
80
).
∎

Furthermore, we can write
H_{P,\gamma\circ\pi_{p}}(p)
as a one-dimensional singular integral.

Lemma 9.2
.

With notation as above, for
z\neq 0
and
a\in\mathbb{R}
, let

L(z)=L_{a}(z):=\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}Y_{\mathsf{L}}\widehat{K}((X+aY)^{x}Z^{z})\,\mathrm{d}x.

(83)

For
p\in\Gamma_{\alpha}
and
a=\nabla_{\alpha}\alpha(p)
,

H_{P,\gamma\circ\pi_{p}}(p)=\lim_{r\to 0}\int_{\mathbb{R}\setminus(-r,r)}(\gamma(pZ^{z})-\gamma(\mathbf{0}))L_{a}(z)\,\mathrm{d}z.

(84)

Proof.

After a left translation, we may suppose that
p=\mathbf{0}
.
Let
W=X+aY
so that
P=\langle W,Z\rangle
. Since
Y_{\mathsf{L}}\widehat{K}
is
(-4)
–homogeneous, the integral in (
83
) converges absolutely. Note that for any
z\in\mathbb{R}
and any
t>0
,

L(t^{2}z)=\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}Y_{\mathsf{L}}\widehat{K}(W^{w}Z^{t^{2}z})\,\mathrm{d}w=\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}tY_{\mathsf{L}}\widehat{K}(W^{tw}Z^{t^{2}z})\,\mathrm{d}w\\
=\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}t^{-3}Y_{\mathsf{L}}\widehat{K}(W^{w}Z^{z})\,\mathrm{d}w=t^{-3}L(z).

(85)

We first write both sides of (
84
) in terms of integrals over subsets of
P
.
On one hand, for
r>0
, let

I_{r}:=\int_{\mathbb{R}\setminus(-r^{2},r^{2})}(\gamma(Z^{z})-\gamma(\mathbf{0}))L(z)\,\mathrm{d}z.

By (
85
), since
\gamma
is bounded, this integral converges absolutely. The right side of (
84
) is equal to
\lim_{r\to 0}I_{r}
. For
q=W^{w}Z^{z}\in P
, we have
\pi_{p}(q)=Z^{z}=Z^{z(q)}
, and we define
\gamma_{0}\colon P\to\mathbb{R}
,

\gamma_{0}(q)=\gamma(\pi_{p}(q))-\gamma(\mathbf{0})=\gamma(Z^{z(q)})-\gamma(\mathbf{0}).

Then by Fubini’s Theorem,

I_{r}=\int_{\mathbb{R}\setminus(-r^{2},r^{2})}\gamma_{0}(Z^{z})\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}Y_{\mathsf{L}}\widehat{K}(W^{w}Z^{z})\,\mathrm{d}w\,\mathrm{d}z=\int_{E_{r}}\gamma_{0}(q)Y_{\mathsf{L}}\widehat{K}(q)\,\mathrm{d}q,

where
E_{r}=\{W^{w}Z^{z}\in P\mid|z|\geq r^{2}\}
.

On the other hand, let

J_{r}:=\int_{V_{0}\setminus B(\mathbf{0},r)}(\gamma(\pi_{p}(q))-\gamma(\mathbf{0}))Y_{\mathsf{L}}\widehat{K}(\Pi_{P}(q))\,\mathrm{d}q\\
=\int_{V_{0}\setminus B(\mathbf{0},r)}\gamma_{0}(\Pi_{P}(q))Y_{\mathsf{L}}\widehat{K}(\Pi_{P}(q))\,\mathrm{d}q=\int_{\Pi_{P}(V_{0}\setminus B(\mathbf{0},r))}\gamma_{0}(q)Y_{\mathsf{L}}\widehat{K}(q)\,\mathrm{d}q.

This integral likewise converges absolutely, and
\lim_{r\to 0}J_{r}
is equal to the left side of (
84
).
Let
F_{r}=\Pi_{P}(V_{0}\setminus B(\mathbf{0},r))\subset P
.

Since
|\slope P|\leq 1
, we have
\|v\|_{\mathrm{Kor}}\approx\|\Pi_{P}(v)\|_{\mathrm{Kor}}
for all
v\in V_{0}
. In particular, if
v\in V_{0}\cap B(\mathbf{0},r)
, then
\|\Pi_{P}(v)\|_{\mathrm{Kor}}\leq 2\|v\|_{\mathrm{Kor}}\leq 2r
and
|z(\Pi_{P}(v))|\leq r^{2}
. Therefore,
E_{r}\subset F_{r}
.
We thus consider the difference

J_{r}-I_{r}=\int_{F_{r}\setminus E_{r}}\gamma_{0}(q)Y_{\mathsf{L}}\widehat{K}(q)\,\mathrm{d}q.

For
0<s<S
, let
A_{s,S}=V_{0}\cap(B(\mathbf{0},S)\setminus B(\mathbf{0},s))
, and for
i\geq 0
, let

D_{i}=\Pi_{P}(A_{2^{i}r,2^{i+1}r})\setminus E_{r},

so that up to a measure-zero set,
F_{r}\setminus E_{r}=\bigcup_{i=0}^{\infty}D_{i}
. For
q\in D_{i}
, we have
|z(q)|\leq r^{2}
,
|x(q)|\leq 2^{i+1}r
, and
|Y_{\mathsf{L}}\widehat{K}(q)|\lesssim(2^{i}r)^{-4}
. Furthermore, by the Mean Value Theorem

|\gamma_{0}(q)|=|\gamma(Z^{z(q)})-\gamma(\mathbf{0})|\leq\|\partial_{z}\gamma\|_{\infty}r^{2}\lesssim r^{2}.

Therefore,

|J_{r}-I_{r}|\lesssim\sum_{i=0}^{\infty}\mu(D_{i})\cdot r^{2}\cdot(2^{i}r)^{-4}\lesssim\sum_{i=0}^{\infty}2^{i}r^{5}(2^{i}r)^{-4}=\sum_{i=0}^{\infty}2^{-3i}r\leq 2r.

It follows that
\lim_{r\to 0}J_{r}=\lim_{r\to 0}I_{r}
, which implies (
84
).
∎

For
g\colon\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
, we write

\pv\int_{\mathbb{R}}g(t)\,\mathrm{d}t=\lim_{r\to 0}\int_{\mathbb{R}\setminus(-r,r)}g(t)\,\mathrm{d}t,

as long as the limit on the right exists.

For the rest of this section, we restrict to the special case that
K
is the Riesz kernel

\displaystyle K(x,y,z)

\displaystyle:=\left(\frac{2x(x^{2}+y^{2})-8yz}{((x^{2}+y^{2})^{2}+16z^{2})^{3/2}},\frac{2y(x^{2}+y^{2})+8xz}{((x^{2}+y^{2})^{2}+16z^{2})^{3/2}}\right)

\displaystyle=r^{-6}\left(2x(x^{2}+y^{2})-8yz,2y(x^{2}+y^{2})+8xz\right),

where
r=\|(x,y,z)\|_{\mathrm{Kor}}
. One can calculate that

Y_{\mathsf{L}}\widehat{K}(X^{x}Z^{z})=r^{-10}\left(64z^{3}-20x^{4}z,80x^{2}z^{2}-x^{6}\right).

Integrating this using Mathematica, we find that

\displaystyle L_{0}(1)=\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\frac{\left(64-20x^{4},80x^{2}-x^{6}\right)}{(x^{4}+16)^{\frac{5}{2}}}\,\mathrm{d}x=\left(0,\frac{\Gamma(\frac{3}{4})\Gamma(\frac{7}{4})}{3\sqrt{\pi}}\right)\approx(0,0.212\dots),

where
\Gamma
is the Euler gamma function. By the symmetry of
\widehat{K}
, we have
L_{0}(1)=L_{0}(-1)
. Let
\xi=y(L_{0}(1))
. By (
85
),
L_{0}(z)=(0,|z|^{-\frac{3}{2}}\xi)
for all
z\geq 0
.

A similar calculation shows

X_{\mathsf{L}}\widehat{K}(X^{x}Z^{z})=r^{-10}\left(48x^{2}z^{2}-3x^{6},20x^{4}z-64z^{3}\right)

M_{0}(\pm 1):=\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}X_{\mathsf{L}}\widehat{K}(X^{x}Z^{\pm 1})\,\mathrm{d}x=\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\frac{\left(48x^{2}-3x^{6},\pm(20x^{4}-64)\right)}{(x^{4}+16)^{\frac{5}{2}}}\,\mathrm{d}x=(0,0).

These calculations imply that for any left-invariant horizontal vector field
F=aX_{\mathsf{L}}+bY_{\mathsf{L}}
and any
z\neq 0
, the integral

\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}F\widehat{K}(X^{x}Z^{z})\,\mathrm{d}x=aM_{0}(z)+bL_{0}(z)

is normal to
V_{0}
and is zero only if
F
is a multiple of
X
. The orthogonal symmetry of
K
implies that

\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}F\widehat{K}(W^{w}Z^{z})\,\mathrm{d}w

is likewise normal to
P
and is zero only if
F
is a multiple of
W
. In particular,
L_{a}(z)=|z|^{-\frac{3}{2}}L_{a}(1)
is nonzero and normal to
X+aY
.

We use this formula to prove a lower bound for
G_{f_{i},\nu_{i}}^{\prime}(0)
. Let
\kappa\colon V_{0}\to\mathbb{R}
be as in Section
3
. That is,
\kappa
is a bump function supported on
U=[0,1]\times\{0\}\times[0,1]
. Let
m>0
be such that
\kappa(x,0,z)\geq m
whenever
x,z\in[\frac{1}{4},\frac{3}{4}]
.
Recall that
r_{i}=A^{-1}\rho^{-i}
and that we defined a set of pseudoquads
\mathcal{Q}_{i}=\{Q_{i,1},\dots,Q_{i,k_{i}}\}
that partition
U
, parametrizations
R_{i,j}\colon[0,Ar_{i}]\times[0,r_{i}^{2}]\to Q_{i,j}
, bump functions
\kappa_{i,j}

\kappa_{i,j}(R_{i,j}(s,t))=A^{-1}r_{i}\kappa(A^{-1}r_{i}^{-1}s,r_{i}^{-2}t),

and a set
J_{i}\subset\{1,\dots,k_{i}\}
such that
\nu_{i}=\sum_{j\in J_{i}}\kappa_{i,j}
.

By Lemma
3.7
, there are functions
g_{i,j}
such that

R_{i,j}(s,t)=R_{i,j}(0,0)+(s,g_{i,j}(s,t)),

(86)

where
\partial_{t}g_{i,j}(s,t)\in[\frac{3}{4},\frac{4}{3}]
for all
s
and
t
.

As in Section
3.1
, we can rescale
f_{i}
and
\nu_{i}
by a factor of
r_{i}^{-1}
to get functions
\alpha
and
\gamma
that satisfy Lemma
3.12
. By Lemmas
9.1
and
9.2
and the scale invariance of the Riesz transform, for any
p\in\Gamma_{f_{i}}
,

G^{\prime}_{f_{i},\nu_{i}}(0)(p)=\pv\int_{\mathbb{R}}(\nu_{i}(pZ^{z})-\nu_{i}(p))L_{a}(z)\,\mathrm{d}z+O(A^{-2})\\
=L_{a}(1)\cdot\pv\int_{\mathbb{R}}(\nu_{i}(pZ^{z})-\nu_{i}(p))|z|^{-\frac{3}{2}}\,\mathrm{d}z+O(A^{-2}),

(87)

where
a=\nabla_{f_{i}}f_{i}(p)
.

This lets us prove the following bound.

Lemma 9.3
.

Suppose that
j\in J_{i}
and
s\in[\frac{1}{4}Ar_{i},\frac{3}{4}Ar_{i}]
and let
p=R_{i,j}(s,0)
. There is a
c>0
such that if
A
is sufficiently large, then
|G^{\prime}_{f_{i},\nu_{i}}(0)(p)|\geq cA^{-1}
.

Proof.

Since
j\in J_{i}
, we have

\nu_{i}(R_{i,j}(s,t))=\kappa_{i,j}(R_{i,j}(s,t))=A^{-1}r_{i}\kappa(A^{-1}r_{i}^{-1}s,r_{i}^{-2}t)

for all
t
. In particular,
\nu_{i}(p)=0
. Since
\nu_{i}
is nonnegative,

\pv\int_{\mathbb{R}}(\nu_{i}(pZ^{z})-\nu_{i}(p))|z|^{-\frac{3}{2}}\,\mathrm{d}z=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\nu_{i}(pZ^{z})|z|^{-\frac{3}{2}}\,\mathrm{d}z\geq 0.

Let
z_{0}=z(p)=g_{i,j}(s,0)
. Then for
t\in[0,r_{i}^{2}]
, we have
R_{i,j}(s,t)=pZ^{g_{i,j}(s,t)-z_{0}}
. We thus substitute
z=g_{i,j}(s,t)-z_{0}
. Since
\partial_{z}g_{i,j}\in[\frac{3}{4},\frac{4}{3}]
, we have
\,\mathrm{d}z\approx\,\mathrm{d}t
and
z\approx t
, so

\int_{\mathbb{R}}\nu_{i}(pZ^{z})|z|^{-\frac{3}{2}}\,\mathrm{d}z\geq\int_{0}^{g_{i,j}(s,r_{i}^{2})-z_{0}}\nu_{i}(pZ^{z})|z|^{-\frac{3}{2}}\,\mathrm{d}z\\
\approx\int_{0}^{r_{i}^{2}}\nu_{i}(R_{i,j}(s,t))|t|^{-\frac{3}{2}}\,\mathrm{d}t\approx\int_{0}^{r_{i}^{2}}A^{-1}r_{i}\kappa(A^{-1}r_{i}^{-1}s,r_{i}^{2}t)t^{-\frac{3}{2}}\,\mathrm{d}t.

Let
\hat{s}=A^{-1}r_{i}^{-1}s
and note that
\hat{s}\in[\frac{1}{4},\frac{3}{4}]
. We substitute
u=r_{i}^{2}t
and use the fact that
\kappa(\hat{s},u)\geq m
for all
u\in[\frac{1}{4},\frac{3}{4}]
to obtain

\int_{\mathbb{R}}\nu_{i}(pZ^{z})|z|^{-\frac{3}{2}}\,\mathrm{d}z\gtrsim A^{-1}\int_{0}^{1}r_{i}\kappa(\hat{s},u)(r_{i}^{-2}u)^{-\frac{3}{2}}r_{i}^{-2}\,\mathrm{d}u\geq A^{-1}\int_{\frac{1}{4}}^{\frac{3}{4}}mu^{-\frac{3}{2}}\,\mathrm{d}u\gtrsim\frac{A^{-1}}{2}.

By (
87
), there is a
c_{0}>0
such that
|G^{\prime}_{f_{i},\nu_{i}}(0)(p)|\gtrsim c_{0}A^{-1}|L_{a}(1)|+O(A^{-2})
. Let
c=\frac{c_{0}}{2}\min_{a\in[-1,1]}|L_{a}(1)|
. When
A
is sufficiently large,
|G^{\prime}_{f_{i},\nu_{i}}(0)(p)|\geq cA^{-1}
, as desired.
∎

Now we use Lemma
8.2
to prove part (
4
) of Lemma
6.3
.

Proof of part (
4
) of Lemma
6.3
.

Recall that
S_{i}=\bigcup_{j\not\in J_{i}}Q_{i,j}
. By Proposition
3.1
, there is an
\epsilon>0
such that if
i\leq\epsilon A^{4}
, then
|S_{i}|\leq\frac{1}{2}
and thus

\sum_{j\in J_{i}}|Q_{i,j}|=1-|S_{i}|\geq\frac{1}{2}.

(88)

Let
c
be as in Lemma
9.3
, so that

|G^{\prime}_{f_{i},\nu_{i}}(0)(R_{i,j}(s,0))|\geq cA^{-1}

for all
j\in J_{i}
and
s\in[\frac{1}{4}Ar_{i},\frac{3}{4}Ar_{i}]
.
Let
t\in[0,r_{i}^{2}]
, let
p=R_{i,j}(s,0)
, and let
q=R_{i,j}(s,t)
.
Since
\partial_{t}g_{i,j}(s,t)\in[\frac{3}{4},\frac{4}{3}]
for all
s
and
t
, we have

d_{\mathrm{Kor}}(p,q)\approx\sqrt{g_{i,j}(s,t)-g_{i,j}(s,0)}\approx\sqrt{t}.

By Corollary
8.3
, there is an
a>0
such that

\left|G_{f_{i},\nu_{i}}^{\prime}(0)(p)-G_{f_{i},\nu_{i}}^{\prime}(0)(q)\right|\lesssim(r_{i}^{-1}\sqrt{t})^{a}+\rho^{-\frac{1}{2}}.

We can thus choose
\rho_{0},\delta>0
depending only on
\kappa
such that if
\rho>\rho_{0}
and
t\in[0,\delta r_{i}^{2}]
, then

|G_{f_{i},\nu_{i}}^{\prime}(0)(R_{i,j}(s,t))|\geq\frac{c}{2}A^{-1}.

Then

\int_{Q_{i,j}}|G_{f_{i},\nu_{i}}^{\prime}(0)(q)|\,\mathrm{d}q=\int_{0}^{r_{i}^{2}}\int_{0}^{Ar_{i}}|G_{f_{i},\nu_{i}}^{\prime}(0)(R_{i,j}(s,t))|\partial_{z}g_{i,j}(s,t)\,\mathrm{d}s\,\mathrm{d}t\\
\gtrsim\int_{0}^{\delta r_{i}^{2}}\int_{\frac{1}{4}Ar_{i}}^{\frac{3}{4}Ar_{i}}|G_{f_{i},\nu_{i}}^{\prime}(0)(R_{i,j}(s,t))|\,\mathrm{d}s\,\mathrm{d}t\gtrsim\delta A^{-1}|Q_{i,j}|\gtrsim A^{-1}|Q_{i,j}|.

By (
88
),

\int_{U}|G_{f_{i},\nu_{i}}^{\prime}(0)(q)|\,\mathrm{d}q\gtrsim\sum_{j\in J_{i}}\int_{Q_{i,j}}|G_{f_{i},\nu_{i}}^{\prime}(0)(q)|\,\mathrm{d}q\gtrsim\sum_{j\in J_{i}}A^{-1}|Q_{i,j}|\geq\frac{1}{2}A^{-1},

as desired.
∎

10.
Quasi-orthogonality

In this section, we prove part (
3
) of Lemma
6.3
, which claims that there is an
\epsilon>0
such that
|\langle F_{i}^{\prime}(0),F_{j}^{\prime}(0)\rangle|\lesssim\rho^{-\epsilon}
for all
0\leq i<j
.

Recall that
\nu_{i}
oscillates with wavelength roughly
r_{i}=A^{-1}\rho^{-i}
, so we expect that
F_{i}^{\prime}(0)
also oscillates with wavelength roughly
r_{i}
. Since
r_{j}<\rho^{-1}r_{i}
,
F_{j}^{\prime}(0)
has higher frequency than
F_{i}^{\prime}(0)
. We thus bound
\langle F_{i}^{\prime}(0),F_{j}^{\prime}(0)\rangle
by partitioning
\Psi_{f_{j}}(U)\subset\Gamma_{f_{j}}
into sets of diameter on the order of
r_{j}\rho^{\delta}
for some small
\delta>0
. Let
Q
be such a set. Since
j>i
, Lemma
8.2
implies that
F_{i}^{\prime}(0)
is nearly constant on
Q
. We claim that the average of
F_{j}^{\prime}(0)
on
Q
is small and thus
\int_{Q}F_{i}^{\prime}(0)(q)F_{j}^{\prime}(0)(q)\,\mathrm{d}q
is small.

We start by bounding the average of
F_{j}^{\prime}(0)
on rectangles (Section
10.1
). We will then bound the average of
F_{j}^{\prime}(0)
on pseudoquads (Section
10.2
) and complete the proof of Lemma
6.3
.(
3
) by tiling
U
by pseudoquads (Section
10.3
).

10.1.
Averaging over rectangles

We begin the proof of Lemma
6.3
.(
3
) by bounding the average of
F_{j}^{\prime}(0)
on rectangles of scale roughly
r_{i}\rho^{\epsilon}
.

Let
P\subset\mathbb{H}
be a vertical plane of slope
a
and let
W=X+aY
so that
P=\langle W,Z\rangle
. For
v\in P
and
r>0
, we define

E(v,r;P):=\{vW^{w}Z^{z}\mid|w|\leq r,|z|\leq r^{2}\}.

We call
E(v,r;P)
a
rectangle
in
P
.
In this section, we prove the following lemma.

Lemma 10.1
.

There is an
\epsilon>0
such that
when
\rho
is sufficiently large, the following property holds.
Let
f_{i}
,
\nu_{i}
, and
\Sigma_{i}=\Gamma_{f_{i}}
be as in Section
3
. Let
i\geq 0
, let
p_{0}\in\Sigma_{i}
, and let
P
be the tangent plane to
\Sigma_{i}
at
p_{0}
. Let
p\in P\cap B(p_{0},\rho^{\epsilon}r_{i})
and
2r_{i}<R<\rho^{\epsilon}r_{i}
. Then

\frac{1}{R^{3}}\left|\int_{E(p,R;P)}F_{i}^{\prime}(0)(q)\,\mathrm{d}q\right|\lesssim\rho^{-\epsilon}+\frac{\log(Rr_{i}^{-1})}{Rr_{i}^{-1}}.

(89)

After a rescaling and translation, it suffices to consider the case that
\alpha
and
\gamma
satisfy Lemma
3.12
and
p_{0}=\mathbf{0}\in\Gamma_{\alpha}
.
Let
P
be the tangent plane to
\Gamma_{\alpha}
at
p_{0}
and
W=X+\nabla_{\alpha}\alpha(p_{0})Y
. Let
\epsilon>0
be as in Lemma
8.1
. It suffices to show that for
p\in B(p_{0},\rho^{\frac{\epsilon}{2}};P)
and
2<R<\rho^{\frac{\epsilon}{2}}
,

\frac{1}{R^{3}}\left|\int_{E(p,R;P)}G_{\alpha,\gamma}^{\prime}(0)(q)\,\mathrm{d}q\right|\lesssim\rho^{-\epsilon}+\frac{\log(R)}{R}.

(90)

By Lemma
8.1
, when
\rho
is sufficiently large,

\frac{1}{R^{3}}\left|\int_{E(p,R;P)}G_{\alpha,\gamma}^{\prime}(0)(q)\,\mathrm{d}q-\int_{E(p,R;P)}H_{P,\gamma}(q)\,\mathrm{d}q\right|\lesssim\rho^{-\epsilon}.

(91)

Then Lemma
10.1
is a consequence of the following bound.

Lemma 10.2
.

Let
P
and
\gamma
be as above. Let
p\in P\cap B(p_{0},\sqrt{\rho})
and let
2<R<\sqrt{\rho}
. Then

\frac{1}{R^{3}}\left|\int_{E(p,R;P)}H_{P,\gamma}(q)\,\mathrm{d}q\right|\lesssim\frac{\log(R)}{R}.

We first reduce Lemma
10.2
to a question about a singular integral on
P\times P
. For
r>0
, let

\Delta_{r}=\{(v,w)\in P\times P\mid d_{\mathrm{Kor}}(v,w)<r\}

and for
U\subset P\times P
, define

\pv\int_{U}M(v,w)\,\mathrm{d}(v,w):=\lim_{r\to 0}\int_{U\setminus\Delta_{r}}M(v,w)\,\mathrm{d}(v,w).

Recall that for
v\in P
, we defined

H_{P,\phi}(v)=\pv(v)\int_{pV_{0}}(\phi(w)-\phi(v))Y_{\mathsf{L}}\widehat{K}(v^{-1}\Pi_{P}(w))\,\mathrm{d}w.

Lemma 10.3
.

Let
\lambda
be a bounded smooth function which is constant on cosets of
\langle Y\rangle
. Let
p\in\mathbb{H}
and let
P
be a vertical plane through
p
with finite slope. Then

H_{P,\lambda}(p)=\pv(p)\int_{P}(\lambda(q)-\lambda(p))Y_{\mathsf{L}}\widehat{K}(p^{-1}q)\,\mathrm{d}q

(92)

and

\int_{E(p,R;P)}H_{P,\lambda}(q)\,\mathrm{d}q=\pv\int_{E(p,R;P)\times P}(\lambda(w)-\lambda(v))Y_{\mathsf{L}}\widehat{K}(v^{-1}w)\,\mathrm{d}(v,w).

(93)

Proof.

Without loss of generality, we suppose that
p=\mathbf{0}
. Let
D_{r}:=V_{0}\cap B(\mathbf{0},r)
and
D^{P}_{r}:=P\cap B(\mathbf{0},r)
, and let
A_{r,R}:=D_{R}\setminus D_{r}
and
A^{P}_{r,R}:=D^{P}_{R}\setminus D^{P}_{r}
.
Let
\lambda_{0}(q)=\lambda(q)-\lambda(\mathbf{0})
so that
H_{P,\lambda}(\mathbf{0})=\lim_{\begin{subarray}{c}r\to 0\\
R\to\infty\end{subarray}}L_{r,R}
, where

L_{r,R}:=\int_{D_{R}\setminus D_{r}}\lambda_{0}(q)Y_{\mathsf{L}}\widehat{K}(\Pi_{P}(q))\,\mathrm{d}q.

Likewise, since
\lambda
is constant on cosets of
\langle Y\rangle
, we can write the right side of (
92
) as
\lim_{\begin{subarray}{c}r\to 0\\
R\to\infty\end{subarray}}M_{r,R}
, where

M_{r,R}:=\int_{D^{P}_{R}\setminus D^{P}_{r}}\lambda_{0}(q)Y_{\mathsf{L}}\widehat{K}(q)\,\mathrm{d}q=\int_{\Pi(D^{P}_{R})\setminus\Pi(D^{P}_{r})}\lambda_{0}(q)Y_{\mathsf{L}}\widehat{K}(\Pi_{P}(q))\,\mathrm{d}q.

Then

\displaystyle L_{r,R}-M_{r,R}

\displaystyle=\int_{V_{0}}\left(\mathbf{1}_{D^{P}_{R}}-\mathbf{1}_{D^{P}_{r}}-\mathbf{1}_{\Pi(D^{P}_{R})}+\mathbf{1}_{\Pi(D^{P}_{r})}\right)(q)\lambda_{0}(q)Y_{\mathsf{L}}\widehat{K}(\Pi_{P}(q))\,\mathrm{d}q

\displaystyle=\int_{V_{0}}(\mathbf{1}_{D_{R}}-\mathbf{1}_{\Pi(D^{P}_{R})})(q)\lambda_{0}(q)Y_{\mathsf{L}}\widehat{K}(\Pi_{P}(q))\,\mathrm{d}q

\displaystyle\qquad-\int_{V_{0}}(\mathbf{1}_{D_{r}}-\mathbf{1}_{\Pi(D^{P}_{r})})(q)\lambda_{0}(q)Y_{\mathsf{L}}\widehat{K}(\Pi_{P}(q))\,\mathrm{d}q

\displaystyle=:I_{R}-I_{r}.

Note that there is a
c>1
depending on the slope of
P
such that

\supp(\mathbf{1}_{D_{s}}-\mathbf{1}_{\Pi(D^{P}_{s})})\subset A_{c^{-1}s,cs}

for all
s>0
.

Since
\lambda
is bounded and
Y_{\mathsf{L}}\widehat{K}
is
(-4)
–homogeneous,

|I_{R}|\lesssim|A_{c^{-1}R,cR}|\cdot\|\lambda\|_{\infty}(c^{-1}R)^{-4}\lesssim_{\lambda,P}R^{-1},

so
|I_{R}|\to 0
as
R\to\infty
.

Let
\theta(x,y,z)=(-x,-y,z)
and let
\lambda_{0}^{\mathsf{e}}(q)=\frac{1}{2}(\lambda_{0}(q)+\lambda_{0}(\theta(q)))
be the even part of
\lambda_{0}
. Then
\mathbf{1}_{D_{r}}-\mathbf{1}_{\Pi(D^{P}_{r})}
is even, so

I_{r}=\int_{V_{0}}(\mathbf{1}_{D_{r}}(q)-\mathbf{1}_{\Pi(D^{V}_{r})}(q))\lambda_{0}^{\mathsf{e}}(q)Y_{\mathsf{L}}\widehat{K}(\Pi_{P}(q))\,\mathrm{d}q.

Since
\lambda_{0}
is smooth and
\lambda_{0}(\mathbf{0})=0
, we have
|\lambda_{0}^{\mathsf{e}}(q)|\lesssim_{\lambda}\|q\|_{\mathrm{Kor}}^{2}
when
\|q\|_{\mathrm{Kor}}
is sufficiently small, so when
r
is sufficiently small,

|I_{r}|\lesssim_{\lambda}|A_{c^{-1}r,cr}|\cdot r^{2}r^{-4}\lesssim_{\lambda,P}r.

Therefore,
|L_{r,R}-M_{r,R}|\lesssim_{\lambda,P}r+R^{-1}
, which implies (
92
).

Now let
E=E(p,R;P)
. Since
Y_{\mathsf{L}}\widehat{K}
is
(-4)
–homogeneous and
\lambda
is bounded, for any
r>0
and
v\in P
,

\int_{vA^{P}_{r,\infty}}(\lambda(w)-\lambda(v))Y_{\mathsf{L}}\widehat{K}(v^{-1}w)\,\mathrm{d}w

converges absolutely. Furthermore, by the bounds above and Lemma
7.1
, there is a
c>0
depending only on
\lambda
and
P
such that for
v\in E
,

\left|H_{P,\lambda}(v)-\int_{vA^{P}_{r,\infty}}(\lambda(w)-\lambda(v))Y_{\mathsf{L}}\widehat{K}(v^{-1}w)\,\mathrm{d}w\right|\lesssim cr.

Therefore, using uniform convergence to exchange the integral and the limit,

\displaystyle\int_{E}H_{P,\lambda}(q)\,\mathrm{d}q

\displaystyle=\int_{E}\lim_{r\to 0}\int_{vA^{P}_{r,\infty}}(\lambda(w)-\lambda(v))Y_{\mathsf{L}}\widehat{K}(v^{-1}w)\,\mathrm{d}w\,\mathrm{d}v

\displaystyle=\lim_{r\to 0}\int_{E}\int_{vA^{P}_{r,\infty}}(\lambda(w)-\lambda(v))Y_{\mathsf{L}}\widehat{K}(v^{-1}w)\,\mathrm{d}w\,\mathrm{d}v

\displaystyle=\pv\int_{E\times P}(\lambda(w)-\lambda(v))Y_{\mathsf{L}}\widehat{K}(v^{-1}w)\,\mathrm{d}(v,w).

This proves (
93
).
∎

Now we prove Lemma
10.2
.

Proof of Lemma
10.2
.

Let
E=E(p,R;P)
. By Lemma
10.3

\int_{E}H_{P,\gamma}(v)\,\mathrm{d}v=\pv\int_{E\times P\setminus E}(\gamma(w)-\gamma(v))Y_{\mathsf{L}}\widehat{K}(v^{-1}w)\,\mathrm{d}(v,w)\\
+\pv\int_{E\times E}(\gamma(w)-\gamma(v))Y_{\mathsf{L}}\widehat{K}(v^{-1}w)\,\mathrm{d}(v,w)=:J_{1}+J_{2}.

We claim that
|J_{1}|\lesssim R^{2}\log R
and
|J_{2}|\lesssim R^{2}
.

We first consider
J_{1}
.
First, we claim that
|\gamma(u)-\gamma(v)|\lesssim A^{-1}d_{\mathrm{Kor}}(u,v)
for all
v\in E
and
u\in P
. Let
\kappa:=d_{\mathrm{Kor}}(u,v)
.
On one hand, if
\kappa\geq 1
, then

|\gamma(u)-\gamma(v)|\lesssim\|\gamma\|_{\infty}\lesssim A^{-1}\leq A^{-1}\kappa,

so we consider the case that
\kappa\leq 1
. Then
d_{\mathrm{Kor}}(u,p_{0})\leq d_{\mathrm{Kor}}(u,v)+d_{\mathrm{Kor}}(v,p_{0})\leq 2R
. Let
W=X+\slope(P)Y
and write
u=vW^{w}Z^{z}
for some
|w|\leq\kappa
and
|z|\leq\kappa^{2}
. Since
P
is tangent to
\Gamma_{\alpha}
at
p_{0}
, Lemma
2.9
and Lemma
3.12
imply that for all
q\in P\cap B(p_{0},2R)
,

d_{\mathrm{Kor}}(q,\Gamma_{\alpha})\lesssim\rho^{-1}d_{\mathrm{Kor}}(q,p_{0})^{2}\lesssim 1.

Lemma
8.4
implies that
|W\gamma(q)|\lesssim A^{-1}
and
|Z\gamma(q)|\lesssim A^{-1}
. By the Mean Value Theorem,
|\gamma(u)-\gamma(v)|\lesssim(\kappa+\kappa^{2})A^{-1}\lesssim\kappa A^{-1}
, as desired.

Now let
v\in E
and
\epsilon=d_{\mathrm{Kor}}(v,\partial E)
; suppose
\epsilon>0
.
Let

j_{1}(v)=\int_{P\setminus E}\left|(\gamma(w)-\gamma(v))Y_{\mathsf{L}}\widehat{K}(v^{-1}w)\right|\,\mathrm{d}w.

Since
Y_{\mathsf{L}}\widehat{K}
is
(-4)
–homogeneous, by Lemma
5.3
,

j_{1}(v)\lesssim\int_{P\setminus B(v,\epsilon)}|\gamma(v)-\gamma(w)|d_{\mathrm{Kor}}(v,w)^{-4}\,\mathrm{d}w\\
\lesssim\int_{P\setminus B(v,\epsilon)}\min\{\|\gamma\|_{\infty},A^{-1}d_{\mathrm{Kor}}(v,w)\}d_{\mathrm{Kor}}(v,w)^{-4}\,\mathrm{d}w\lesssim\int_{\epsilon}^{\infty}\min\{r^{-4},r^{-3}\}\cdot r^{2}\,\mathrm{d}r,

so there is a
C>0
such that

j_{1}(v)\leq\begin{cases}C|\log\epsilon|+C&0<\epsilon<1\\
C\epsilon^{-1}&\epsilon\geq 1.\end{cases}

For any
\epsilon_{0}>0
,

\bigl|\{v\in E\mid d_{\mathrm{Kor}}(v,\partial E)<\epsilon_{0}\}\bigr|\lesssim\min\{R^{3},R^{2}\epsilon_{0}\},

so for any
t>0
,

\bigl|\{v\in E\mid j_{1}(v)>t\}\bigr|\lesssim\begin{cases}R^{3}&0\leq t<\frac{C}{R}\\
\frac{C}{t}R^{2}&\frac{C}{R}\leq t<C\\
R^{2}\exp\big(\frac{C-t}{C}\big)&C\leq t.\end{cases}

Therefore,

\int_{E}j_{1}(v)\,\mathrm{d}v\lesssim\frac{C}{R}R^{3}+\int_{CR^{-1}}^{C}\frac{C}{t}R^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t+\int_{C}^{\infty}R^{2}\exp\big(1-\frac{t}{C}\big)\,\mathrm{d}t\\
\leq CR^{2}+CR^{2}\log R+CR^{2}\lesssim R^{2}\log R.

By Fubini’s Theorem and dominated convergence,

|J_{1}|=\left|\lim_{r\to 0}\int_{E}\int_{P\setminus(E\cup B(v,r))}(\gamma(w)-\gamma(v))Y_{\mathsf{L}}\widehat{K}(v^{-1}w)\,\mathrm{d}(v,w)\right|\leq\int_{E}j_{1}(v)\,\mathrm{d}v\lesssim R^{2}\log R.

Now we consider
J_{2}
. We have

\displaystyle J_{2}

\displaystyle=\pv\int_{E\times E}(\gamma(w)-\gamma(v))Y_{\mathsf{L}}\widehat{K}(v^{-1}w)\,\mathrm{d}(v,w)

\displaystyle=\pv\int_{E\times E}\gamma(w)Y_{\mathsf{L}}\widehat{K}(v^{-1}w)\,\mathrm{d}(v,w)-\pv\int_{E\times E}\gamma(v)Y_{\mathsf{L}}\widehat{K}(v^{-1}w)\,\mathrm{d}(v,w).

Exchanging
v
and
w
in the first term, we get

J_{2}=\pv\int_{E\times E}\gamma(v)(Y_{\mathsf{L}}\widehat{K}(w^{-1}v)-Y_{\mathsf{L}}\widehat{K}(v^{-1}w))\,\mathrm{d}(v,w)=\pv\int_{E\times E}\gamma(v)M(v^{-1}w)\,\mathrm{d}(v,w),

where
M(p)=Y_{\mathsf{L}}\widehat{K}(p^{-1})-Y_{\mathsf{L}}\widehat{K}(p)
.
We use the following lemma to show that
M
is vertically antisymmetric, i.e.,
M(W^{w}Z^{z})=-M(W^{w}Z^{-z})
for all
w,z\in\mathbb{R}
.

Lemma 10.4
.

Let
N\colon\mathbb{H}\to\mathbb{R}^{2}
be an orthogonal kernel. For any horizontal vector
W
, let
W_{\mathsf{L}}
be the corresponding left-invariant vector field. Let
\theta(x,y,z)=(-x,-y,z)
be the homomorphism that rotates
\mathbb{H}
around the
z
–axis by
\pi
.
Then for any
g\in\mathbb{H}
and any horizontal vector
W
,

q_{W}(g):=W_{\mathsf{L}}N(g)-W_{\mathsf{L}}N(g^{-1})-W_{\mathsf{L}}N(\theta(g))+W_{\mathsf{L}}N(\theta(g^{-1}))=0.

We defer the proof until after the proof of Lemma
10.2
. Let
h\colon\mathbb{H}\to\mathbb{H}
,
h(x,y,z)=(x,y,-z)
, so that
h(q)=\theta(q^{-1})
for any
q\in\mathbb{H}
. Then
\widehat{K}
is an orthogonal kernel, so for
q=W^{w}Z^{z}\in P
,

M(W^{w}Z^{z})+M(W^{w}Z^{-z})=Y_{\mathsf{L}}\widehat{K}(q^{-1})-Y_{\mathsf{L}}\widehat{K}(q)+Y_{\mathsf{L}}\widehat{K}(\theta(q))-Y_{\mathsf{L}}\widehat{K}(\theta(q^{-1}))=0.

By Fubini’s theorem,

\displaystyle J_{2}

\displaystyle=\lim_{r\to 0}\int_{E\times E\setminus\Delta_{r}}\gamma(v)M(v^{-1}w)\,\mathrm{d}(v,w)

\displaystyle=\lim_{r\to 0}\int_{E}\gamma(v)\int_{E\setminus B(v,r)}M(v^{-1}w)\,\mathrm{d}w\,\mathrm{d}v

\displaystyle=\lim_{r\to 0}\int_{E}\gamma(v)\int_{v^{-1}E\setminus B(\mathbf{0},r)}M(w)\,\mathrm{d}w\,\mathrm{d}v.

For
S\subset P
and
r>0
, let
k_{r}(S)=\int_{S\setminus B(\mathbf{0},r)}M(w)\,\mathrm{d}w
. Then

S=(S\setminus h(S))\sqcup(S\cap h(S)).

The symmetry of
M
implies that
k_{r}(S\cap h(S))=0
and thus
k_{r}(S)=k_{r}(S\setminus h(S))
.

Let
w_{0}\in(-R,R)
and
z_{0}\in[0,R^{2})
, so that
v=W^{w_{0}}Z^{z_{0}}
lies in the top half of
E
. Let
\delta=R^{2}-z_{0}\in(0,R^{2})
. Then

v^{-1}E=\{W^{w}Z^{z}:|w+w_{0}|\leq R,z\in(\delta-2R^{2},\delta)\}

and

v^{-1}E\setminus h(v^{-1}E)=\{W^{w}Z^{z}:|w+w_{0}|\leq R,z\in(\delta-2R^{2},-\delta]\}.

That is,
v^{-1}E\setminus h(v^{-1}E)\subset A^{P}_{2\sqrt{\delta},\infty}
. Therefore, for all
r>0
, we can use Lemma
5.3
and
(-4)
–homogeneity of
M
to show that

|k_{r}(v^{-1}E)|=|k_{r}(v^{-1}E\setminus h(v^{-1}E)|\leq\int_{A^{P}_{2\sqrt{\delta},\infty}}|M(w)|\,\mathrm{d}w\lesssim\int_{2\sqrt{\delta}}^{\infty}\rho^{-2}\,\mathrm{d}\rho\lesssim\delta^{-\frac{1}{2}}.

More generally, letting
\delta(v)=\bigl|R^{2}-|z(v)|\bigr|
, we have
|k_{r}(v^{-1}E)|\lesssim\delta(v)^{-\frac{1}{2}}
for all
r>0
and all
v\in E
. By dominated convergence,

|J_{2}|=\left|\lim_{r\to 0}\int_{E}\gamma(v)k_{r}(v^{-1}E)\,\mathrm{d}v\right|\lesssim\|\gamma\|_{\infty}\int_{E}\delta(v)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\,\mathrm{d}v\\
\lesssim 2R\int_{-R^{2}}^{R^{2}}\bigl|R^{2}-|z|\bigr|^{-\frac{1}{2}}\,\mathrm{d}z=4R\int_{0}^{R^{2}}z^{-\frac{1}{2}}\,\mathrm{d}z=8R^{2}.

Therefore,

\left|\int_{E}H_{P,\gamma}(v)\,\mathrm{d}v\right|\leq|J_{1}|+|J_{2}|\lesssim R^{2}\log R,

as desired.
∎

We used Lemma
10.4
in the proof of Lemma
10.2
, and we prove it now.

Proof of Lemma
10.4
.

We first consider the case that
g=(x,0,z)\in V_{0}
. Any left-invariant horizontal field can be written as a linear combination of
X_{\mathsf{L}}
and
Y_{\mathsf{L}}
, so it suffices to consider
W=X
or
W=Y
.

Let
I\colon\mathbb{H}\to\mathbb{H}
be an involutory linear isometry of
\mathbb{H}
that fixes
\mathbf{0}
. Then
N(I(h))=I(N(h))
for all
h\in\mathbb{H}
, so by the chain rule,

W_{\mathsf{L}}N(I(g))=I_{*}(W_{\mathsf{L}})[N\circ I](g)=I_{*}(W_{\mathsf{L}})[I\circ N](g)=I(I_{*}(W_{\mathsf{L}})N(g)).

Let
\phi(x,y,z)=(x,-y,-z)
and
\psi(x,y,z)=(-x,y,-z)
so that

q_{W}(g)=W_{\mathsf{L}}N(g)-W_{\mathsf{L}}N(\psi(g))-W_{\mathsf{L}}N(\theta(g))+W_{\mathsf{L}}N(\phi(g)).

Let
X_{\mathsf{L}}N(g)=(a,b)
. Since
\phi
,
\psi
, and
\theta
are involutory isometries of
\mathbb{H}
,

\displaystyle q_{X}(g)

\displaystyle=X_{\mathsf{L}}Ng)-\psi(\psi_{*}(X_{\mathsf{L}})N(g))-\theta(\theta_{*}(X_{\mathsf{L}})N(g))+\phi(\phi_{*}(X_{\mathsf{L}})N(g))

\displaystyle=X_{\mathsf{L}}N(g)+\psi(X_{\mathsf{L}}N(g))+\theta(X_{\mathsf{L}}N(g))+\phi(X_{\mathsf{L}}N(g))

\displaystyle=(a,b)+(-a,b)+(-a,-b)+(a,-b)=0.

Let
Y_{\mathsf{L}}N(g)=(c,d)
. Then

\displaystyle q_{Y}(g)

\displaystyle=Y_{\mathsf{L}}N(g)-\psi(\psi_{*}(Y_{\mathsf{L}})N(g))-\theta(\theta_{*}(Y_{\mathsf{L}})N(g))+\phi(\phi_{*}(Y_{\mathsf{L}})N(g))

\displaystyle=Y_{\mathsf{L}}N(g)-\psi(Y_{\mathsf{L}}N(g))+\theta(Y_{\mathsf{L}}N(g))-\phi(Y_{\mathsf{L}}N(g))

\displaystyle=(c,d)-(-c,d)+(-c,-d)-(c,-d)=0.

Thus the lemma holds for
g\in V_{0}
.

Let
g\in V_{0}
and let
R\colon\mathbb{H}\to\mathbb{H}
be a rotation around the
z
–axis. Let
W^{\prime}=R(W)
so that
R_{*}(W_{\mathsf{L}})=W_{\mathsf{L}}^{\prime}
. Then, as above,
W_{\mathsf{L}}N(R(g))=R(R_{*}(W_{\mathsf{L}})N(g)),
and since
R
commutes with
\theta
,

\displaystyle q_{W}(R(g))

\displaystyle=W_{\mathsf{L}}N(R(g))-W_{\mathsf{L}}N(R(g^{-1}))-W_{\mathsf{L}}N(R(\theta(g)))+W_{\mathsf{L}}N(R(\theta(g^{-1})))

\displaystyle=R\left(W_{\mathsf{L}}^{\prime}N(g)-W_{\mathsf{L}}^{\prime}N(g^{-1})-W_{\mathsf{L}}^{\prime}N(\theta(g))+W_{\mathsf{L}}^{\prime}N(\theta(g^{-1}))\right)

\displaystyle=R(q_{W^{\prime}}(g))=0.

Any point in
\mathbb{H}
can be written as
R(g)
for some rotation
R
and some
g\in V_{0}
, so
q_{W}(h)=0
for all
h\in\mathbb{H}
.
∎

10.2.
Averaging over pseudoquads

In the previous section, we bounded the average of
F_{i}^{\prime}(0)
on rectangles of the form
E(p,r;P)
, where
P
is tangent to
\Sigma_{i}
at
p_{0}
and
d_{\mathrm{Kor}}(p_{0},p)\leq r_{i}\rho^{\epsilon}
. The projections of these rectangles do not tile
V_{0}
, because
P
depends on
p_{0}
, so in this section, we will bound the average of
F_{i}^{\prime}(0)
on pseudoquads for
\Sigma_{i}
.

We will need the following bound on the size of a pseudoquad of given height and width.

Lemma 10.5
.

Let
\psi
be a
\lambda
–intrinsic Lipschitz function for some
\lambda\in(0,1)
. Let
\delta_{x},\delta_{z}\geq 0
and let
g_{1},g_{2}\in C^{1}(\mathbb{R})
be functions such that for all
x
,
g_{1}(x)\leq g_{2}(x)
,
g_{i}^{\prime}(x)=-\psi(x,0,g_{i}(x))
, and
\delta_{z}=g_{2}(0)-g_{1}(0)
.
Then for any
x\in\mathbb{R}
,

|g_{1}(x)-g_{2}(x)|\lesssim_{\lambda}\delta_{z}+x^{2}.

Let

Q=\{(x,0,z)\mid x\in[0,\delta_{x}],z\in[g_{1}(x),g_{2}(x)]\}.

Then

\diam\Psi_{\psi}(Q)\lesssim_{\lambda}\delta_{x}+\sqrt{\delta_{z}}

and

|Q|\lesssim_{\lambda}\delta_{x}\delta_{z}+\delta_{x}^{3}.

Proof.

By (
11
), since
\psi
is
\lambda
–intrinsic Lipschitz, the
g_{i}
satisfy

|g_{i}^{\prime}(x)-g_{i}^{\prime}(x^{\prime})|=|\psi(x,0,g_{i}(x))-\psi(x^{\prime},0,g_{i}(x^{\prime}))|\leq\|\nabla_{\psi}\psi\|_{\infty}|x-x^{\prime}|\leq L|x-x^{\prime}|,

(94)

for all
x,x^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}
, where
L=\lambda(1-\lambda^{2})^{-\frac{1}{2}}
. By Lemma
2.6
,

|\psi(0,0,z_{1})-\psi(0,0,z_{2})|\leq\frac{2}{1-\lambda}d_{\mathrm{Kor}}((0,0,z_{1}),(0,0,z_{2}))=\frac{4}{1-\lambda}\sqrt{|z_{2}-z_{1}|}.

(95)

Then
|g_{1}^{\prime}(0)-g_{2}^{\prime}(0)|=|\psi(0,0,g_{1}(0))-\psi(0,0,g_{2}(0))|\leq\frac{4}{1-\lambda}\sqrt{\delta_{z}}
, and

|g_{1}^{\prime}(x)-g_{2}^{\prime}(x)|\leq\frac{4}{1-\lambda}\sqrt{\delta_{z}}+2L|x|

for all
x
. Integrating this inequality and using the definition of
\delta_{z}
, we find

|g_{1}(x)-g_{2}(x)|\leq\delta_{z}+\frac{4}{1-\lambda}|x|\sqrt{\delta_{z}}+Lx^{2}\lesssim_{\lambda}\delta_{z}+x^{2}.

In particular,

|Q|=\int_{0}^{\delta_{x}}|g_{2}(x)-g_{1}(x)|\,\mathrm{d}x\leq\delta_{x}\cdot\left(\delta_{z}+\frac{4}{1-\lambda}\delta_{x}\sqrt{\delta_{z}}+L\delta_{x}^{2}\right)\lesssim_{\lambda}\delta_{x}\delta_{z}+\delta_{x}^{3}.

Finally, let

E=\{\Psi_{\psi}(0,0,z)\mid z\in[g_{1}(0),g_{2}(0)]\}

be the left edge of
\Psi_{\psi}(Q)
. By
(
95
),
\diam(E)\lesssim\sqrt{\delta_{z}}
.
Every point
p\in\Psi_{\psi}(Q)
lies on a horizontal curve in
\Psi_{\psi}(Q)
that intersects
E
, and we can parametrize this curve as
\gamma=(\gamma_{x},\gamma_{y},\gamma_{z})\colon[0,\delta_{x}]\to Q
where
x(\gamma(t))=t
. By (
94
),
|\gamma_{y}^{\prime}(t)|\leq L
, so

\ell(\gamma)=\int_{0}^{\delta_{x}}\sqrt{1+\gamma_{y}^{\prime}(t)^{2}}\,\mathrm{d}t\leq\delta_{x}\sqrt{1+L^{2}},

and

d_{\mathrm{Kor}}(\Psi_{\psi}(0,0,g_{1}(0)),p)\leq\diam E+\ell(\gamma)\lesssim_{\lambda}\sqrt{\delta_{z}}+\delta_{x},

as desired.
∎

Now we bound the integral of
F_{i}^{\prime}(0)
on a pseudoquad.

Lemma 10.6
.

Let
r_{i}=A^{-1}\rho^{-i}
and let
f_{i}
,
\nu_{i}
, and
\Sigma_{i}=\Gamma_{f_{i}}
be as in Section
3
. There is a
\delta>0
such that if
\rho
is sufficiently large, then for any pseudoquad
Q
for
\Sigma_{i}
with
\delta_{x}(Q)\leq r_{i}\rho^{\delta}
and
\delta_{z}(Q)\leq r_{i}^{2}\rho^{2\delta}
, we have

\left|\int_{Q}F_{i}^{\prime}(0)(q)\,\mathrm{d}q\right|\lesssim r_{i}^{3}\rho^{3\delta-\frac{\delta}{3}}.

Proof.

Let
\epsilon>0
be as in Lemma
10.1
and let
\delta=\frac{\epsilon}{2}
.
After a left-translation, we may suppose that
f_{i}(\mathbf{0})=0
and that the lower left corner of
\Psi_{f_{i}}(Q)
is
\mathbf{0}
. That is,

Q=\{(x,0,z)\mid x\in[0,\delta_{x}(Q)],z\in[g_{1}(x),g_{2}(x)]\}

where
g_{1},g_{2}\colon[0,\delta_{x}(Q)]\to\mathbb{R}
are functions with characteristic graphs such that
g_{1}(0)=0
,
g_{2}(0)=\delta_{z}(Q)
.
By Lemma
10.5
,
|g_{2}(x)-g_{1}(x)|\lesssim r_{i}^{2}\rho^{2\delta}
for all
x\in[0,\delta_{x}(Q)]
,

\diam(\Psi_{f_{i}}(Q))\lesssim r_{i}\rho^{\delta}
,
and
|Q|\lesssim r_{i}^{3}\rho^{3\delta}
.
In particular, for any
q\in Q
, the intrinsic Lipschitz condition implies

|f_{i}(q)|\lesssim|f_{i}(\mathbf{0})|+\diam(Q)\lesssim r_{i}\rho^{\delta}.

(96)

Let
P
be the tangent plane to
\Sigma_{i}
at
\mathbf{0}
, and let
\sigma=\nabla_{f_{i}}f_{i}(\mathbf{0})
be the slope of
P
. Note that
|\sigma|\leq 1
.
Let
W=X+\sigma Y
so that
P=\langle W,Z\rangle
.
We will cover
\Pi_{P}(Q)
by rectangles. Recall that

\Pi_{P}(x,0,z)=(x,0,z)\cdot Y^{\sigma x}=\left(x,\sigma x,z+\frac{\sigma}{2}x^{2}\right)=W^{x}Z^{z+\frac{\sigma}{2}x^{2}}.

Let
\bar{g}_{j}(x)=g_{j}(x)+\frac{\sigma}{2}x^{2}
, so that

\Pi_{P}(X^{x}Z^{g_{j}(x)})=X^{x}Z^{g_{j}(x)}Y^{\sigma x}=W^{x}Z^{\bar{g}_{j}(x)}

and

\Pi_{P}(Q)=\left\{W^{w}Z^{z}\mid w\in[0,\delta_{x}(Q)],z\in[\bar{g}_{1}(w),\bar{g}_{2}(w)]\right\}.

Note that
d_{\mathrm{Kor}}(q,\Pi_{P}(q))\leq|x(q)|
, so
\diam(\Pi_{P}(Q))\lesssim\diam(Q)\lesssim r_{i}\rho^{\delta}
.

Since
g_{j}
has a characteristic graph, it satisfies

g^{\prime}_{j}(x)=-f_{i}(x,0,g_{j}(x))=-f_{i}(X^{x}Z^{g_{j}(x)});

it follows that
\bar{g}_{j}
satisfies

\bar{g}^{\prime}_{j}(x)=-f_{i}(X^{x}Z^{g_{j}(x)})+\sigma x=\sigma x-f_{i}(W^{x}Z^{\bar{g}_{j}(x)}).

(97)

In particular, for
x\in[0,\delta_{x}(Q)]
,

|\bar{g}_{j}^{\prime}(x)|\leq|\sigma|\delta_{x}+\left|f_{i}(W^{x}Z^{\bar{g}_{j}^{\prime}(x)})\right|\lesssim r_{i}\rho^{\delta}.

(98)

Let
R=r_{i}\rho^{\frac{\delta}{2}}
and let

D=\{W^{w}Z^{z}\mid w\in[0,2R],z\in[0,2R^{2}]\}

This is a translate of
E(\mathbf{0},R;P)
. Let

\mathcal{T}=\{W^{2jR}Z^{2kR^{2}}D\mid j,k\in\mathbb{Z}\}

be a tiling of
P
by translates of
D
, let

\mathcal{S}_{0}=\{E\in\mathcal{T}\mid\interior E\subset\Pi_{P}(Q)\},

and let

\mathcal{S}_{1}=\{E\in\mathcal{T}\mid\interior E\cap\partial\Pi_{P}(Q)\neq\emptyset\}.

The rectangles in
\mathcal{S}_{0}
and
\mathcal{S}_{1}
cover
\Pi_{P}(Q)
, and

\left|\int_{Q}F^{\prime}_{i}(0)(q)\,\mathrm{d}q\right|\leq\sum_{E\in\mathcal{S}_{0}}\left|\int_{E}F^{\prime}_{i}(0)(q)\,\mathrm{d}q\right|+\sum_{E\in\mathcal{S}_{1}}\int_{E}|F^{\prime}_{i}(0)(q)|\,\mathrm{d}q.

(99)

Since each rectangle in
\mathcal{S}_{0}
has measure
4R^{3}
, we have
4R^{3}\cdot\#\mathcal{S}_{0}\leq|Q|\lesssim r_{i}^{3}\rho^{3\delta}
. When
\rho
is sufficiently large, we have
E\subset B(\mathbf{0},r_{i}\rho^{\epsilon})
for every
E\in\mathcal{S}_{0}\cup\mathcal{S}_{1}
, so Lemma
10.1
implies that

\left|\int_{E}F^{\prime}_{i}(0)(q)\,\mathrm{d}q\right|\lesssim R^{3}\rho^{-\epsilon}+R^{3}\frac{\log(Rr_{i}^{-1})}{Rr_{i}^{-1}}=R^{3}\rho^{-\epsilon}+R^{3}\frac{\log\rho^{\frac{\delta}{2}}}{\rho^{\frac{\delta}{2}}}\lesssim R^{3}\rho^{-\frac{\delta}{3}}

for any
E\in\mathcal{S}_{0}
. Then

\sum_{E\in\mathcal{S}_{0}}\left|\int_{E}F^{\prime}_{i}(0)(q)\,\mathrm{d}q\right|\lesssim\#\mathcal{S}_{0}\cdot R^{3}\rho^{-\frac{\delta}{3}}\lesssim r_{i}^{3}\rho^{3\delta-\frac{\delta}{3}}.

(100)

Now we consider the
\mathcal{S}_{1}
term. We first bound the number of elements of
\mathcal{S}_{1}
.
If
E\in\mathcal{S}_{1}
, then
E
intersects one of the edges of
\Pi_{P}(Q)
. Let
\mathcal{S}^{\text{lr}}_{1}\subset\mathcal{S}_{1}
be the set of rectangles that intersect the left or right edge and let
\mathcal{S}^{\text{tb}}_{1}\subset\mathcal{S}_{1}
be the set that intersect the top or bottom edge.

By Lemma
10.5
, there is a
C>1
such that the left and right edges of
\Pi_{P}(Q)
are vertical segments of height at most
Cr_{i}^{2}\rho^{2\delta}
. Since each
E\in\mathcal{T}
is a rectangle of height
2R^{2}
,

\#\mathcal{S}_{1}^{\text{lr}}\leq\frac{Cr_{i}^{2}\rho^{2\delta}}{R^{2}}+2\lesssim C\rho^{\delta}.

The top and bottom edges of
\Pi_{P}(Q)
are the curves

\gamma_{j}=\{W^{w}Z^{\bar{g}_{j}(w)}\mid w\in[0,\delta_{x}(Q)]\}.

We can partition
\mathcal{T}
into strips of rectangles with the same
x
–coordinates, i.e.

\mathcal{T}_{k}=\{E\in\mathcal{T}\mid x(E)=[2kR,2(k+1)R]\}.

Then for each
0\leq k\leq\frac{\delta_{x}(Q)}{2R}
,

|\{E\in\mathcal{T}_{k}\mid E\cap\gamma_{j}\neq\emptyset\}|\leq\frac{1}{2R^{2}}\int_{2kR}^{2(k+1)R}|\bar{g}_{j}^{\prime}(x)|\,\mathrm{d}x+2\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:barg-deriv}}}{{\lesssim}}\frac{1}{R}r_{i}\rho^{\delta}+2\leq\rho^{\frac{\delta}{2}}

and

\#\mathcal{S}_{1}^{\text{tb}}\lesssim\frac{\delta_{x}(Q)}{R}\rho^{\frac{\delta}{2}}\leq\rho^{\delta}.

Therefore,
\#\mathcal{S}_{1}\lesssim\rho^{\delta}
. By part (
1
) of Lemma
6.3
,
\|F_{i}^{\prime}(0)\|_{\infty}\lesssim A^{-1}
, so

\sum_{E\in\mathcal{S}_{1}}\int_{E}|F^{\prime}_{i}(0)(q)|\,\mathrm{d}q\lesssim\rho^{\delta}R^{3}A^{-1}\leq r_{i}^{3}\rho^{\frac{5}{2}\delta}.

(101)

By (
99
), (
100
), and (
101
),

\left|\int_{Q}F^{\prime}_{i}(0)(q)\,\mathrm{d}q\right|\lesssim r_{i}^{3}\rho^{3\delta-\frac{\delta}{3}}+r_{i}^{3}\rho^{3\delta-\frac{\delta}{2}}\lesssim r_{i}^{3}\rho^{3\delta-\frac{\delta}{3}},

as desired.
∎

10.3.
Proof of Lemma
6.3
.(
3
)

Let
0\leq i<j\leq N
.
Let
\delta>0
be as in Lemma
10.6
; note that we can take
\delta<\frac{1}{2}
. Let
\epsilon>0
be as in Lemma
8.2
; we take
\epsilon<1
. We claim that

|\langle F_{i}^{\prime}(0),F_{j}^{\prime}(0)\rangle|\lesssim\rho^{-\min\{\frac{\epsilon}{4},\frac{\delta}{3}\}}.

Recall that
f_{j}
is supported on the unit square
U=[0,1]\times\{0\}\times[0,1]
, so that the top and bottom boundaries of
U
are characteristic curves of
\Sigma_{j}
.

Let

w\in[\frac{1}{2}r_{j}\rho^{\delta},r_{j}\rho^{\delta}]
and
h\in[\frac{1}{2}r_{j}^{2}\rho^{2\delta},r_{j}^{2}\rho^{2\delta}]
be such that
N_{x}:=w^{-1}
and
N_{z}:=h^{-1}
are integers. For
m=0,\dots,N_{x}
and
k=0,\dots,N_{z}
, let
v_{m,k}=(mw,0,kh)\in V_{0}
and let
g_{m,k}\colon[mw,(m+1)w]\to\mathbb{R}
be the function such that the graph
z=g_{m,k}(x)
is a segment of the characteristic curve of
\Sigma_{j}
through
v_{m,k}
. For
m=0,\dots,N_{x}-1
and
k=0,\dots,N_{z}-1
, let
Q_{m,k}
be the pseudoquad

Q_{m,k}:=\{(x,0,z)\mid x\in[mw,(m+1)w],z\in[g_{m,k}(x),g_{m,k+1}(x)]\};

this is the pseudoquad of
\Sigma_{j}
with lower-left corner
v_{m,k}
,
\delta_{x}(Q_{m,k})=w
, and
\delta_{z}(Q_{m,k})=h
.
The pseudoquads
Q_{m,k}
then have disjoint interiors and cover
U
.

By Lemma
10.6
, for every
m
and
k
,

\left|\int_{Q_{m,k}}F_{j}^{\prime}(0)(q)\,\mathrm{d}q\right|\lesssim r_{j}^{3}\rho^{3\delta-\frac{\delta}{3}}.

(102)

Suppose that
p,q\in Q_{m,k}
. We claim that
\Psi_{f_{i}}(p)
is close to
\Psi_{f_{i}}(q)
and thus
|F_{i}^{\prime}(0)(p)-F_{i}^{\prime}(0)(q)|
is small.
Let
p_{n}=\Psi_{f_{n}}(p)
and
q_{n}=\Psi_{f_{n}}(q)
.
By Lemma
10.5
,

d_{\mathrm{Kor}}(p_{j},q_{j})\leq\diam\Psi_{f_{j}}(Q_{m,k})\lesssim r_{j}\rho^{\delta}.

Since
i<j
,

\|f_{j}-f_{i}\|_{\infty}\leq\sum_{n=i}^{j-1}\|\nu_{n}\|_{\infty}\lesssim\sum_{n=i}^{j-1}A^{-1}r_{n}\lesssim r_{i}.

Let
a=f_{i}(p)-f_{j}(p)
and let
b=x(q)-x(p)
. Then
p_{i}=p_{j}Y^{a}
, and

d_{\mathrm{Kor}}(p_{i},q_{i}\langle Y\rangle)\leq d_{\mathrm{Kor}}(p_{i},q_{j}Y^{a})=\|Y^{-a}p_{j}^{-1}q_{j}Y^{a}\|_{\mathrm{Kor}}\\
=\|p_{j}^{-1}q_{j}Z^{ab}\|_{\mathrm{Kor}}\lesssim d_{\mathrm{Kor}}(p_{j},q_{j})+\sqrt{ab}\lesssim r_{j}\rho^{\delta}+\sqrt{r_{i}r_{j}\rho^{\delta}}.

Since
j>i
, we have
r_{j}\leq\rho^{-1}r_{i}
, so

d_{\mathrm{Kor}}(p_{i},q_{i}\langle Y\rangle)\lesssim r_{i}\rho^{-1+\delta}+r_{i}\rho^{\frac{-1+\delta}{2}}\lesssim r_{i}\rho^{-\frac{1}{4}}.

Let
m=d_{\mathrm{Kor}}(p_{i},q_{i}\langle Y\rangle)
and let
c\in q_{i}\langle Y\rangle
satisfy
d_{\mathrm{Kor}}(p_{i},c)=m
. Note that
p_{i}=\Psi_{f_{i}}(p_{i})
and so
y(p_{i})=f_{i}(p_{i})
. As the
y
function is 1-Lipschitz we get that
|f_{i}(p_{i})-y(c)|\lesssim m
. By Lemma
2.6
,
|f_{i}(p_{i})-f_{i}(q_{i})|\lesssim d_{\mathrm{Kor}}(p_{i},q_{i}\langle Y\rangle)
. Thus,

d_{\mathrm{Kor}}(p_{i},q_{i})\leq d_{\mathrm{Kor}}(p_{i},c)+|y(c)-f_{i}(p_{i})|+|f_{i}(p_{i})-f_{i}(q_{i})|\lesssim m\lesssim r_{i}\rho^{-\frac{1}{4}}.

Therefore, by Corollary
8.3
,

|F_{i}^{\prime}(0)(p)-F_{i}^{\prime}(0)(q)|=|F_{i}^{\prime}(0)(p_{i})-F_{i}^{\prime}(0)(q_{i})|\lesssim\rho^{-\frac{\epsilon}{4}}.

(103)

Then

\displaystyle\Bigg|\int_{U}

\displaystyle F_{i}^{\prime}(0)(q)F_{j}^{\prime}(0)(q)\,\mathrm{d}q\Bigg|\leq\sum_{m,k}\left|\int_{Q_{m,k}}F_{i}^{\prime}(0)(q)F_{j}^{\prime}(0)(q)\,\mathrm{d}q\right|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{m,k}\left|\int_{Q_{m,k}}F_{j}^{\prime}(0)(q)\left[\left(F_{i}^{\prime}(0)(q)-F_{i}^{\prime}(0)(v_{m,k})\right)+F_{i}^{\prime}(0)(v_{m,k})\right]\,\mathrm{d}q\right|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{m,k}\int_{Q_{m,k}}\left|F_{i}^{\prime}(0)(q)-F_{i}^{\prime}(0)(v_{m,k})\right||F_{j}^{\prime}(0)(q)|\,\mathrm{d}q

\displaystyle\qquad+\sum_{m,k}|F_{i}^{\prime}(0)(v_{m,k})|\left|\int_{Q_{m,k}}F_{j}^{\prime}(0)(q)\,\mathrm{d}q\right|,

where the sums are all taken over
0\leq m<N_{x}
and
0\leq k<N_{z}
.
Lemma
6.3
.(
1
) implies that
\|F_{n}^{\prime}(0)\|_{\infty}\lesssim A^{-1}\lesssim 1
, so by (
103
),

\sum_{m,k}\int_{Q_{m,k}}\left|F_{i}^{\prime}(0)(q)-F_{i}^{\prime}(0)(v_{m,k})\right||F_{j}^{\prime}(0)(q)|\,\mathrm{d}q\lesssim|U|\rho^{-\frac{\epsilon}{4}}\|F_{j}^{\prime}(0)\|_{\infty}\lesssim\rho^{-\frac{\epsilon}{4}}.

Likewise, by (
102
),

\sum_{m,k}|F_{i}^{\prime}(0)(v_{m,k})|\left|\int_{Q_{m,k}}F_{j}^{\prime}(0)(q)\,\mathrm{d}q\right|\lesssim N_{x}N_{z}r_{j}^{3}\rho^{3\delta-\frac{\delta}{3}}\lesssim\rho^{-\frac{\delta}{3}}.

Therefore,

\big|\langle F_{i}^{\prime}(0),F_{j}^{\prime}(0)\rangle\big|\lesssim\rho^{-\frac{\delta}{3}}+\rho^{-\frac{\epsilon}{4}},

as desired.

11.
Second derivative bounds

In this section, we will prove the following lemma.

Lemma 11.1
.

For any
A>1
and any
C>0
, if
\rho
is sufficiently large, then the following bounds hold. Let
\alpha,\gamma\colon\mathbb{H}\to\mathbb{R}
be functions that satisfy Lemma
3.12
.
Then

\left\|G_{\alpha,\gamma}^{\prime\prime}(t)\right\|_{\infty}\lesssim_{C}A^{-3}

for all
t\in[0,1]
.

We first set some notation that we will use in the rest of this section. Similarly to Section
7
, given functions
\alpha
and
\gamma
that satisfy Lemma
3.12
, for
\tau\in\mathbb{R}
, we define

a_{\tau}(w):=(\alpha+\tau\gamma)(Y^{(\alpha+\tau\gamma)(\mathbf{0})}w)-(\alpha+\tau\gamma)(\mathbf{0})

(as in (
50
)) and

b_{\tau}(w):=\gamma(Y^{(\alpha+\tau\gamma)(\mathbf{0})}w).

By Lemma
2.2
, these are translates of
\alpha
and
\gamma
in the sense that
a_{\tau}(\mathbf{0})=0
,

\Gamma_{a_{\tau}}=Y^{-(\alpha+\tau\gamma)(\mathbf{0})}\Gamma_{\alpha+\tau\gamma},

and

\Gamma_{a_{\tau}+tb_{\tau}}=Y^{-(\alpha+\tau\gamma)(\mathbf{0})}\Gamma_{\alpha+(\tau+t)\gamma}.

By the left-invariance of the Riesz transform, for any
\tau,t\in\mathbb{R}
,

G_{\alpha,\gamma}(\tau+t)(\mathbf{0})=G_{a_{\tau},b_{\tau}}(t)(\mathbf{0})=F_{a_{\tau+t}}(\mathbf{0})=\pv(\mathbf{0})\int_{V_{0}}\widehat{K}(\Psi_{a_{\tau+t}}(v))\,\mathrm{d}v.

(104)

We will use (
104
) to decompose
G_{\alpha,\gamma}(\tau)(\mathbf{0})
and differentiate the decomposition.
We fix some
\tau\in[0,1]
and abbreviate
a=a_{\tau}
and
b=b_{\tau}
. For
w\in\mathbb{H}
, let
\overline{w}=\Psi_{a}(w)
and
w_{t}=Y^{tb(\mathbf{0})}wY^{-tb(\mathbf{0})}=wZ^{-b(\mathbf{0})x(w)}
. Then

\Psi_{a_{\tau+t}}(w)=Y^{-tb(\mathbf{0})}\Psi_{a+tb}(w_{t})=Y^{-tb(\mathbf{0})}\overline{w_{t}}Y^{tb(w_{t})}.

(105)

For
0<r<R
, let
D_{r}=B(\mathbf{0},r)\cap V_{0}
and
A_{r,R}=D_{R}\setminus D_{r}
. Then we can decompose
G_{\alpha,\gamma}(\tau+t)(\mathbf{0})=G_{a,b}(t)(\mathbf{0})
as follows:

\displaystyle G_{a,b}(t)(\mathbf{0})

\displaystyle=\pv(\mathbf{0})\int_{D_{1}}\widehat{K}(\Psi_{a_{\tau+t}}(v))\,\mathrm{d}v+\pv(\mathbf{0})\int_{V_{0}\setminus D_{1}}\widehat{K}(\Psi_{a_{\tau+t}}(v))\,\mathrm{d}v

\displaystyle=\pv(\mathbf{0})\int_{D_{1}}\widehat{K}(\Psi_{a_{\tau+t}}(v))\,\mathrm{d}v+\pv(\mathbf{0})\int_{V_{0}\setminus D_{1}}\widehat{K}(Y^{-tb(\mathbf{0})}\overline{w_{t}}Y^{tb(w_{t})})\,\mathrm{d}w

\displaystyle=\pv(\mathbf{0})\int_{D_{1}}\widehat{K}(\Psi_{a_{\tau+t}}(v))\,\mathrm{d}v+\lim_{R\to\infty}\int_{A_{1,R}^{t}}\widehat{K}(Y^{-tb(\mathbf{0})}\overline{w}Y^{tb(w)})\,\mathrm{d}w

\displaystyle=:G^{\mathrm{sm}}_{\tau}(t)+G^{\mathrm{lg}}_{\tau}(t),

where

A_{1,R}^{t}:=Y^{tb(\mathbf{0})}A_{1,R}Y^{-tb(\mathbf{0})}.

For
0<r<1<R
, let

G^{\mathrm{sm}}_{\tau,r}(t):=\int_{A_{r,1}}\widehat{K}(\Psi_{a_{\tau+t}}(v))\,\mathrm{d}v

G^{\mathrm{lg}}_{\tau,R}(t):=\int_{A_{1,R}^{t}}\widehat{K}(Y^{-tb(\mathbf{0})}\overline{w}Y^{tb(w)})\,\mathrm{d}w.

We will show the following bounds.

Lemma 11.2
.

For any
\tau\in[0,1]
and any
1\leq R<R^{\prime}
,

\left|(G^{\mathrm{lg}}_{\tau,R^{\prime}})^{\prime\prime}(0)-(G^{\mathrm{lg}}_{\tau,R})^{\prime\prime}(0)\right|\lesssim A^{-3}R^{-1}.

(106)

Lemma 11.3
.

For any
\tau\in[0,1]
and any
0<r^{\prime}<r\leq 1

\left|(G^{\mathrm{sm}}_{\tau,r})^{\prime\prime}(0)-(G^{\mathrm{sm}}_{\tau,r^{\prime}})^{\prime\prime}(0)\right|\lesssim A^{-3}r.

Proof of Lemma
11.1
.

These lemmas show that the functions
\tau\mapsto(G^{\mathrm{sm}}_{\tau,r})^{\prime\prime}(0)
and
\tau\mapsto(G^{\mathrm{lg}}_{\tau,R})^{\prime\prime}(0)
are uniformly Cauchy on the interval
\tau\in[0,1]
as
r\to 0
and
R\to\infty
. Let
f^{\mathrm{sm}}(\tau)=\lim_{r\to 0}(G^{\mathrm{sm}}_{\tau,r})^{\prime\prime}(0)
and
f^{\mathrm{lg}}(\tau)=\lim_{R\to\infty}(G^{\mathrm{lg}}_{\tau,R})^{\prime\prime}(0)
. Then
(G^{\mathrm{sm}}_{\tau})^{\prime\prime}(0)=f^{\mathrm{sm}}(\tau)
and
(G^{\mathrm{lg}}_{\tau})^{\prime\prime}(0)=f^{\mathrm{lg}}(\tau)
.
Moreover, as
(G^{\mathrm{sm}}_{\tau,1})^{\prime\prime}(0)=0=(G^{\mathrm{lg}}_{\tau,1})^{\prime\prime}(0)
for all
\tau
,

\displaystyle|(G^{\mathrm{sm}}_{\tau})^{\prime\prime}(0)|

\displaystyle=\lim_{r\to 0}|(G^{\mathrm{sm}}_{\tau,r})^{\prime\prime}(0)-(G^{\mathrm{sm}}_{\tau,1})^{\prime\prime}(0)|\lesssim A^{-3},

\displaystyle|(G^{\mathrm{lg}}_{\tau})^{\prime\prime}(0)|

\displaystyle=\lim_{R\to\infty}|(G^{\mathrm{lg}}_{\tau,R})^{\prime\prime}(0)-(G^{\mathrm{lg}}_{\tau,1})^{\prime\prime}(0)|\lesssim A^{-3}.

Thus

\displaystyle\left|G_{a,b}^{\prime\prime}(\tau)(\mathbf{0})\right|=\left|(G^{\mathrm{lg}}_{\tau})^{\prime\prime}(0)+(G^{\mathrm{sm}}_{\tau})^{\prime\prime}(0)\right|\lesssim A^{-3},

as desired.
∎

Before we prove Lemmas
11.2
and
11.3
, we will need some lemmas. The first proves bounds on the derivatives of
a
and
b
which follow from Lemma
3.12
.

Lemma 11.4
.

There is a
c>0
such that for any
k\leq 3
and any word
F\in\{\nabla_{a},Z\}^{k}

\|Fb\|_{\infty}\leq cA^{-\#\nabla_{a}(F)-1},

(107)

If
F\notin\{\id,\nabla_{\alpha}\}
, then

\|Fa\|_{\infty}\leq cA^{-\#\nabla_{a}(F)-1}.

(108)

Proof.

Let
m=\alpha(\mathbf{0})+\tau\gamma(\mathbf{0})
and let
\lambda\colon\mathbb{H}\to\mathbb{H}
,
\lambda(p)=Y^{m}p
. Then
\nabla_{a}=\lambda^{*}(\nabla_{\alpha+\tau\gamma})
, and by the Chain Rule, if
F\in\{\nabla_{a},Z\}^{k}
, then

Fa(p)=F^{\prime}[\alpha+\tau\gamma-m](\lambda(p)),

where
F^{\prime}
is obtained from
F
by replacing
\nabla_{a}
by
\nabla_{\alpha+\tau\gamma}
.

Let
\hat{\nabla}=A\nabla_{\alpha+\tau\gamma}
. It suffices to prove that for any
k\leq 3
and any
E\in\{\hat{\nabla},Z\}^{k}
,

\|E\gamma\|_{\infty}\leq cA^{-1}

and that if
E\notin\{\id,\hat{\nabla}\}
,

\|E\alpha\|_{\infty}\leq cA^{-1}.

Let
g=-A\tau\gamma
and
\nabla=A\nabla_{\alpha}
so that
\hat{\nabla}=\nabla+gZ
.
Suppose by induction that for any
k\leq d
, we can write any
E\in\{\hat{\nabla},Z\}^{k}
as

E=\sum_{i}D_{i,1}[g]\dots D_{i,k_{i}}[g]\cdot C_{i}

(109)

where
C_{i},D_{i,j}\in\{\nabla,Z\}^{*}
and
\ell(C_{i})+\sum_{j}\ell(D_{i,j})=k
. We call the
C_{i}
’s the
monomials
of
E
. For instance,
Z
is trivially of the form (
109
), we can write
\hat{\nabla}=\nabla+gZ
, and

\hat{\nabla}^{2}=\nabla^{2}+\nabla g\cdot Z+g\cdot\nabla Z+g\cdot Z\nabla+g\cdot Zg\cdot Z+g^{2}\cdot Z^{2}.

By the product rule, if
E
can be written in this form, then so can
ZE
and
\hat{\nabla}E
, and each monomial of
ZE
or
\hat{\nabla}E
is a monomial of
E
or a monomial of
E
with one additional letter.

If
E\not\in\{\id,\hat{\nabla}\}
, then
E
ends in either
Z
,
\hat{\nabla}^{2}
, or
Z\hat{\nabla}
. Since
\id
and
\nabla
are not monomials of
Z
,
\hat{\nabla}^{2}
, or
Z\hat{\nabla}
, they cannot be monomials of
E
.

By Lemma
3.12
, if
k\leq 3
and
C\in\{\nabla,Z\}^{k}
, then
\|C\gamma\|_{\infty}\lesssim A^{-1}
, and if
C\not\in\{\id,\nabla\}
, then
\|C\alpha\|_{\infty}\lesssim A^{-1}
. If
E\in\{\hat{\nabla},Z\}^{k}
is as in (
109
), then

\|E\gamma\|_{\infty}\lesssim\sum_{i}\|D_{i,1}[A\gamma]\|_{\infty}\cdots\|D_{i,k_{i}}[A\gamma]\|_{\infty}\|C_{i}\gamma\|_{\infty}\lesssim A^{-1}.

Moreover, if
E\not\in\{\id,\hat{\nabla}\}
, then
C\not\in\{\id,\nabla\}
for all
i
, so

\|E\alpha\|_{\infty}\lesssim\sum_{i}\|D_{i,1}[A\gamma]\|_{\infty}\cdots\|D_{i,k_{i}}[A\gamma]\|_{\infty}\|C_{i}\alpha\|_{\infty}\lesssim A^{-1}

as well.
∎

As a consequence,
Za
,
b
, and
Zb
are close to even. Recall that for a function
f\colon\mathbb{H}\to\mathbb{R}
, we define the even and odd parts of
f
by

\displaystyle f^{\mathsf{e}}(v)

\displaystyle=\frac{f(v)+f(\theta(v))}{2}

\displaystyle f^{\mathsf{o}}(v)

\displaystyle=\frac{f(v)-f(\theta(v))}{2}

so that
f=f^{\mathsf{e}}+f^{\mathsf{o}}
. Furthermore, if
g\colon\mathbb{H}\to\mathbb{R}
, then

\displaystyle(fg)^{\mathsf{e}}

\displaystyle=[(f^{\mathsf{e}}+f^{\mathsf{o}})(g^{\mathsf{e}}+g^{\mathsf{o}})]^{\mathsf{e}}=f^{\mathsf{e}}g^{\mathsf{e}}+f^{\mathsf{o}}g^{\mathsf{o}}

(110)

\displaystyle(fg)^{\mathsf{o}}

\displaystyle=f^{\mathsf{e}}g^{\mathsf{o}}+f^{\mathsf{o}}g^{\mathsf{e}}.

Lemma 11.5
.

Let
a
be as above and let
m\colon\mathbb{H}\to\mathbb{R}
be a smooth function that is constant on cosets of
\langle Y\rangle
. Let
v\in B(\mathbf{0},\sqrt{\rho})
. Then

|m(v)-m(\theta(v))|\lesssim\|v\|_{\mathrm{Kor}}\|\nabla_{a}m\|_{\infty}+A^{-1}\|v\|_{\mathrm{Kor}}\|Zm\|_{\infty}.

If
\rho>A^{2}
and
m=Za
,
m=b
, or
m=Zb
, then for any
v\in\mathbb{H}
, we have
|m^{\mathsf{e}}(v)|\leq A^{-1}
and

|m^{\mathsf{o}}(v)|=\frac{1}{2}|m(v)-m(\theta(v))|\lesssim A^{-2}\|v\|_{\mathrm{Kor}}.

Proof.

Let
p=\Psi_{\alpha}(\mathbf{0})
and let
\sigma=\nabla_{\alpha}\alpha(\mathbf{0})
. Let
W=X+\sigma Y
and
P=\langle W,Z\rangle
. We first bound the distance from
\Gamma_{a}
to
P
. By Lemma
2.9
and Lemma
3.12
, for
q\in\mathbb{H}
,

|\alpha(q)-\alpha(\mathbf{0})-\sigma x(q)|\lesssim A^{-1}\rho^{-1}d_{\mathrm{Kor}}(p,q)^{2}.

Let
u\in B(\mathbf{0},3\sqrt{\rho})
and let
u^{\prime}=Y^{(\alpha+\tau\gamma)(\mathbf{0})}u
. Then

d_{\mathrm{Kor}}(p,u^{\prime})=d_{\mathrm{Kor}}(Y^{\alpha(\mathbf{0})},Y^{(\alpha+\tau\gamma)(\mathbf{0})}u)\leq|\tau\gamma(\mathbf{0})|+\|u\|_{\mathrm{Kor}}\lesssim A^{-1}+\sqrt{\rho}\lesssim\sqrt{\rho}.

We have

a(u)=\alpha(u^{\prime})-\alpha(\mathbf{0})+\tau(\gamma(u^{\prime})-\gamma(\mathbf{0})),

so

|a(u)-\sigma x(u)|=|\alpha(u^{\prime})-\alpha(\mathbf{0})-\sigma x(u)|+2|\tau|\|\gamma\|_{\infty}\\
\lesssim A^{-1}\rho^{-1}d_{\mathrm{Kor}}(p,u^{\prime})^{2}+A^{-1}\lesssim A^{-1}.

(111)

Recall that for all
q\in\mathbb{H}
, we have
(\nabla_{a})_{q}=X_{q}+(y(q)-a(q))Z_{q}
. If
u\in P
, then
y(u)=\sigma x(u)
, so

W_{u}=(\nabla_{a})_{u}-(\sigma x(u)-a(u))Z_{u}+\sigma Y_{u}.

Let
m\colon\mathbb{H}\to\mathbb{R}
be a smooth function which is constant on cosets of
\langle Y\rangle
. Then
Ym=0
, so for
u\in P
,

Wm(u)=\nabla_{a}m(u)-(\sigma x(u)-a(u))Zm(u).

By (
111
),

|Wm(u)|\lesssim\|\nabla_{a}m\|_{\infty}+A^{-1}\|Zm\|_{\infty}.

(112)

Let
w,z
be such that
\Pi_{P}(v)=W^{w}Z^{z}
; note that
|w|\leq\|v\|_{\mathrm{Kor}}\leq\sqrt{\rho}
, so

\|\Pi_{P}(v)\|_{\mathrm{Kor}}\leq\|v\|_{\mathrm{Kor}}+|y(v)-\sigma w|\leq 3\sqrt{\rho}.

Then, by the Mean Value Theorem and (
112
),

|m(v)-m(\theta(v))|=|m(W^{w}Z^{z})-m(W^{-w}Z^{z})|\lesssim\|v\|_{\mathrm{Kor}}(\|\nabla_{a}m\|_{\infty}+A^{-1}\|Zm\|_{\infty}),

as desired.

Finally, if
m=Za
,
m=b
, or
m=Zb
and
\rho>A^{2}
then
\|m\|_{\infty}\lesssim A^{-1}
and
\|\nabla_{a}m\|_{\infty}+A^{-1}\|Zm\|_{\infty}\lesssim A^{-2}
. Therefore,

|m(v)-m(\theta(v))|\lesssim A^{-2}\|v\|_{\mathrm{Kor}}

for all
v\in B(\mathbf{0},A)
and

|m(v)-m(\theta(v))|\lesssim\|m\|_{\infty}\lesssim A^{-2}\|v\|_{\mathrm{Kor}}

for all
v\not\in B(\mathbf{0},A)
.
∎

Finally, we bound functions of the form
N_{T}(v):=T(\overline{v})
when
T
is a homogeneous kernel.

Lemma 11.6
.

Let
v\in\mathbb{H}
,
v\neq\mathbf{0}
. Let
T
be a smooth
k
–homogeneous kernel on
\mathbb{H}
. Then
|N_{T}(v)|\lesssim_{T}\|v\|_{\mathrm{Kor}}^{k}
and

|N_{T}(\theta(v))-(-1)^{k}N_{T}(v)|\lesssim_{T}A^{-1}\|v\|_{\mathrm{Kor}}^{k+1}.

Proof.

Let
\lambda(v)=\nabla_{a}a(\mathbf{0})\cdot x(v)
be the function whose graph is the vertical plane tangent to
\Gamma_{a}
at
\mathbf{0}
. By Lemma
11.4
and Lemma
2.9
,

a(v)=\lambda(v)+O(A^{-1}\|v\|_{\mathrm{Kor}}^{2})
, and
|a(v)+a(\theta(v))|\lesssim A^{-1}\|v\|_{\mathrm{Kor}}^{2}
.

As in the proof of Lemma
5.2
,

\theta(\overline{v})=\overline{\theta(v)}Y^{-a(v)-a(\theta(v))},

and any point
w
on the segment from
\theta(\overline{v})
to
\overline{\theta(v)}
satisfies
\|w\|_{\mathrm{Kor}}\approx\|v\|_{\mathrm{Kor}}
. The mean value theorem and the
(k-1)
–homogeneity of
Y_{\mathsf{L}}T
imply that

|N_{T}(\theta(v))-(-1)^{k}N_{T}(v)|=\big|T(\overline{\theta(v)})-T(\theta(\overline{v}))\big|\\
\lesssim_{T}|a(v)+a(\theta(v))|\|v\|_{\mathrm{Kor}}^{k-1}\lesssim A^{-1}\|v\|_{\mathrm{Kor}}^{k+1},

as desired.
∎

Now we prove Lemma
11.2
.

Proof of Lemma
11.2
.

As above, we let
a=a_{\tau}
and
b=b_{\tau}
. Let

\phi_{t}(w)=Y^{-tb(\mathbf{0})}\overline{w}Y^{tb(w)},

so that
G^{\mathrm{lg}}_{\tau,R}(t)=\int_{A^{t}_{1,R}}\widehat{K}(\phi_{t}(w))\,\mathrm{d}w.

As in the proof of Lemma
7.2
, we define
M_{r}(x)=\frac{1}{4}\sqrt{r^{4}-x^{4}}
and

A_{r,R}(x,t)=[-M_{R}(x)-tb(\mathbf{0})x,M_{R}(x)-tb(\mathbf{0})x]\\
\setminus(-M_{r}(x)-tb(\mathbf{0})x,M_{r}(x)-tb(\mathbf{0})x)

so that
A_{r,R}^{t}=\{(x,0,z):z\in A_{r,R}(x,t)\}
and

\displaystyle G^{\mathrm{lg}}_{\tau,R}(t)=\int_{-R}^{R}\int_{A_{1,R}(x,0,t)}\widehat{K}(\phi_{t}(x,0,z))\,\mathrm{d}z\,\mathrm{d}x.

Taking the derivative with respect to
t
gives

\displaystyle\left(G^{\mathrm{lg}}_{\tau,R}\right)^{\prime}(t)

\displaystyle=\int_{-R}^{R}\int_{A_{1,R}(x,t)}\partial_{t}\left[\widehat{K}(\phi_{t}(x,0,z))\right]\,\mathrm{d}z\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\qquad-\int_{-R}^{R}b(\mathbf{0})x\widehat{K}(\phi_{t}(x,0,u))\big|_{u=-M_{R}(x)-b(\mathbf{0})tx}^{M_{R}(x)-b(\mathbf{0})tx}\,\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\qquad+\int_{-1}^{1}b(\mathbf{0})x\widehat{K}(\phi_{t}(x,0,u))\big|_{u=-M_{1}(x)-b(\mathbf{0})tx}^{M_{1}(x)-b(\mathbf{0})tx}\,\mathrm{d}x=:J_{1}^{R}-J_{2}^{R}+J_{2}^{1},

where
f(u)\big|_{u=a}^{b}
or
[f(u)]_{u=a}^{b}
denotes
f(b)-f(a)
. We have

\frac{\partial J_{1}^{R}}{\partial t}\bigg|_{t=0}=\int_{A_{1,R}}\partial_{t}^{2}\left[\widehat{K}(\phi_{t}(w))\right]_{t=0}\,\mathrm{d}w\\
-\left[\int_{-r}^{r}b(\mathbf{0})x\partial_{t}\left[\widehat{K}(\phi_{t}(x,0,u))\right]_{t=0}\big|_{u=-M_{r}(x)}^{M_{r}(x)}\,\mathrm{d}x\right]_{r=1}^{R}=:I_{1}^{R}-I^{R}_{2}+I^{1}_{2}.

By (
105
), if
w=(x,0,M)\in V_{0}
, then

\phi_{t}(x,0,M-b(\mathbf{0})tx)=\phi_{t}(w_{t})=\Psi_{a_{\tau+t}}(w),

so

J_{2}^{r}=\int_{-r}^{r}b(\mathbf{0})x\widehat{K}(\Psi_{a_{\tau+t}}(x,0,z))\big|_{z=-M_{r}(x)}^{M_{r}(x)}\,\mathrm{d}x,

and

\frac{\partial J_{2}^{r}}{\partial t}\bigg|_{t=0}=\int_{-r}^{r}b(\mathbf{0})x\partial_{t}\left[\widehat{K}(\Psi_{a_{\tau+t}}(x,0,z))\right]_{t=0}\big|_{z=-M_{r}(x)}^{M_{r}(x)}\,\mathrm{d}x=:I_{3}^{r}.

Then

\left(G^{\mathrm{lg}}_{\tau,R}\right)^{\prime\prime}(0)=I_{1}^{R}-I^{R}_{2}+I^{1}_{2}+I_{3}^{R}-I_{3}^{1}.

To prove (
106
), it suffices to show that
|I_{j}^{R}-I_{j}^{R^{\prime}}|\lesssim A^{-3}R^{-1}
for all
1\leq R<R^{\prime}
.

The following calculations will be helpful. Let
w\in V_{0}
. We have
\phi_{t}(w)=Y^{-tb(\mathbf{0})}wY^{a(w)+tb(w)}
and
\phi_{0}(w)=\overline{w}
, so

\displaystyle\partial_{t}[\widehat{K}(\phi_{t}(w))]=-b(\mathbf{0})Y_{\mathsf{R}}\widehat{K}(\phi_{t}(w))+b(w)Y_{\mathsf{L}}\widehat{K}(\phi_{t}(w)).

(113)

Taking a second derivative gives

\partial_{t}^{2}[\widehat{K}(\phi_{t}(w))]_{t=0}=b(\mathbf{0})^{2}Y_{\mathsf{R}}^{2}\widehat{K}(\overline{w})-b(\mathbf{0})b(w)(Y_{\mathsf{R}}Y_{\mathsf{L}}+Y_{\mathsf{L}}Y_{\mathsf{R}})\widehat{K}(\overline{w})\\
\qquad+b(w)^{2}Y_{\mathsf{L}}^{2}\widehat{K}(\overline{w}).

(114)

By (
105
),

a_{\tau+t}(w)=a(w_{t})+tb(w_{t})-tb(\mathbf{0}),

so

\partial_{t}[a_{\tau+t}(w)]=-b(\mathbf{0})x\partial_{z}[a+tb](w_{t})+b(w_{t})-b(\mathbf{0})

(115)

and

\partial_{t}[\widehat{K}(\Psi_{a_{\tau+t}}(w)]]_{t=0}=Y_{\mathsf{L}}\widehat{K}(\overline{w})(b(w)-b(\mathbf{0})-b(\mathbf{0})x\partial_{z}a(w)).

(116)

Bounding
I_{1}^{R}
: By (
114
), we have that

\displaystyle I_{1}^{R}

\displaystyle=\int_{A_{1,R}}b(\mathbf{0})^{2}Y_{\mathsf{R}}^{2}\widehat{K}(\overline{w})\,\mathrm{d}w+\int_{A_{1,R}}b(w)^{2}Y_{\mathsf{L}}^{2}\widehat{K}(\overline{w})\,\mathrm{d}w

\displaystyle\qquad-\int_{A_{1,R}}b(\mathbf{0})b(w)(Y_{\mathsf{R}}Y_{\mathsf{L}}+Y_{\mathsf{L}}Y_{\mathsf{R}})\widehat{K}(\overline{w})\,\mathrm{d}w

\displaystyle=\sum_{i}\int_{A_{1,R}}b_{i}(w)N_{T_{i}}(w)\,\mathrm{d}w,

where
b_{1}(w)=b(\mathbf{0})^{2}
,
b_{2}(w)=b(w)^{2}
,
b_{3}(w)=b(\mathbf{0})b(w)
, and the
T_{i}
’s are smooth
(-5)
–homogeneous kernels. By (
110
) and Lemma
11.5
, for any
w\in\mathbb{H}
, we have

\displaystyle|b^{\mathsf{e}}(w)|

\displaystyle\leq\|b\|_{\infty}\lesssim A^{-1}

\displaystyle|b^{\mathsf{o}}(w)|

\displaystyle\lesssim A^{-2}\|w\|_{\mathrm{Kor}}

\displaystyle|b_{i}^{\mathsf{e}}(w)|

\displaystyle\leq\|b\|_{\infty}^{2}\lesssim A^{-2}

\displaystyle|b_{i}^{\mathsf{o}}(w)|

\displaystyle\lesssim\|b\|_{\infty}|b^{\mathsf{o}}(w)|\lesssim A^{-3}\|w\|_{\mathrm{Kor}}.

By Lemma
11.6
,

\displaystyle|N_{T_{i}}^{\mathsf{e}}(w)|

\displaystyle\leq A^{-1}\|w\|_{\mathrm{Kor}}^{-4}

\displaystyle|N_{T_{i}}^{\mathsf{o}}(w)|

\displaystyle\lesssim\|w\|_{\mathrm{Kor}}^{-5}.

Therefore, by the symmetry of
A_{1,R}
and Lemma
5.3
,

|I_{1,R}|\leq\sum_{i}\left|\int_{A_{1,R}}(b_{i}N_{T_{i}})^{\mathsf{e}}\,\mathrm{d}w\right|=\sum_{i}\left|\int_{A_{1,R}}b_{i}^{\mathsf{e}}N_{T_{i}}^{\mathsf{e}}+b_{i}^{\mathsf{o}}N_{T_{i}}^{\mathsf{o}}\,\mathrm{d}w\right|\\
\lesssim\int_{A_{1,R}}A^{-3}\|w\|_{\mathrm{Kor}}^{-4}\,\mathrm{d}w\lesssim\int_{1}^{R}A^{-3}\kappa^{-2}\,\mathrm{d}\kappa\lesssim A^{-3}R^{-1}.

Bounding
I_{2}^{r}
: Let
w_{+}(x)=(x,0,M_{r}(x))
and
w_{-}(x)=(x,0,M_{r}(x))
. By (
113
), we have
I_{2}^{r}=\int_{-r}^{r}h_{+}(x)-h_{-}(x)\,\mathrm{d}x
, where

h_{\pm}(x)=b(\mathbf{0})x\big(bN_{Y_{\mathsf{L}}\widehat{K}}-b(\mathbf{0})N_{Y_{\mathsf{R}}\widehat{K}}\big)(w_{\pm}(x))

h_{\pm}(x)=b(\mathbf{0})x\big(b(w_{\pm}(x))N_{Y_{\mathsf{L}}\widehat{K}}(w_{\pm}(x))-b(\mathbf{0})N_{Y_{\mathsf{R}}\widehat{K}}(w_{\pm}(x))\big)

Let
h^{\mathsf{e}}_{\pm}(x)=\frac{1}{2}(h_{\pm}(x)+h_{\pm}(-x))
; then
I_{2}^{r}=\int_{-r}^{r}h^{\mathsf{e}}(x)\,\mathrm{d}x
. Since
\|w_{\pm}(x)\|_{\mathrm{Kor}}=r
,

\displaystyle|h_{\pm}^{\mathsf{e}}(x)|

\displaystyle=\left|b(\mathbf{0})x\big(bN_{Y_{\mathsf{L}}\widehat{K}}-b(\mathbf{0})N_{Y_{\mathsf{R}}\widehat{K}}\big)^{\mathsf{o}}(w_{\pm}(x))\right|

\displaystyle=\left|b(\mathbf{0})x\big(b^{\mathsf{e}}N^{\mathsf{o}}_{Y_{\mathsf{L}}\widehat{K}}+b^{\mathsf{o}}N^{\mathsf{e}}_{Y_{\mathsf{L}}\widehat{K}}-b(\mathbf{0})N^{\mathsf{o}}_{Y_{\mathsf{R}}\widehat{K}}\big)(w_{\pm}(x))\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim A^{-1}r\left(A^{-1}\cdot A^{-1}r^{-3}+A^{-2}r\cdot r^{-4}+A^{-1}\cdot A^{-1}r^{-3}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim A^{-3}r^{-2},

and
|I_{2}^{r}|\lesssim A^{-3}r^{-1}
.

Bounding
I_{3}^{r}
:
By (
116
),
I_{3}^{r}=\int_{-r}^{r}k_{+}(x)-k_{-}(x)+l_{+}(x)-l_{-}(x)\,\mathrm{d}x
, where

\displaystyle k_{\pm}(x)

\displaystyle=b(\mathbf{0})xN_{Y_{\mathsf{L}}\widehat{K}}(w_{\pm}(x))\big(b(w_{\pm}(x))-b(\mathbf{0})\big)

\displaystyle l_{\pm}(x)

\displaystyle=b(\mathbf{0})^{2}x^{2}\partial_{z}a(w_{\pm}(x))N_{Y_{\mathsf{L}}\widehat{K}}(w_{\pm}(x))

Since
\|w_{\pm}(x)\|_{\mathrm{Kor}}=r\geq 1
and
Y_{\mathsf{L}}\widehat{K}
is
(-4)
–homogeneous,

|l_{\pm}(x)|\lesssim A^{-2}r^{2}A^{-1}r^{-4}=A^{-3}r^{-2}.

By Lemma
11.5
, for any
w\in\mathbb{H}
, we have
|(\partial_{z}a)^{\mathsf{e}}(w)|\lesssim A^{-1}
and
|\partial_{z}a^{\mathsf{o}}(w)|\lesssim A^{-2}\|w\|_{\mathrm{Kor}}
.
Therefore,

k^{\mathsf{e}}_{\pm}(x)=b(\mathbf{0})x\left(N^{\mathsf{e}}_{Y_{\mathsf{L}}\widehat{K}}(w_{\pm}(x))b^{\mathsf{o}}(w_{\pm}(x))+N^{\mathsf{o}}_{Y_{\mathsf{L}}\widehat{K}}(w_{\pm}(x))\big(b^{\mathsf{e}}(w_{\pm}(x))-b(\mathbf{0})\big)\right)

and

|k^{\mathsf{o}}_{\pm}(x)|\lesssim A^{-1}r\cdot r^{-4}\cdot A^{-2}r+A^{-1}r\cdot A^{-1}r^{-3}\cdot A^{-1}\lesssim A^{-3}r^{-2}.

Therefore,

|I_{3}^{r}|=\left|\int_{-r}^{r}k^{\mathsf{e}}_{+}(x)-k^{\mathsf{e}}_{-}(x)+l_{+}(x)-l_{-}(x)\,\mathrm{d}x\right|\lesssim A^{-3}r^{-1}.

Thus, for any
R^{\prime}>R\geq 1
, we have

\left|\left(G^{\mathrm{lg}}_{\tau,R^{\prime}}\right)^{\prime\prime}(0)-\left(G^{\mathrm{lg}}_{\tau,R}\right)^{\prime\prime}(0)\right|\leq\left|I_{1}^{R^{\prime}}-I_{1}^{R}\right|+\left|I_{2}^{R^{\prime}}-I_{2}^{R}\right|+\left|I_{3}^{R^{\prime}}-I_{3}^{R}\right|\lesssim A^{-3}R^{-1}.

This proves the lemma.
∎

Proof of Lemma
11.3
.

Let
0<r<1
and recall that

G^{\mathrm{sm}}_{\tau,r}(t):=\int_{A_{r,1}}\widehat{K}(\Psi_{a_{\tau+t}}(v))\,\mathrm{d}v.

We claim that

\left|(G^{\mathrm{sm}}_{\tau,r})^{\prime\prime}(0)-(G^{\mathrm{sm}}_{\tau,r^{\prime}})^{\prime\prime}(0)\right|\lesssim A^{-3}r

for all
0<r^{\prime}<r<1
.

As above, we abbreviate
a=a_{\tau}
and
b=b_{\tau}
. Recall that
a(\mathbf{0})=0
.
For
v=(x,y,z)
, let

q_{1}(v):=\partial_{t}[a_{\tau+t}(v)]_{t=0}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:pt-a-tau-t}}}{{=}}-b(\mathbf{0})x\partial_{z}a(v)+b(v)-b(\mathbf{0})

and

q_{2}(v):=\partial^{2}_{t}[a_{\tau+t}(v)]_{t=0}=b(\mathbf{0})^{2}x^{2}\partial^{2}_{z}a(v)-2b(\mathbf{0})x\partial_{z}b(v).

Then

\frac{\,\mathrm{d}^{2}}{\,\mathrm{d}t^{2}}\widehat{K}(vY^{a_{\tau+t}(v)})\Big|_{t=0}=\frac{\,\mathrm{d}}{\,\mathrm{d}t}\partial_{t}[a_{\tau+t}(v)]Y_{\mathsf{L}}\widehat{K}(vY^{a_{\tau+t}(v)})\Big|_{t=0}\\
=\partial^{2}_{t}[a_{\tau+t}(v)]_{t=0}Y_{\mathsf{L}}\widehat{K}(\overline{v})+\big(\partial_{t}[a_{\tau+t}(v)]_{t=0}\big)^{2}Y_{\mathsf{L}}^{2}\widehat{K}(\overline{v}),

and

(G^{\mathrm{sm}}_{\tau,r})^{\prime\prime}(0)=\int_{A_{r,1}}q_{2}(v)N_{Y_{\mathsf{L}}\widehat{K}}(v)+q_{1}^{2}(v)N_{Y_{\mathsf{L}}^{2}\widehat{K}}(v)\,\mathrm{d}v.

As above, we decompose these into odd and even terms. Let
b_{0}(v)=b(v)-b(\mathbf{0})
and let
\kappa=\|v\|_{\mathrm{Kor}}
. By Lemma
11.4
and Lemma
2.9
,

b_{0}(v)=x(v)\nabla_{a}b(\mathbf{0})+O(A^{-1}\kappa^{2})=O(A^{-2}\kappa+A^{-1}\kappa^{2})=O(A^{-1}\kappa),

(117)

so
|b_{0}^{\mathsf{e}}(v)|\lesssim A^{-1}\kappa^{2}
and
|b_{0}^{\mathsf{o}}(v)|\lesssim A^{-1}\kappa
. Therefore, by Lemma
11.5
,

|q_{1}^{\mathsf{e}}(v)|\leq b(\mathbf{0})x|\partial_{z}a^{\mathsf{o}}(v)|+|b_{0}^{\mathsf{e}}(v)|\lesssim A^{-1}\kappa\cdot A^{-2}\kappa+A^{-1}\kappa^{2}\lesssim A^{-1}\kappa^{2}

and

|q_{1}^{\mathsf{o}}(v)|\leq b(\mathbf{0})x|\partial_{z}a^{\mathsf{e}}(v)|+|b_{0}^{\mathsf{o}}(v)|\lesssim A^{-1}\kappa\cdot A^{-1}+A^{-2}\kappa\lesssim A^{-2}\kappa.

Therefore,
|(q_{1}^{2})^{\mathsf{o}}(v)|=2|q_{1}^{\mathsf{e}}(v)q_{1}^{\mathsf{o}}(v)|\lesssim A^{-3}\kappa^{3}
and

|(q_{1}^{2})^{\mathsf{e}}(v)|=|q_{1}^{\mathsf{e}}(v)^{2}+q_{1}^{\mathsf{o}}(v)^{2}|\lesssim A^{-2}\kappa^{2}.

For
q_{2}
, on one hand,

|q_{2}(v)|\leq b(\mathbf{0})^{2}x^{2}\cdot\|\partial^{2}_{z}a\|_{\infty}+2|b(\mathbf{0})x|\cdot\|\partial_{z}b\|_{\infty}\lesssim A^{-2}\kappa^{2}\cdot A^{-1}+A^{-1}\kappa\cdot A^{-1}\lesssim A^{-2}\kappa,

so
|q_{2}^{\mathsf{o}}(v)|\lesssim A^{-2}\kappa
. On the other hand, by Lemma
11.5
,

|q_{2}^{\mathsf{e}}(v)|\leq b(\mathbf{0})^{2}x^{2}\|\partial^{2}_{z}a\|_{\infty}+|2b(\mathbf{0})x(\partial_{z}b)^{\mathsf{o}}(v)|\lesssim A^{-2}\kappa^{2}\cdot A^{-1}+A^{-1}\kappa\cdot A^{-2}\kappa\lesssim A^{-3}\kappa^{2}.

Since
Y_{\mathsf{L}}\widehat{K}
is
(-4)
–homogeneous and
Y_{\mathsf{L}}^{2}\widehat{K}
is
(-5)
–homogeneous, we can use Lemma
11.6
to bound
N_{Y_{\mathsf{L}}\widehat{K}}
and
N_{Y_{\mathsf{L}}^{2}\widehat{K}}
. Thus

\displaystyle|(G^{\mathrm{sm}}_{\tau,r})^{\prime\prime}(0)

\displaystyle-(G^{\mathrm{sm}}_{\tau,r^{\prime}})^{\prime\prime}(0)|=\left|\int_{A_{r^{\prime},r}}(q_{2}N_{Y_{\mathsf{L}}\widehat{K}})^{\mathsf{e}}+(q_{1}^{2}N_{Y_{\mathsf{L}}^{2}\widehat{K}})^{\mathsf{e}}\,\mathrm{d}v\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left|\int_{A_{r^{\prime},r}}q_{2}^{\mathsf{e}}N_{Y_{\mathsf{L}}\widehat{K}}^{\mathsf{e}}+q_{2}^{\mathsf{o}}N_{Y_{\mathsf{L}}\widehat{K}}^{\mathsf{o}}+(q_{1}^{2})^{\mathsf{e}}N_{Y_{\mathsf{L}}^{2}\widehat{K}}^{\mathsf{e}}+(q_{1}^{2})^{\mathsf{o}}N_{Y_{\mathsf{L}}^{2}\widehat{K}}^{\mathsf{o}}\,\mathrm{d}v\right|

\displaystyle\leq\int_{A_{r^{\prime},r}}A^{-3}\kappa^{2}\cdot\kappa^{-4}+A^{-2}\kappa\cdot A^{-1}\kappa^{-3}+A^{-2}\kappa^{2}\cdot A^{-1}\kappa^{-4}+A^{-3}\kappa^{3}\cdot\kappa^{-5}\,\mathrm{d}v

\displaystyle\approx\int_{r^{\prime}}^{r}A^{-3}\kappa^{-2}\cdot\kappa^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\kappa\leq A^{-3}r,

where we used Lemma
5.3
to replace
\,\mathrm{d}v
by
\kappa^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\kappa
. This proves the lemma.
∎

12.
Proof of Theorem
1.1

In this section we will finally finish the proof of Theorem
1.1
. First, recall that for any intrinsic Lipschitz function
\phi\colon\mathbb{H}\to\mathbb{R}
and any function
g\colon\mathbb{H}\to\mathbb{R}
which is constant on cosets of
\langle Y\rangle
, we have defined a parametric version of the Riesz transform by

\mathsf{Rsz}_{\phi}g(p):=\tilde{T}^{\mathsf{R}}_{\phi}g(p)=\pv(\Psi_{\phi}(p))\int_{\Psi_{\phi}(p)V_{0}}\widehat{\mathsf{R}}(\Psi_{\phi}(p)^{-1}\Psi_{\phi}(v))g(v)\,\mathrm{d}v.

Note that when
\phi
is smooth and bounded and
p\in\Gamma_{\phi}
, Proposition
5.1
implies that

\mathsf{Rsz}_{\phi}g=T^{\mathsf{R}}[g\,\mathrm{d}\eta_{\phi}].

In order to bound the
L_{2}
norm of
\mathsf{Rsz}
on
\Gamma_{\phi_{A}}
(where
\phi_{A}
is as in Proposition
1.3
) we will need the following lemma, which allows us to replace
\mathbf{1}
by an
L_{2}
function.

Lemma 12.1
.

Let
\phi
satisfy the hypotheses of Proposition
5.1
. Let
E\subset F
be two bounded subsets of
V_{0}
so that
d(E,F^{c})>0
. Then there is a
C>0
depending only on
\phi
,
E
, and
F
such that for every
p\in\Psi_{\phi}(E)
, the principal value
\tilde{T}_{\phi}\mathbf{1}_{F\langle Y\rangle}(p)
exists and satisfies

\left|\tilde{T}_{\phi}\mathbf{1}_{F\langle Y\rangle}(p)-\tilde{T}_{\phi}\mathbf{1}(p)\right|<C.

Proof.

Since we know
\tilde{T}_{\phi}\mathbf{1}(p)
exists by Lemma
5.2
, it suffices to show that there is a
C>0
such that for all
p\in E
,
\tilde{T}_{\phi}[\mathbf{1}_{F\langle Y\rangle}-\mathbf{1}](p)
exists and

\left|\tilde{T}_{\phi}\left[\mathbf{1}-\mathbf{1}_{F\langle Y\rangle}\right](p)\right|=\left|\tilde{T}_{\phi}\mathbf{1}_{\mathbb{H}\setminus F\langle Y\rangle}(p)\right|<C.

Let
\chi=\mathbf{1}_{\mathbb{H}\setminus F\langle Y\rangle}
.
By compactness and the boundedness of
F
, there is an
0<\epsilon<1
such that for all
p\in E
we have

\Pi(B_{\epsilon}(p)\cap pV_{0})\subset\Pi(F)\subset\Pi(B_{\epsilon^{-1}}(p)\cap pV_{0}).

Therefore, for
r<\epsilon
and
R>\epsilon^{-1}
, we have
\chi=0
on
A_{r,\epsilon}(p)
and
\chi=1
on
A_{\epsilon^{-1},R}(p)
, so

\tilde{T}_{\phi}[\chi](p)=\lim_{\begin{subarray}{c}r\to 0\\
R\to\infty\end{subarray}}\tilde{T}_{\phi;r,R}[\chi](p)=\tilde{T}_{\phi;\epsilon,\epsilon^{-1}}[\chi](p)+\lim_{R\to\infty}\tilde{T}_{\phi;\epsilon^{-1},R}[\mathbf{1}](p).

By Lemma
5.2
, this limit exists and satisfies
|\tilde{T}_{\phi}[\chi](p)|\lesssim\log\epsilon+\epsilon
for all
p\in E
. This proves the lemma.
∎

Given a set
E\subseteq\mathbb{H}
, we define the
L_{2}
norm
\|\cdot\|_{E}:=\|\cdot\|_{L_{2}(\mu|_{E})}
.

Let
W=[-1,2]\times\{0\}\times[-1,2]\subset V_{0}
and let
\overline{W}=W\langle Y\rangle
. Then
U
and
W
satisfy Lemma
12.1
, so Proposition
1.3
implies that

\|\mathsf{Rsz}_{\phi_{A}}{\bf 1}_{\overline{W}}\|_{U}\geq\|\mathsf{Rsz}_{\phi_{A}}{\bf 1}\|_{U}-\|\mathsf{Rsz}_{\phi_{A}}{\bf 1}-\mathsf{Rsz}_{\phi_{A}}{\bf 1}_{\overline{W}}\|_{U}\geq cA-C\gtrsim A

(118)

when
A
is sufficiently large, and thus that

\|\mathsf{Rsz}_{\phi_{A}}{\bf 1}_{\overline{W}}\|_{L_{2}(\eta_{\phi_{A}})}\geq\|\mathsf{Rsz}_{\phi_{A}}{\bf 1}_{\overline{W}}\|_{U}\gtrsim A.

Since
{\bf 1}_{\overline{W}}\in L_{2}(\eta_{\phi_{A}})
, the operator norm of
\mathsf{Rsz}_{\phi_{A}}
on
L_{2}(\eta_{\phi})
goes to infinity with
A
. By gluing together graphs with different values of
A
, we can construct a single intrinsic Lipschitz function
\phi
such that
\mathsf{Rsz}_{\phi}
is unbounded on
L_{2}(\eta_{\phi})
.
