Proof of Theorem
1.1
.

For
x\in V_{0}
,
r>0
, let
\tau_{x,r}\colon\mathbb{H}\to\mathbb{H}
be the affine transform
\tau_{x,r}(v)=xs_{r}(v)
. Note that
\tau_{x,r}(V_{0})=V_{0}
.
Let
x_{1},x_{2},\dots\in V_{0}
and let
r_{1},r_{2},\dots>0
so that the subsets
W_{n}=\tau_{x_{n},r_{n}}(W)
are disjoint subsets of
W
. Let
U_{n}=\tau_{x_{n},r_{n}}(U)
and let
\overline{W}_{n}=W_{n}\langle Y\rangle
.

Let
\phi_{n}
be as in Proposition
1.3
and let

\hat{\phi}_{n}(u)=r_{n}\phi_{n}(\tau_{x_{n},r_{n}}^{-1}(u))

so that
\Gamma_{\hat{\phi}_{n}}=\tau_{x_{n},r_{n}}(\Gamma_{\phi_{n}})
. Note that
\hat{\phi}_{n}(v)=0
for
v\not\in U_{n}\langle Y\rangle
.
Let
f\colon V_{0}\to\mathbb{R}
be the function

f(v)=\begin{cases}\hat{\phi}_{n}(v)&v\in U_{n}\langle Y\rangle\\
0&\text{otherwise.}\end{cases}

Then
f
is an intrinsic Lipschitz function supported in
\overline{W}
.

Since
f|_{\overline{W}_{n}}=\hat{\phi}_{n}|_{\overline{W}_{n}}
, we have
\mathsf{Rsz}_{f}{\bf 1}_{\overline{W}_{n}}(v)=\mathsf{Rsz}_{\hat{\phi}_{n}}{\bf 1}_{\overline{W}_{n}}(v)
for all
v\in\overline{W}_{n}
. By the translation- and scale-invariance of the Riesz kernel, for all
v\in V_{0}
,

\displaystyle\mathsf{Rsz}_{\hat{\phi}_{n}}\mathbf{1}_{\overline{W}_{n}}(v)=\mathsf{Rsz}_{\phi_{n}}\mathbf{1}_{\overline{W}}(\tau_{x_{n},r_{n}}^{-1}(v)).

(119)

Since
(\tau_{x,r})_{*}(\mu)=r_{n}^{-3}\mu
, this implies

\begin{split}\|\mathsf{Rsz}_{f}{\bf 1}_{\overline{W}_{n}}(v)\|_{L_{2}(\eta_{f})}&\geq\|\mathsf{Rsz}_{f}{\bf 1}_{\overline{W}_{n}}(v)\|_{U_{n}}=r_{n}^{-\frac{3}{2}}\|\mathsf{Rsz}_{\phi_{n}}{\bf 1}_{\overline{W}}(v)\|_{U}\\
&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{eq:set-pv-bound}}}{{\gtrsim}}r_{n}^{-\frac{3}{2}}n\approx n\|{\bf 1}_{\overline{W}_{n}}(v)\|_{L_{2}(\eta_{f})}\end{split}

for all sufficiently large
n
. Thus,
\mathsf{Rsz}_{f}
is unbounded on
L_{2}(\eta_{f})
.
∎

Appendix A
Intrinsic Sobolev spaces and derivative bounds on
\Gamma_{f_{i}}

In this section, we bound the derivatives of the functions constructed in Section
3
.
We first introduce some Sobolev spaces
W_{i,d}
and
W^{\prime}_{i,d}
that consist of functions on
\mathbb{H}
.

Recall that for vector fields
V_{1},\dots,V_{k}
, we let
\{V_{1},\dots,V_{k}\}^{l}
denote the set of words of length
l
and let
\{V_{1},\dots,V_{k}\}^{*}
denote the set of words of any length. We let
\#V_{i}(D)
denote the number of occurrences of
V_{i}
in
D
. For
i\geq 0
, let
r_{i}=A^{-1}\rho^{-i}
, and let
f_{i}
and
\eta_{i}
be as in Section
3
. Let
\partial_{i}=\nabla_{f_{i}}
and let

\displaystyle\hat{Z}_{i}

\displaystyle=r_{i}^{2}Z

\displaystyle\hat{\partial}_{i}

\displaystyle=Ar_{i}\partial_{i}

\displaystyle\hat{\nu}_{i}

\displaystyle=Ar_{i}^{-1}\nu_{i}

\displaystyle\hat{f}_{i}

\displaystyle=Ar_{i}^{-1}f_{i}.

(120)

The first two scaling factors correspond to the width and height of the pseudoquads in the construction of
\nu_{i}
; the third and fourth normalize
\nu_{i}
so that
\|\hat{\nu}_{i}\|_{\infty}\approx 1
.

For any smooth function
g\colon\mathbb{H}\to\mathbb{R}
which is constant on cosets of
\langle Y\rangle
, let

\|g\|_{W_{i,d}}=\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}D\in\{\hat{\partial}_{i},\hat{Z}\}^{*}\\
\ell(D)\leq d\end{subarray}}\|Dg\|_{\infty}

and

\|g\|_{W^{\prime}_{i,d}}=\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}D\in\{\hat{\partial}_{i},\hat{Z}\}^{*}\\
\ell(D)\leq d\\
D\not\in\{\id,\hat{\partial}_{i}\}\end{subarray}}\|Dg\|_{\infty}.

In this section, we will prove the following proposition, which is equivalent to Lemma
3.11
.

Proposition A.1
.

For any
d>0
, if
\rho>1
is sufficiently large, then for all
i
,

\|\nu_{i}\|_{W_{i,d}}\lesssim_{d}A^{-1}r_{i}

and

\|f_{i}\|_{W_{i,d}^{\prime}}\lesssim_{d}A^{-1}\rho^{-1}r_{i}.

Equivalently,
\|\hat{\nu}_{i}\|_{W_{i,d}}\lesssim_{d}1
and
\|\hat{f}_{i}\|_{W_{i,d}}\lesssim_{d}\rho^{-1}
.

The proof of Proposition
A.1
can be broken into two parts. In the first part, we bound
D\nu_{i}
in the case that
D=\hat{Z}_{i}^{k}\hat{\partial}_{i}^{j}
.

Lemma A.2
.

Given
d\geq 2
, there exists
\rho_{0}>0
so that if
\rho\geq\rho_{0}
, then

\displaystyle\|\hat{Z}_{i}^{k}\hat{f}_{i}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq 2\rho^{-1}

\displaystyle\forall i\geq 0,1\leq k\leq d

(121)

\displaystyle\|\hat{Z}_{i}^{k}\hat{\partial}_{i}^{j}\hat{\nu}_{i}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{j}1

\displaystyle\forall i\geq 0,0\leq j\leq d,0\leq k\leq d.

(122)

In the second part, we use Lemma
A.2
as part of an inductive argument. First, we bound
\hat{\nu}_{i}
in terms of
\|\hat{f}_{i}\|_{W^{\prime}_{i,d}}
.

Lemma A.3
.

For any
d>0
, there is a
c_{1}>1
such that for any
i\geq 0
, if
\rho>1
is sufficiently large and
\|\hat{f}_{i}\|_{W^{\prime}_{i,d}}<1
, then

\|\hat{\nu}_{i}\|_{W_{i,d}}\leq c_{1}.

(123)

To bound
\|\hat{f}_{i+1}\|_{W_{i+1,d}^{\prime}}
, we compare
\|\cdot\|_{W_{i+1,d}^{\prime}}
and
\|\cdot\|_{W_{i,d}}
.

Lemma A.4
.

For any
d>0
, if
\rho
is sufficiently large, then for any
i
and any
g\in W_{i,d}^{\prime}
,

\|g\|_{W_{i+1,d}^{\prime}}\lesssim_{d}(1+\|\hat{\nu}_{i}\|_{W_{i,d}})^{d}\rho^{-2}\|g\|_{W_{i,d}^{\prime}}.

(124)

In particular, if
\hat{\nu}_{i}
satisfies (
123
), then there is a
c_{2}>0
depending only on
d
such that
\|g\|_{W_{i+1,d}^{\prime}}\leq c_{2}\rho^{-2}\|g\|_{W_{i,d}^{\prime}}.

Given these lemmas, we prove Proposition
A.1
by induction on
i
.

Proof of Proposition
A.1
.

Let
c_{1}>1
and
c_{2}
be as in Lemma
A.3
and Lemma
A.4
. Let
c_{3}=2c_{1}c_{2}
. We claim that if
\rho
is sufficiently large, then
\|\hat{\nu}_{i}\|_{W_{i,d}}\leq c_{1}
and
\|\hat{f}_{i}\|_{W_{i,d}^{\prime}}\leq c_{3}\rho^{-1}
for all
i\geq 0
. We suppose that
\rho>c_{3}
so that this bound implies
\|\hat{f}_{i}\|_{W_{i,d}^{\prime}}<1
.

We proceed by induction on
i
. When
i=0
, we have
f_{0}=0
, so
\|\hat{f}_{0}\|_{W^{\prime}_{0,d}}=0
. Suppose that
i\geq 0
and
\|\hat{f}_{i}\|_{W_{i,d}^{\prime}}\leq c_{3}\rho^{-1}<1
. Lemma
A.3
implies that
\|\hat{\nu}_{i}\|_{W_{i,d}}\leq c_{1}
. By Lemma
A.4
,

\|\hat{f}_{i+1}\|_{W_{i+1,d}^{\prime}}\leq Ar_{i+1}^{-1}c_{2}\rho^{-2}\|f_{i}+\nu_{i}\|_{W_{i,d}^{\prime}}\\
\leq c_{2}\rho^{-1}(\|\hat{f}_{i}\|_{W_{i,d}^{\prime}}+\|\hat{\nu}_{i}\|_{W_{i,d}})\leq c_{2}c_{3}\rho^{-2}+c_{1}c_{2}\rho^{-1}\leq 2c_{1}c_{2}\rho^{-1}.

That is,
\|\hat{f}_{i+1}\|_{W_{i+1,d}^{\prime}}\leq c_{3}\rho^{-1}
. By induction,
\|\hat{\nu}_{i}\|_{W_{i,d}}\leq c_{1}
and
\|\hat{f}_{i}\|_{W_{i,d}^{\prime}}\leq c_{3}\rho^{-1}
for all
i\geq 0
.
∎

In the following subsections, we will prove these lemmas.

A.1.
Proof of Lemma
A.2

Recall that in Section
3
, we defined
\nu_{i}
using maps
R_{i,j}\colon[0,Ar_{i}]\times[0,r_{i}^{2}]\to Q_{i,j}
for
j=1,\dots,A^{-1}r_{i}^{-3}
and a subset
J_{i}\subset\{1,\dots,A^{-1}r_{i}^{-3}\}
. For each
j\in J_{i}
,
Q_{i,j}
is a pseudoquad for
\Gamma_{f_{i}}
and
R_{i,j}
is the parametrization of
Q_{i,j}
that satisfies
\partial_{s}[R_{i,j}(s,t)]=\nabla_{f_{i}}
and
\partial_{t}[R_{i,j}(0,t)]=Z
. The
Q_{i,j}
’s have disjoint interiors and their union is the unit square
U=[0,1]\times\{0\}\times[0,1]
.

We then define
\nu_{i}
and
f_{i}
by letting
\kappa\colon[0,1]^{2}\to\mathbb{R}
be a bump function,

\kappa_{i,j}(R_{i,j}(s,t))=A^{-1}r_{i}\kappa(A^{-1}r_{i}s,r_{i}^{-2}t),

(125)

\nu_{i}=\sum_{j\in J_{i}}\kappa_{i,j}
, and
f_{i+1}=f_{i}+\nu_{i}
. Let
S_{i}=\bigcup_{j\not\in J_{i}}Q_{i,j}
, so that
f_{i}|_{S_{i}}=f_{i+1}|_{S_{i}}
. Recall that
S_{0}=\emptyset
and that
S_{i}\subset S_{i+1}
for all
i
.

We prove Lemma
A.2
by induction on
i
. We will show that if
(
121
) holds for some
i\geq 0
, then (
122
) holds for
i
and (
121
) holds for
i+1
. Since
\nu_{i}=0
and
f_{i+1}=f_{i}
on
S_{i}
, it suffices to prove that (
122
) and (
121
) hold on
Q_{i,j}
for all
j\in J_{i}
.

First, we restate (
122
) and (
121
) in terms of
flow coordinates
on
Q_{i,j}
. Let
j\in J_{i}
and define coordinates
(s,t)
on
Q_{i,j}
by letting
(s(v),t(v))=R_{i,j}^{-1}(v)
for all
v\in Q_{i,j}
. Then
\frac{\partial}{\partial s}=\nabla_{f_{i}}=\partial_{i}
on
Q_{i,j}
.
We define rescaled coordinate systems on
Q_{i,j}
by
(\hat{s},\hat{t})=(A^{-1}r_{i}^{-1}s,r_{i}^{-2}t)
and
(\hat{x}_{i},\hat{z}_{i})=(A^{-1}r_{i}^{-1}x,r_{i}^{-2}z)
, so that
0\leq\hat{s}\leq 1
and
0\leq\hat{t}\leq 1
on
Q_{i,j}
.
Let
\hat{f}_{i}=Ar_{i}^{-1}f_{i}
and
\hat{\nu}_{i}=Ar_{i}^{-1}\nu_{i}
as in (
120
).
Let
\hat{S}:=\frac{\partial}{\partial\hat{s}}=\hat{\partial}_{i}
and
\hat{T}:=\frac{\partial}{\partial\hat{t}}
.
By (
125
), we have

\hat{\nu}_{i}(R_{i,j}(Ar_{i}\hat{s},r_{i}^{2}\hat{t}))=Ar_{i}^{-1}\kappa_{i,j_{0}}(R_{i,j}(s,t))=\kappa(\hat{s},\hat{t}),

so
\|\hat{T}^{k}\hat{S}^{l}\hat{\nu}_{i}\|_{\infty}=\|\partial_{lk}\kappa\|_{\infty}\lesssim_{k,l}1,

where
\partial_{lk}\kappa
is the appropriate partial derivative of
\kappa
.
With this notation, (
122
) and (
121
) can be stated as

\displaystyle\|\hat{Z}_{i}^{k}\hat{f}_{i}\|_{L_{\infty}(Q_{i,j})}

\displaystyle\leq 2\rho^{-1}

for any
j\in J_{i}
and
1\leq k\leq d
,

(F
i
)

\displaystyle\|\hat{Z}_{i}^{k}\hat{S}^{m}\hat{\nu}_{i}\|_{L_{\infty}(Q_{i,j})}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{k,m}1

for any
j\in J_{i}
,
0\leq k\leq d
, and
0\leq m\leq d
.

(H
i
)

To prove (F
i
) and (H
i
), we will need some bounds from
[
NY20
]
. The bounds in
[
NY20
]
apply to
\psi_{i}
rather than
f_{i}
, but for each
v\in U
, there is an
i^{\prime}\leq i
such that
f_{i}
and
\psi_{i^{\prime}}
agree on a neighborhood of
v
. Therefore, by Lemma 3.10 of
[
NY20
]
,

\left\|\frac{\partial f_{i}}{\partial z}\right\|_{\infty}\leq\max_{i^{\prime}\leq i}\left\|\frac{\partial\psi_{i^{\prime}}}{\partial z}\right\|_{\infty}\leq\max_{i^{\prime}\leq i}2\rho^{i^{\prime}-1}\leq 2\rho^{i-1},

and

\frac{\partial\hat{z}_{i}}{\partial\hat{t}}=\left(\frac{\partial\hat{t}}{\partial\hat{z}_{i}}\right)^{-1}=\frac{\partial z}{\partial t}\in\left[\frac{3}{4},\frac{4}{3}\right].

(126)

Thus

\left\|\hat{Z}_{i}\hat{f}_{i}\right\|_{\infty}=\frac{Ar_{i}^{-1}}{r_{i}^{-2}}\left\|\frac{\partial f_{i}}{\partial z}\right\|_{\infty}\leq 2r_{i}A\rho^{i-1}=2\rho^{-1}.

(127)

Suppose by induction on
i
that (F
i
) holds for some
i\geq 0
. Note that
f_{0}=0
, so (F
0
) holds. For
i\geq 0
and
1\leq d_{0}\leq d
, let (P
{}_{i,d_{0}}
) be the statement

\left\|\frac{\partial^{k}\hat{z}_{i}}{\partial\hat{t}^{k}}\right\|_{\infty}\lesssim\rho^{-1}\text{ for $2\leq k\leq d_{0}$.}

(P
{}_{i,d_{0}}
)

We will show that (F
i
) implies (P
i,d
) and use (P
i,d
) to prove (H
i
) and (F
i+1
). We must be careful to ensure that the implicit constants in (P
i,d
) and (H
i
) are independent of
i
.

Suppose that (P
{}_{i,d_{0}}
) holds for some
1\leq d_{0}<d
; note that (P
i,1
) is vacuous. In
[
NY20
, Lemma 3.10]
, it was calculated that

\frac{\partial z}{\partial t}=\exp\left(-\int_{0}^{s}\frac{\partial f_{i}}{\partial z}\big(R_{i,j}(\sigma,t)\big)\,\mathrm{d}\sigma\right)

(128)

and

\frac{\partial^{2}z}{\partial t^{2}}=-\frac{\partial z}{\partial t}\int_{0}^{s}\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\left[\frac{\partial f_{i}}{\partial z_{i}}\right]\,\mathrm{d}\sigma,

(129)

where the integrand is evaluated at
R_{i,j}(\sigma,t)
. Thus

\frac{\partial^{2}\hat{z}_{i}}{\partial\hat{t}^{2}}=-r_{i}^{2}\frac{\partial\hat{z}_{i}}{\partial\hat{t}}\int_{0}^{\hat{s}}r_{i}^{-2}\frac{\partial}{\partial\hat{t}}\left[\frac{A^{-1}r_{i}}{r_{i}^{2}}\frac{\partial\hat{f}_{i}}{\partial\hat{z}_{i}}\right]Ar_{i}\,\mathrm{d}\hat{\sigma}=-\frac{\partial\hat{z}_{i}}{\partial\hat{t}}\int_{0}^{\hat{s}}\frac{\partial}{\partial\hat{t}}\left[\frac{\partial\hat{f}_{i}}{\partial\hat{z}_{i}}\right]\,\mathrm{d}\hat{\sigma},

(130)

where the integrand is evaluated at
R_{i,j}(Ar_{i}\hat{\sigma},t)
. By the product rule, for
k\geq 2
,

\displaystyle\frac{\partial^{k}\hat{z}_{i}}{\partial\hat{t}^{k}}=-\sum_{j=1}^{k-1}\binom{k-2}{j-1}\frac{\partial^{j}\hat{z}_{i}}{\partial\hat{t}^{j}}\int_{0}^{\hat{s}}\frac{\partial^{k-j}}{\partial\hat{t}^{k-j}}\left[\frac{\partial\hat{f}_{i}}{\partial\hat{z}_{i}}\right]\,\mathrm{d}\hat{\sigma}.

(131)

Since
\frac{\partial}{\partial\hat{t}}=\frac{\partial\hat{z}_{i}}{\partial\hat{t}}\frac{\partial}{\partial\hat{z}_{i}}
, an inductive argument (or the Faà di Bruno formula) shows that there are constants
c_{\mathbf{a},n}
such that

\frac{\partial^{n}}{\partial\hat{t}^{n}}=\left(\frac{\partial\hat{z}_{i}}{\partial\hat{t}}\right)^{n}\frac{\partial^{n}}{\partial\hat{z}_{i}^{n}}+\sum_{j=1}^{n-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{a}\in\mathbb{N}^{j},\\
\|a\|_{1}=n\end{subarray}}c_{\mathbf{a},n}\left(\prod_{\ell=1}^{j}\frac{\partial^{a_{\ell}}\hat{z}_{i}}{\partial\hat{t}^{a_{\ell}}}\right)\frac{\partial^{j}}{\partial\hat{z}_{i}^{j}}.

Suppose that
n\leq d_{0}
. By (P
{}_{i,d_{0}}
) and (
126
),
\frac{\partial^{m}\hat{z}_{i}}{\partial\hat{t}^{m}}\lesssim 1
if
m=1
and
\frac{\partial^{m}\hat{z}_{i}}{\partial\hat{t}^{m}}\lesssim\rho^{-1}
if
2\leq m\leq d_{0}
. For each
\mathbf{a}
in the sum, the coefficients of
\mathbf{a}
are between
1
and
d_{0}
, and not all of them are
1
. Therefore,

\prod_{\ell=1}^{j}\frac{\partial^{a_{\ell}}\hat{z}_{i}}{\partial\hat{t}^{a_{\ell}}}\lesssim_{d_{0}}\rho^{-1}
, and

\frac{\partial^{n}}{\partial\hat{t}^{n}}=\left(\frac{\partial\hat{z}_{i}}{\partial\hat{t}}\right)^{n}\frac{\partial^{n}}{\partial\hat{z}_{i}^{n}}+\sum_{j=1}^{n-1}O_{d_{0}}(\rho^{-1})\frac{\partial^{j}}{\partial\hat{z}_{i}^{j}}.

(132)

We apply this to (
131
).
By (F
i
), when
1\leq n\leq d_{0}
,

\left|\frac{\partial^{n}}{\partial\hat{t}^{n}}\left[\frac{\partial\hat{f}_{i}}{\partial\hat{z}_{i}}\right]\right|\leq\left|\left(\frac{\partial\hat{z}_{i}}{\partial\hat{t}}\right)^{n}\frac{\partial^{n+1}\hat{f}_{i}}{\partial\hat{z}_{i}^{n+1}}\right|+\sum_{j=1}^{n-1}O_{d_{0}}(\rho^{-1})\left|\frac{\partial^{j+1}\hat{f}_{i}}{\partial\hat{z}_{i}^{j+1}}\right|\lesssim_{d_{0}}\rho^{-1}.

(133)

By (P
{}_{i,d_{0}}
), (
131
), and (
133
),

\left|\frac{\partial^{d_{0}+1}\hat{z}_{i}}{\partial\hat{t}^{d_{0}+1}}\right|\lesssim_{d_{0}}\sum_{j=1}^{d_{0}}\left|\frac{\partial^{j}\hat{z}_{i}}{\partial\hat{t}^{j}}\right|\int_{0}^{\hat{s}}\left|\frac{\partial^{d_{0}+1-j}}{\partial\hat{t}^{d_{0}+1-j}}\left[\frac{\partial\hat{f}_{i}}{\partial\hat{z}_{i}}\right]\right|\,\mathrm{d}\hat{\sigma}\lesssim_{d_{0}}\sum_{j=1}^{d_{0}}|\hat{s}|\rho^{-1}\lesssim_{d_{0}}\rho^{-1}.

That is, (F
i
) and (P
{}_{i,d_{0}}
) imply (P
{}_{i,d_{0}+1}
). By induction, this implies (P
i,d
). Furthermore, the implicit constant in (P
{}_{i,d_{0}+1}
) depends only on
d
and the implicit constant in (P
{}_{i,d_{0}}
), so the implicit constant in (P
i,d
) depends only on
d
.

Consequently, (
132
) holds for all
1\leq n\leq d
. Solving the resulting system of equations for
\frac{\partial^{n}}{\partial\hat{z}^{n}_{i}}
, we obtain

\frac{\partial^{n}}{\partial\hat{z}^{n}_{i}}=\left(\frac{\partial\hat{t}}{\partial\hat{z}_{i}}\right)^{n}\frac{\partial^{n}}{\partial\hat{t}^{n}}+\sum_{\ell=1}^{n-1}O_{d}(\rho^{-1})\frac{\partial^{\ell}}{\partial\hat{t}^{\ell}}.

In particular, for
m,k\in\{1,\dots,d\}
,

\left|\hat{Z}^{k}_{i}\hat{S}^{m}\hat{\nu}_{i}\right|\leq\left|\left(\frac{\partial\hat{t}}{\partial\hat{z}_{i}}\right)^{k}\hat{T}^{k}\hat{S}^{m}\hat{\nu}_{i}\right|+\sum_{\ell=1}^{k-1}O_{d}(\rho^{-1})\left|\hat{T}^{\ell}\hat{S}^{m}\hat{\nu}_{i}\right|\\
\lesssim_{d}\left\|\frac{\partial\hat{t}}{\partial\hat{z}_{i}}\right\|_{\infty}^{k}\|\partial_{mk}\kappa\|_{\infty}+\sum_{\ell=1}^{k-1}\rho^{-1}\|\partial_{m\ell}\kappa\|_{\infty}\lesssim_{d}1.

This proves (H
i
), with implicit constant depending only on
d
.

It remains to prove (F
i+1
). Suppose that
\rho>2
.
By (
127
), we have
\|\hat{Z}_{i+1}\hat{f}_{i+1}\|_{\infty}\leq 2\rho^{-1}
.
Since

\hat{f}_{i+1}=Ar_{i+1}^{-1}\sum_{j=0}^{i}\nu_{j}=Ar_{i+1}^{-1}\sum_{j=0}^{i}A^{-1}r_{j}\hat{\nu}_{j}=\sum_{j=0}^{i}\rho^{i+1-j}\hat{\nu}_{j}=\sum_{m=1}^{i+1}\rho^{m}\hat{\nu}_{i+1-m},

and
\hat{Z}_{i+1}=\rho^{-2m}\hat{Z}_{i+1-m}
, for
2\leq k\leq d
,

\left\|\hat{Z}^{k}_{i+1}\hat{f}_{i+1}\right\|_{\infty}\leq\sum_{m=1}^{i+1}\left\|\rho^{-2km}\hat{Z}^{k}_{i+1-m}[\rho^{m}\hat{\nu}_{i+1-m}]\right\|_{\infty}\lesssim_{d}\sum_{m=1}^{i+1}\rho^{(1-2k)m}\leq 2\rho^{1-2k}.

That is, there is a
c>0
depending only on
d
such that
\|\hat{Z}^{k}_{i+1}\hat{f}_{i+1}\|_{\infty}\leq c\rho^{-3}
. We take
\rho>\sqrt{c}
, so that
\|\hat{Z}^{k}_{i+1}\hat{f}_{i+1}\|_{\infty}\leq c\rho^{-3}\leq 2\rho^{-1}
for all
2\leq k\leq d
. This proves (F
i+1
). By induction, (F
i
) and (H
i
) hold for all
i
.

A.2.
Proof of Lemmas
A.3
and
A.4

First, we prove Lemma
A.3
by rewriting words
D\in\{\hat{\partial}_{i},\hat{Z}_{i}\}
as sums of operators of the form
\hat{Z}_{i}^{k}\hat{\partial}_{i}^{j}
.

Lemma A.5
.

Let
d>0
. Suppose that
\|f_{i}\|_{W_{i,d}^{\prime}}<A^{-1}r_{i}
.

For any
0\leq l<d
, there is a
c_{l}>0
such that any word
D\in\{\hat{\partial}_{i},\hat{Z}_{i}\}^{l}
can be written as

D=\sum_{j+k\leq l}g_{j,k}(D)\hat{Z}_{i}^{k}\hat{\partial}_{i}^{j},

(134)

where for all
j
and
k
,
g_{j,k}(D)
is a smooth function such that
\|g_{j,k}(D)\|_{W_{i,d-l}}\leq c_{l}
.

We call the right side of (
134
) the
standard form
of
D
and we call the
g_{j,k}(D)
’s the
coefficients
of
D
.

The following lemma will be helpful in proving Lemma
A.5
. Let
\|\cdot\|_{K\to K^{\prime}}
denote the operator norm with respect to the norms
K
and
K^{\prime}
.

Lemma A.6
.

For any
d\geq 0
,

\|\hat{Z}_{i}\|_{W^{\prime}_{i,d}\to W_{i,d-1}}\leq 1
and
\|\hat{\partial}_{i}\|_{W_{i,d}\to W_{i,d-1}}\leq 1
.
For
g,h\in W_{i,d}
,
\|gh\|_{W_{i,d}}\lesssim_{d}\|g\|_{W_{i,d}}\|h\|_{W_{i,d}}
.

Proof.

The operator bounds on
\hat{Z}_{i}
and
\hat{\partial}_{i}
follow from the definitions of
W_{i,d}
and
W^{\prime}_{i,d}
. Let
D\in\{\hat{Z}_{i},\hat{\partial}_{i}\}^{l}
with
0\leq l\leq d
and suppose that
D=D_{1}\cdots D_{l}
where
D_{i}\in\{\hat{Z}_{i},\hat{\partial}_{i}\}
. Given a subset
A\subseteq\{1,...,l\}
, we let

\displaystyle D_{A}=D_{i_{1}}\cdots D_{i_{j}}

where
i_{1}<...<i_{j}
are the elements of
A
. By the product rule,

\displaystyle\|D(gh)\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle=\left\|\sum_{A\subseteq\{1,...,l\}}D_{A}(g)D_{A^{c}}(h)\right\|_{\infty}\leq 2^{d}\|g\|_{W_{i,d}}\|h\|_{W_{i,d}}.

∎

We prove Lemma
A.5
by induction on
l
.

Proof of Lemma
A.5
.

If
l=0
, then
D=\id
, so we can take
g_{0,0}(D)=1
and
c_{l}=1
. Let
0\leq l<d
and suppose that the lemma holds for words of length
l
. Let
D\in\{\hat{\partial}_{i},\hat{Z}_{i}\}^{l+1}
. Then
D=\delta D_{0}
for some
\delta\in\{\hat{\partial}_{i},\hat{Z}_{i}\}
and
D_{0}\in\{\hat{\partial}_{i},\hat{Z}_{i}\}^{l}
, and there are coefficients
g_{j,k}=g_{j,k}(D_{0})\colon\mathbb{H}\to\mathbb{R}
such that

\displaystyle D_{0}=\sum_{j+k\leq l}g_{j,k}\hat{Z}_{i}^{k}\hat{\partial}_{i}^{j},

where
\|g_{j,k}\|_{W_{i,d-l}}\leq c_{l}
for all
j
and
k
.

First, we consider the case that
\delta=\hat{Z}_{i}
. Then

\displaystyle D=\hat{Z}_{i}D_{0}=\sum_{j+k\leq l}\hat{Z}_{i}g_{j,k}\cdot\hat{Z}_{i}^{k}\hat{\partial}_{i}^{j}+\sum_{j+k\leq l}g_{j,k}\hat{Z}_{i}^{k+1}\hat{\partial}_{i}^{j}.

(135)

This sum is in standard form, and by Lemma
A.6
,
\|\hat{Z}_{i}g_{j,k}\|_{W_{i,d-l-1}}\leq\|g_{j,k}\|_{W_{i,d-l}}\leq c_{l}
. Moreover,
\|g_{j,k}\|_{W_{i,d-l-1}}\leq\|g_{j,k}\|_{W_{i,d-l}}\leq c_{l}
. Thus, the lemma holds for words of length
l+1
that start with
\hat{Z}_{i}
.

Second, we consider the case that
D=\hat{\partial}_{i}D_{0}
. We have

\displaystyle D=\hat{\partial}_{i}D_{0}=\sum_{j+k\leq l}\hat{\partial}_{i}g_{j,k}\cdot\hat{Z}_{i}^{k}\hat{\partial}_{i}^{j}+\sum_{j+k\leq l}g_{j,k}\hat{\partial}_{i}\hat{Z}_{i}^{k}\hat{\partial}_{i}^{j}=:\textup{I}+\textup{II}.

(136)

Then
I
is already in standard form, and its coefficients satisfy
\|\hat{\partial}_{i}g_{j,k}\|_{W_{i,d-l-1}}\leq\|g_{j,k}\|_{W_{i,d-l}}\leq c_{l}
.
To write
II
in standard form, we use the identity

\hat{\partial}_{i}\hat{Z}_{i}-\hat{Z}_{i}\hat{\partial}_{i}=Ar_{i}^{3}[\partial_{i},Z]=Ar_{i}^{3}[X+(y-f_{i})Z,Z]=Ar_{i}^{3}Zf_{i}\cdot Z=\hat{Z}_{i}\hat{f}_{i}\cdot\hat{Z}_{i}.

Since
\|f_{i}\|_{W^{\prime}_{i,d}}\leq A^{-1}r_{i}
,

\|\hat{Z}_{i}\hat{f}_{i}\|_{W_{i,d-1}}\leq\|\hat{f}_{i}\|_{W^{\prime}_{i,d}}\leq Ar_{i}^{-1}\|f_{i}\|_{W^{\prime}_{i,d}}\leq 1.

(137)

Suppose that
E=g_{j,k}\cdot\hat{\partial}_{i}\hat{Z}_{i}^{k}\hat{\partial}_{i}^{j}
is a summand of
II
. If
k=0
, there is nothing to do. Otherwise, if
k>0
, then

E=g_{j,k}\cdot\hat{Z}_{i}\hat{\partial}_{i}\hat{Z}_{i}^{k-1}\hat{\partial}_{i}^{j}+g_{j,k}\cdot\hat{Z}_{i}\hat{f}_{i}\cdot\hat{Z}_{i}^{k}\hat{\partial}_{i}^{j}.

The first term is a multiple of a word of length at most
l+1
that starts with
\hat{Z}_{i}
. By the argument above it can be written in standard form, and by Lemma
A.6
, the norms of its coefficients are bounded by a function of
c_{l}
. The second term is already in standard form, and by Lemma
A.6
and (
137
), its coefficient
g_{j,k}\cdot\hat{Z}_{i}\hat{f}_{i}
is bounded by a function of
c_{l}
.

Thus,
II
can be written as a sum of terms in standard form. The coefficients of each term are bounded by a function of
c_{l}
and there are at most
(l+1)^{2}
terms, so
D=\textup{I}+\textup{II}
can be written in standard form, with coefficients bounded by some
c_{l+1}
that depends only on
l
.
∎

Lemma
A.3
follows directly.

Proof of Lemma
A.3
.

Let
D\in\{\hat{Z}_{i},\hat{\partial}_{i}\}^{l}
for
l\leq d
. By Lemma
A.5
, we can write
D
in standard form as

\displaystyle D=\sum_{j+k\leq l}g_{j,k}\hat{Z}_{i}^{k}\hat{\partial}_{i}^{j},

where
\|g_{j,k}\|_{\infty}\lesssim_{d}1
.
Then, by Lemma
A.2
,

\|D\nu_{i}\|_{\infty}=\left\|\sum_{j+k\leq l}g_{j,k}\hat{Z}_{i}^{k}\hat{\partial}_{i}^{j}\nu_{i}\right\|_{\infty}\leq\sum_{j+k\leq l}\|g_{j,k}\|_{\infty}\|\hat{Z}_{i}^{k}\hat{\partial}_{i}^{j}\nu_{i}\|_{\infty}\\
\overset{\eqref{e:eta-sobolev-bound}}{\lesssim_{d}}\sum_{j+k\leq l}A^{-i}r_{i}\lesssim_{d}A^{-1}r_{i}.

∎

Finally, we prove Lemma
A.4
.

Proof of Lemma
A.4
.

Note that

\hat{Z}_{i+1}=r_{i+1}^{2}Z=\rho^{-2}r_{i}^{2}Z=\rho^{-2}\hat{Z}_{i}

(138)

and

\hat{\partial}_{i+1}=Ar_{i+1}\partial_{i+1}=\rho^{-1}Ar_{i}(\partial_{i}-\nu_{i}Z)=\rho^{-1}(\hat{\partial}_{i}-\hat{\nu}_{i}\hat{Z}_{i}).

(139)

Let
0\leq l\leq d
and let
D\in\{\hat{\partial}_{i+1},\hat{Z}_{i+1}\}^{l}
be a word of length
l
such that
D\not\in\{\id,\hat{\partial}_{i+1}\}
. Let
n=2\#\hat{Z}_{i+1}(D)+\#\hat{\partial}_{i+1}(D)
and note that
n\geq 2
.
We replace
\hat{Z}_{i+1}
by
\rho^{-2}\hat{Z}_{i}
and
\hat{\partial}_{i+1}
by
\rho^{-1}(\hat{\partial}_{i}-\hat{\nu}_{i}\hat{Z}_{i})
and distribute to get an expression
D=\rho^{-n}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\pm D_{j}
, where
D_{j}\in\{\hat{\partial}_{i},\hat{\nu}_{i},\hat{Z}_{i}\}^{*}
for each
j=1,\dots,m
and
m\leq 2^{l}
. Furthermore,
l\leq\ell(D_{j})\leq 2l
for all
j
.

If
\ell(D)=1
, then
D=\hat{Z}_{i+1}
, so
m=1
and
D_{1}=\hat{Z}_{i}
. Otherwise,
\ell(D)\geq 2
and
\ell(D_{j})\geq 2
. Since every
\hat{\nu}_{i}
in
D_{j}
is followed by
\hat{Z}_{i}
, if
D_{j}
ends in
\hat{\partial}_{i}
, then the previous letter is either
\hat{\partial}_{i}
or
\hat{Z}_{i}
. That is, we can write
D_{j}=D^{\prime}_{j}E_{j}
, where
E_{j}=\hat{Z}_{i}
,
E_{j}=\hat{\partial}_{i}^{2}
, or
E_{j}=\hat{Z}_{i}\hat{\partial}_{i}
.

Since
E_{j}\not\in\{\id,\hat{\partial}_{i}\}
, we have
\|E_{j}\|_{W^{\prime}_{i,d}\to W_{i,d-\ell(E_{j})}}\leq 1
. By Lemma
A.6
, for any
0\leq k\leq d
, we have
\|\hat{\partial}_{i}\|_{W_{i,k}\to W_{i,k-1}}\leq 1
,
\|\hat{Z}_{i}\|_{W_{i,k}\to W_{i,k-1}}\leq 1
, and

\|\hat{\nu}_{i}\|_{W_{i,k}\to W_{i,k}}\lesssim_{d}\|\hat{\nu}_{i}\|_{W_{i,d}}.

Let
L\lesssim_{d}1+\|\hat{\nu}_{i}\|_{W_{i,d}}
be such that each letter of
D_{j}
has operator norm at most
L
. Then

\|D_{j}\|_{W^{\prime}_{i,d}\to L_{\infty}}\leq\|E_{j}\|_{W^{\prime}_{i,d}\to W_{i,d-\ell(E_{j})}}\|D_{j}\|_{W_{i,d-\ell(E_{j})}\to W_{i,d-l}}\lesssim_{d}L^{d}.

Therefore,

\|Dg\|_{\infty}\leq\rho^{-n}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\|D_{j}\|_{W^{\prime}_{i,d}\to L_{\infty}}\|g\|_{W^{\prime}_{i,d}}\lesssim\rho^{-2}L^{d}\|g\|_{W^{\prime}_{i,d}}.

Since this holds for all
D\in\{\hat{\partial}_{i+1},\hat{Z}_{i+1}\}^{*}
such that
\ell(D)\leq d
and
D\not\in\{\id,\hat{\partial}_{i+1}\}
,

\|g\|_{W^{\prime}_{i+1,d}}\lesssim\rho^{-2}L^{d}\|g\|_{W^{\prime}_{i,d}}\lesssim_{d}(1+\|\hat{\nu}_{i}\|_{W_{i,d}})^{d}\rho^{-2}\|g\|_{W^{\prime}_{i,d}},

as desired.
∎
